Summary

“I mean, the newer attacks are way more complex, so much that we still haven’t found the physical IP origin of them. But the signature is the same. As for when she personally attacks she leaves behind these green rocks. Kryptonite, they call them. It’s like a zirconia, which she apparently made when she was in MIT. It has a unique radiation signature, harmless as far as anyone can tell. She’s the only one that has the formula, to get the right shade of green or something.”

“That’s not even the worst part.” Kelly commented.

“I feel like I’m gonna regret asking, but what’s the worst part?” Nia questioned.

It was J’onn who answered. “No one has ever fought her, and lived to tell about it”
------
Criminal Mastermind Lena Luthor has returned to the States, after almost a year since the last time she was spotted. DEO team Argo One has to discover why she is back, what is her next move and figure out a way to stop her before it's too late.

Feeling the weight of the sun (Here we come, blue and green)

    The Meeting

    Chapter Notes

    D.E.B.S. Is a classic, and we should watch it more often.
    Title is a line from the song Crystalline Green by Goldfrapp which is in the D.E.B.S. soundtrack.
    I know nothing about the military, or hacking, or crime, so, you know keep that in mind.
    Enjoy.

    Kara sent the text and just ran to the shower. She really didn’t want to know what James was gonna respond to an ominous 6.30 am text that just said: ‘We have to break up. Sorry. Not your fault.’ But she just couldn’t do it anymore. Was it the more courageous way to break up with your year-old boyfriend over text? Not really, but she had been meaning to break up with him for almost two months and she always backed down. Because the thing is, James is a really good guy; respectful, charismatic, funny. And the truth is, that she went pining for him for almost eight months, but as soon as they started dating all the magic went out like a flame without oxygen. And she tried, she really tried. They had swoon worthy date nights with wine, flowers and candles, double dates even, because that’s the thing you do when your respective siblings start dating each other. Her mom loved him. Her friends like him well enough, the sex was also good enough. James was handsome, played sports, was a good dancer, they shared moral values, their jobs were compatible, but together they were boring. She felt more giddy watching Alex and Kelly making eyes at each other than when she was kissing James.

    So, yeah, deciding to break up with James was easy. Actually doing it, that was the hard thing, because she cared about him, and she knew he loved her but she didn’t. Not like that.

    At 6.45 am when she got out of the shower, she ignored the 7 text messages she could already see on her cell phone screen and proceeded to put on her uniform. Kara Danvers was a Government DEO Agent. She was recruited out of National City University, they had been watching her since she took the SAT’s. Apparently, there is a secret test inside the already convoluted test, and she had the perfect score. Or so she had been told. But the biggest surprise came when she learned that her adoptive sister, Alex, was also recruited a year earlier, when she had finished med school. 

    They were both recruited for a relatively new branch of the DEO that specialized in secret ops. The Argo Teams. For all purposes, they were spies. They observed, went undercover, used their appearance to neutralize targets, the whole shebang. Their unit at the DEO was named Argo One, unofficially they were called DEBS, something about them being beauties or something because they were all women. It was something people at the agency didn’t call them to their faces, because they were the unit with the most results. Or most likely, because Alex would kill the bastard that had the balls.

    The team consisted of her sister, Alex as their leader; loyal, somewhat stoic, ferocious, brilliant, the best resting bitch face she had ever seen, she was a doctor which was great in a pinch for their line of work. 

    Kara herself, was the second in command, she was mostly the brawn, great in hand to hand and with weapons (even though Kelly liked to say that her real gift were her words, which wasn’t that much of a stretch considering she technically does have a degree in journalism). Alex was more of planning, Kara more of acting, they balanced each other out.

    Kelly was an army transfer, and she'd graduated as a psychologist. She was their profiler, always observing their target searching for their weak spot, the perfect timing to strike, and how to efficiently get them to cooperate. She was also fantastic with weapons, and acting as a mediator within the team. (And in the last sixteen months, her most important job had been to make Alex happy. And she was excelling at it.)

    Their latest recruit, Nia, had been hand picked by Major General Cat Grant, into the team, after getting an International Relations degree. She was a little green around the edges, but she was great with technology and the terminology of the youth. Kara took her under her wing, training her in hand to hand, and weaponry, mentoring her in any way she could.

    Their branch on the DEO differentiated themselves from the rest, with the use of a particular blue plaid fabric on their uniform. Kara used it the most as a neck-tie.  

    Alex used it the most as the stripes pressed into her uniform pants. Kelly and Nia used it the most as skirts which were technically the correct way to use the fabric, but truth be told, to Kara skirts just felt impractical on the field. Then again, Kelly and Nia rarely went to the field on skirts, maybe Kara just didn’t like the damn skirts, the way it basically demeaned the team, and gave the assholes in headquarters more ammo to call them DEBS.

    She was finishing putting on her ankle boots, sitting on the couch, when the door opened and her sister’s head popped on, her hair going in different directions, half dressed and nursing a cup of coffee. 

    “Minus 20 minutes to leave. Why is my dear brother-in-law calling me to tell you to pick up your phone?” 

    Kara grimaced, at the question and when her phone started ringing she picked it up. Alex just shrugged and left, closing the door behind her.

    “James.” She whispered into her phone.

    “Kara, what the hell?” James didn't really sound angry, he sounded more disappointed than anything else. “What do you mean 'We have to break up’? It’s it something about your job?” Kara let out a deep sigh.

    “No, not really. Can we talk later? I’m really late for work.”

    “Okay, sur-” Kara closed up the call without letting him finish. She really didn’t want to talk to him first thing in the morning. And her actual priority about the whole thing, was Kelly’s reaction to her breaking things up with her brother. And that said a lot about her relationship with James. She really loved Kelly and she loved how happy she made Alex, and she really, really hoped she wouldn’t ruin her sister's relationship with her own break up.

    She picked up her glasses and put them on, fixed up her neck-tie, making sure her white button-up was free of any lint, picked up her keys and closed the door. They lived in a tiny 10-storey apartment building, close to a covert military base and belonging to the DEO. The team were basically neighbors, Alex living across from her. Nia lived two floors up, and technically speaking, Kelly did too, but she spent all her free time at Alex’s. Living and working with someone wasn’t easy, but somehow their little team was great at it.

    “Coming,” she called aloud, when the cell on her hand started ringing showing Alex’s face on the screen, moving down the stairs with quick steps, two floors down to the lobby. She was the last one to arrive at the meet point, and Alex gave her a suspicious look that Kara rebutted with a bright smile.

    “J’onn called a meeting at 08.00 hours. You’re late, and I’ll not be accused of favoritism.”

    Kara rolled her eyes after seeing her watch. “Being on the dot, it’s not late.” She said walking up to the blue Chevrolet Camaro. It was hers, but Alex always drives it. She refuses to sell the Ducati Monster 1200 S Black on Black bike she has, and she would only be caught dead on the Volkswagen Beetle convertible that Kara was gonna buy at first. 

    They compromised, because the Ducati is a bitch to drive on National City, Kara prefers to be in charge of the music than drive, and with four people in the car they could use the carpool lane, it was great. “And you will always be accused of favoritism, because you work with your sister and your girlfriend. The odds are already against you.”

    As always, Alex groaned when Kara played their one daily song of the greatest album in history, No Strings Attached, but still sang it under her breath. Kelly smiled softly in the back, catching Alex gaze on the rearview mirror, and Nia kept trying to sneak a few more minutes of sleep.

    Kara waited for a Dua Lipa song to end a few minutes later, when they were halfway to the DEO to speak. “I broke up with James”

    “What?” Nia asked, over the loud music that was starting to play.

    “I broke up with Jimmy.” She said again, louder.

    “Oh, thank God,” Kelly said, at the same time that Nia asked: “Oh, why?”

    Alex kept quiet, but she looked at her for half a second, with an indecipherable look.

    “I just… Wait, Kelly, did you say ‘ Thank God’ ?” She looked at her sister-in-law, turning on her seat, like she had grown a second head.

    “Yes, Kara. Because I love my brother and I love you, and you’re both great people but you two together are as boring as watching grass growing. You have the sexual chemistry of a wet matchstick.” Alex snorted, and Kelly continued as nothing. “And while that’s not everything in a relationship, you didn’t make each other better, or were any more happier with each other than when you were just friends. Sometimes a relationship is just not meant to be.”

    “Yeah, and watching you two kiss was like watching you having your teeth pulled.”

    “Alex! ” She screamed at her sister, flabbergasted.

    “I thought they were cute together.” Nia piped up, almost in a whisper.

    “They’re both very attractive people,” Kelly rationalized. “But that doesn’t always translate into a successful relationship.”

    “Well,” Alex started while she parked the car into their assigned spot. “I just hope there are no hard feelings between the two of you. Because not only would that make things awkward if there is any joint mission with him and Homeland Security but also because, you know, Kelly and I.”

    Kara blinked at her sister, and then turned around to watch Kelly. “I… I was actually more worried about how you would take the news.”

    Kelly lowly laughed. “No need. I know you care about him, just not like that. It’s all good. Besides, there is a Danvers-Olsen couple still going strong.” She said winking at Alex through the mirror.

    “And not even Pam in HR could do anything against our love, babe. You’re in for the long haul.” Alex said, all cocky getting out of the car, and extending her hand to take Kelly’s.

    Kara smiled at them before also climbing out the car leaving the door open so Nia could do the same. “Where is the meeting?”

    “He said we should go to the canteen, in order for you to feed your bottomless pit of a stomach and actually pay attention,” Alex answered without missing a beat, interlocking her fingers with Kelly’s, who let out a small giggle at the same time as Nia.

    Kara gasped on fake outrage. “Well, that’s just rude. But I am hungry, so I’ll let it slide.”

    “Sure you are.” Murmured Nia.

    They all walked to the canteen, chatting animatedly about the upcoming Endgame Gala. All year long, all the government agencies try to outdo each other with their accomplishments and the Gala is where they can boast about it. This year the Argo One, had caught a big, once upon a time billionaire in a massive pyramid scheme by the name of Maxwell Lord. It was a big deal, the DEO was happy, Alex was furious because she had to seduce the man, and Kara, Kelly and Nia had so much fun teasing her about it. They absolutely made a ridiculous video helping her dress for the big date.

    They joined the queue and ordered their breakfast like everyday. Nia was a fan of sugary goodness, she almost always went with strawberry muffins and cappuccino. 

    Kelly being the healthier of the pack mostly went with fruits, granola and yogurt, and coffee with oat milk.

    Growing up Alex’s favorite breakfast was their mother's Eliza, blueberry pancakes, but she had stopped eating them because Kelly was allergic to blueberries. (Kelly had told her numerous times that she could eat them but Alex simply refused.) Now her favored breakfast consists of an everything-bagel with cream cheese and prosciutto, and black coffee. 

    Kara asked for pancakes with chocolate chips and whipped cream. Bacon and eggs with toast. A banana muffin, a chocolate chip muffin and a latte. (According to Alex, Kara was like a 5-year old without supervision when it came to eating.)

    “I’m still wondering how you can eat so much?” Kelly questioned under her breath, once all their food was delivered.

    “You would think it had something to do with the job, but nope. She has been eating like that since we were kids,” Alex responded, walking over to the table where their boss, J’onn J'onzz was seated. “It’s a miracle she hasn’t any underlying medical condition. Morning J’onn.” She greeted the man, taking a seat by his side.

    “Maybe I just need a lot of energy to deal with you. Morning Papa Bear.” Kara smiled at the man, taking the free seat at his other side.

    “Morning. Please, don’t call me that.” J’onn pleaded, once again.

    “Morning, Papa Bear.” Kelly and Nia, said at the same time, seating next to Alex and Kara respectively.

    He sighed and looked at Kara, but said nothing else while taking a sip of his black coffee. Kara smiled at him and started eating, as if someone were to come and take her food from her. 

    J’onn was a coworker and old friend of Alex and Kara’s father, Jeremiah, who had gone MIA years ago and was presumed dead. Since then, J’onn had taken care of them, and personally trained them once they joined the agency. He was the Director of the DEO and the liaison between the Argo teams and Major General Grant. Argo was basically her pet project, and she was very protective of the teams and expected nothing short of perfection. Exactly at 08.00, J’onn put up a tablet in the middle of the table.

    “Attention, please. We have a new mission.”

    The tablet lit up, and none other than Cat Grant showed up on the screen. Almost, imperceptibly Alex straightened up. Kara stopped eating, Nia and Kelly stopped chit-chatting between them.

    “Good morning, Major General.” J’onn politely greeted.

    “Director J'onzz.” She responded curtly. “Morning, Argo One. It’s important that you know that this mission takes priority over any other case you have open. The window it’s extremely limited and only God knows when we will have another opportunity as good as this one. You will have all resources at your disposal. Be careful and please make me proud.” And then, as if nothing had happened, the screen went back to black. The table stayed silent for a couple of minutes. 

    “She had never talked directly to us before a mission.” Alex said quietly, almost in awe.

    “Yeah, well, this one is a big one.” J’onn said nonchalantly, moving his hand over the tablet to bring up the mission files. The picture of a brunette with striking green eyes, and a jawline so sharp it could probably cut glass, appeared on the screen. “Lena Luthor.”

    Kara dropped the fork and knife she still had in her hands. Alex groaned, massaging her temples as if she already was battling a headache. 

    “Holy shit.” Kelly mumbled, after taking a sharp breath. 

    “Sorry, who exactly is Lena Luthor?” Nia asked.

    “Lena Luthor. Former COO of LuthorCorp. One of the youngest COO’s ever. She’s a certified genius. Almost an Olympic fencer, chess prodigy, three PhDs by the age of 23.” Kelly babbled. Her and Kara had spent months studying her, trying to catch her.

    “Ok. And?” Nia questioned again.

    “One day she just vanished and a series of bank hackings left some companies bankrupt, like these companies never had any money to begin with. Then, she went into the actual companies and wreaked havoc on them. Trashed the place completely, released the blueprints of their prototypes and projects on the web. At first, they were her direct competitors, tech companies. But then she attacked pharmaceutical companies, food conglomerates, the beauty industry, car manufacturers. It had no particular pattern…” Kara explained, half her food forgotten.

    “How do you even know it was her?” 

    Kara smiled at Nia’s question. It was a very good question. “Well, as we said, she is a certified genius, when she was in high school—”

    “At 13, mind you,” Alex interrupted.

    “Yes,” Kara continued. “She hacked into the DoD.”

    “Are you serious?”

    “As a heart attack.” J’onn said, no trace of humor in his deep voice.

    “She said it was just a simple bet with her brother, Lex, that she could do it. But, the Luthors are one of the wealthiest families in the western hemisphere so, the incident went away without much fuzz, and the DoD cataloged the attack as harmless. But as you know, hackers tend to have a signature.”

    “And the signature is the same?”

    “Basically. I mean, the newer attacks are way more complex, so much that we still haven’t found the physical IP origin of them. But the signature is the same. As for when she personally attacks she leaves behind these green rocks. Kryptonite, they call them. It’s like a zirconia, which she apparently made when she was in MIT. It has a unique radiation signature, harmless as far as anyone can tell. She’s the only one that has the formula, to get the right shade of green or something.”

    “That’s not even the worst part,” Kelly commented.

    “I feel like I’m gonna regret asking but, what’s the worst part?” Nia questioned.

    It was J’onn who answered. “No one has ever fought her, and lived to tell about it”

    “Her mother and brother have condemned her actions, but mostly they act as if she never even existed. LuthorCorp is as profitable as ever.” Alex said before turning to J’onn. “What do we have? Last we heard was when she killed those five agents in Antarctica.”

    “Iceland, not Antarctica,” Kara said to herself.

    “Well, apparently now she’s stateside. Her known associate Querl Dox, aka Brainiac has been spotted near the business district here in National City.” J’onn swiped on the screen showing a picture of Brainiac, with his dark hair and naturally tanned skin, wearing sunglasses, to the team. “Intelligence says she is going to meet Veronica Sinclair, aka Roulette, tonight at 20.00 hours.” He swiped at the tablet again showing a picture of a tall woman, covered in snake tattoos, in a plunging red dress.

    “That just screams trouble. A cyber terrorist and a trafficker? Can’t be good.” Kelly said under her breath.

    “Where is the meeting?” Kara asked.

    “Il Palazzo. And that’s all the intelligence we have. Your mission is strictly surveillance.” J’onn said severely, stopping Kara’s protests before they could even begin. “You’re not to intercept her tonight. If there is going to be imminent combat, please do not engage her alone. We know how dangerous she is. She is brilliant, and has training from childhood. We’re going to assume she is also armed and that her associates are well within range, so please,” He looked directly at Kara. “Do not engage, if you can avoid confrontation.”

    Kara sighed. “Understood, sir.”

    J’onn stood up, leaving the tablet with all the info. “Alex, you're in charge. Please, Argo One, be careful. Do not underestimate the target.” Without saying anything else, he left the canteen.

    Kara touched the tablet bringing up the pictures of both Lena Luthor and Roulette. 

    “What exactly could these two be planning?”

     

     

    She was looking at the picture of someone she hadn't seen since her time in Ireland, at boarding school. She couldn’t even believe Veronica had accepted the invitation Brainy had extended.

    “Where is the reservation?” She asked, feeling Brainy standing stoic behind her, also looking at the picture on the screen.

    “Il Palazzo, at 8.00 PM. You know, fancy, discreet, expensive, romantic.” He answered with his cadence, almost robotic.

    “Mhm.” She contemplated the screen for a few more seconds. "Cancel it.” She said standing up and walking through the warehouse, trying to avoid Brainy’s protests.

    “Lena, come on.” He said quickly walking behind her. “You have to get out there. It’s starting to affect our work.”

    “I am fine.” She tried to cut up his ranting. 

    “You need to get laid.”

    “Excuse me!?” She stopped walking, turning around to see his face, offended by the mere insinuation.

    “It’s simple science.” He quickly amended. Standing before her, the tips of his fingers touching each other, his hands forming a sort of triangle, it was his very particular pose. “It’s a fast way to relax yourself. Sex releases oxytocin, but you refuse to have sex if you’re not in a relationship. Logically, we need to find someone who woos you, to make your brain high on dopamine and serotonin. Resulting in you having sex with them and finally relaxing. It’s been two years since Andrea.”

    Lena touched in between her brows awkwardly, and sighed. “Brainy, I don’t need a date to… ‘release oxytocin’. Thank you.” She quietly said. “And about Andrea, I’m fine. I have been out there. I went on that date with the British guy, didn’t I?”

    “No. We all went out as a group with Chester, the British guy, and you faked a migraine to get out of the bar, half way in.” He said, matter of fact. “And you are not fine. She dumped you. And now she is engaged to be married to a guy she went on a date with a week after breaking up with you.”

    Lena grimaced. “Okay, okay. I’ll go to your stupid date.” She continued walking, to go to her room. “But never talk to me about my sex life again. It’s weird, you’re like my little brother. Certainly more of a brother than my actual one.” The last part she said under her breath.

    “We are the same age,” Brainy said, as matter of fact as always. “It is just…” He paused for a second, like searching for the correct words. ”I am worried about you. You haven’t been yourself since… her. And you are getting sloppy. Then, you go on a retirement of sorts to Iceland for almost a year. I can’t do this alone, you know it. You are the brains and heart of this operation.”

    Lena looked at him fondly, somewhat surprised that he had opened up like that. That he cares about her so deeply. But she really shouldn’t have, he had been with her since the very beginning, her only companion. He was the one to bring up his findings and worries to her. And they understand each other, both extremely intelligent, always uncomfortably above everyone else that it’s somewhat like a chasm between them and the rest of the world, there is a sense of kinship between the two.

    “Okay, Brainy. I’ll go on this date with Veronica. Even if she was a pain in the ass at Mount Helena’s. I don’t even think she remembers me.”

    “But you have to be open minded. Do not let your feelings about the past get in the way of it. Please.”

    “I will be open minded, Brainy. I promise.”

     

     

     

    After breakfast and the debrief, Argo One spent most of the day planning the surveillance operation. Nia had dug up the blueprints of Il Palazzo. Kelly had brought up all the info they had about both Veronica Sinclair and Lena Luthor. Alex and Kara were studying everything about the restaurant layout, assigning coordinates, escape routes, and making sure all the equipment and weapons were in working order. 

    At 12.00 hours, they took lunch together and then retired to the bunk beds in the headquarters to nap, because it was going to be a long night apparently.

    It was 17.45 hours when they found themselves getting ready in the locker room, putting on their all black tactical uniform, gathering all equipment and weaponry.

    “Kelly, please, give us an overview about them.” Alex said, once they all were ready, standing on their briefing deck. Kelly took the tablet and put the target’s profiles on the big screen.

    “Veronica Sinclair, aka Roulette. 34 years old. Born in National City, USA. Her family net worth is around 13 billion dollars. She alone owns a few casinos here in National City, in Metropolis, Gotham and Central City. She has been linked to underground fights and has been accused of both property and human trafficking, yet no charges have managed to stick.” Kelly said, while a series of images appeared on the screen. She touched the tie that now all four of them sported behind their bulletproof vest, in a mild nervous tell, and then continued. “With the information I’ve gathered I believe she’s narcissistic, she believes she is untouchable, and gets certain satisfaction with cruelty and violence. She herself is not going to attack in a hand to hand combat manner, she surrounds herself with bodyguards usually more than two. She lives a life of luxury. She's not above firing a gun, or a weapon that can do harm, but remain herself at a safe distance.”

    Kelly touched the tablet again and a new picture appeared on the screen, it was older, taken from a school yearbook. Two faces were highlighted from the group shot.

    “The secondary target and the primary target have history together. This wouldn’t really be a first meeting. They both come from multi billionaire families, and went to the same boarding school, Mount Helena’s in Ireland. However, I didn’t find any evidence about the kind of relationship they had there, if at all. As far as I can tell, the primary target was, at the time, kind of a loner. Apart from her peers because of the large age gap between herself and them.”

    She swiped again at the tablet and the pictures of the main target showed up on screen.

    Kelly kept going, her voice calm and collected.

    “Main target, Lena Kieran Luthor, 28 years old. Her family net worth is around 90 billion dollars, of which we’re uncertain if she has access to. Certified genius with an IQ over 160 points, supposedly. She almost went to the Olympics, foil fencing at age 19. She said at the time, that she didn’t go because she was focusing on school. Has been a chess prodigy since age 5 and has won multiple tournaments. Graduated from MIT. Has several PhDs. She has basic hand to hand training.” Kelly made a pause. Took a breath. “With all the information we’ve gathered, I can infer that she is incredibly resourceful, and can think incredibly fast. She is calculating and precise. I’ve the suspicion that she has an eidetic memory. She is pragmatic, but doesn’t trust easily. Was a model citizen, very well liked and charitable prior to her downfall, two and a half years ago, we don’t know exactly what caused the change in her behavior.”

    “Anything else?” Alex questioned

    “Yes. With the data from previous encounters, she seems extremely violent. She is in a male dominated line of work, so she probably over compensates. There have been no survivors once she’s engaged. She accounts for 13 casualties. However, they all seem from when she’s been sought after. No civilian casualties on her behalf.”

    “Thank you. Kelly.” Alex smiled at her. “Kara please, explain the operation.”

    Kara gently took the tablet from Kelly, moving to the front of the screen, showing now the blueprint of the restaurant. 

    “We’re just to watch the target, figure out her next step and why is she meeting with Roulette. It’s a long game. We’re not to engage with her. If there is an impending confrontation we’re not to go solo. We’re to work in pairs, if needed. Nia, you’re with me. Kelly and Alex you’re team A.” She touched on the blueprint on the tablet, to zoom into the main room. “We will be suspended from the ceiling on the principal main dining area. The ceiling is frankly, ridiculously high, so we shouldn’t have any issue. We’re to use the sonar and wiring equipment to listen to their conversation, and we will need pictures to update the database. Nia, you’re going to listen in and record. Kelly, you’re going to take the pics. Alex and I will guard you, anything should happen.”

    She took a deep breath, changing again to the full blueprint. “If there is any need to escape, we will separate in pairs, and go to our rendezvous point, in Cordova Street, 30 minutes after separation. We’re to use private channel 3 on comms.” Kara gave the tablet to Alex and moved from the front of the screen. Alex left the tablet back on the deck, after swiping to show the pictures of both Lena Luthor and Veronica Sinclair, she kept quiet for a few seconds.

    “Please be careful, don’t be fooled by their cute faces, they’re both ruthless. And even if Roulette wouldn’t punch you she could very well shoot you. And Luthor is  unpredictable. Good luck. Let 's move on.”

    They moved in silence to the unmarked black van, it was close to the 18.40 hours when they were dropped at the backdoor of Il Palazzo, and had no problem breaching the perimeter from up. Kara used a pulley system to drop them one by one into the restaurant, precisely 8 feet from the ceiling. The total drop was 85 feet, so they should be fine. In any case Nia had hacked the place to kill a few lights around them, should anyone glance up. It was already 19.15 hours when they finished setting up. It wasn’t a comfortable position, but it would have to do. 

    “Comms check,” Alex said, quietly. They were so far apart from each other that they couldn’t hear each other without their earbuds. All four of them checked up. Like clockwork at 20.00 hours the target arrived. Secondary target had yet to arrive. 

    “Target in position.” Kelly said once Lena Luthor had taken a seat. “Acquiring pictures.”

    Nia said something about her suit jacket, Kelly said something about her posture. Kara felt some sort of restless energy just from waiting.

    “Heads up, secondary target arriving.” Alex piped up almost 10 minutes later.

    “Kelly, make sure you capture the greeting.”  Kara ordered.

    “Roger that.”

    “Nia?” Alex gently pressed, waiting for the youngling to pass on about what they were talking about. 

    “They are reminiscing about the old times.” Nia said quietly. “How secondary target used to smuggle the targets' favorites to Metropolis.”

    “Target grew up in Metropolis, when she wasn’t in Ireland.” Kelly gently supplied.

    Kara was watching through her binoculars, curious about the interaction. The waiter approached them and they ordered.

    “Target asked if secondary preferred white or red. Secondary prefers vodka. Do you think they’re talking in code? Could be a way to talk openly about their plans without compromising themselves.” Nia said. Kara smiled proudly, it was a good guess.

    “Could be. Make sure you’re recording everything.” Kara kept looking at Lena Luthor. “She looks so young...” She said quietly to herself when she heard something next to her. She moved her binoculars from her face, and found herself in front of her ex-boyfriend. “What the hell are you doing here?”

    “You thought you guys were the only ones here for Luthor?” James said, quietly yet severe. “It’s like a party, CIA, FBI, you, me... You guys were the last to arrive.”

    Kara shot a look at Alex, who made an affirmative nod after looking around. Well, shit.

    “Okay, but why are you interrupting my OP?” She asked, gritting her teeth.

    “Well, since you couldn’t be bothered to call me at all, during the day, to explain yourself about breaking up with me,” he answered quite nonchalantly. “I just wanted to ask for the bracelet I gave you, on our six month anniversary, back. You know, it was my dad’s and since we’re nothing to each other anymore…”

    Kara looked at him, furious. “And you thought this was the moment for that?” She hissed. If it weren’t for the mission and the fact that their target was very, very slippery she would be screaming at his face. The audacity of this man. “Okay, fine. Whatever, Jimmy.” She tried to unhook the bracelet, but then he also tried to do it, and when the damn bracelet broke free, neither of them caught it, and the platinum band fell down, all 85 feet.

    And then, everything went to shit.

     

    Brainy drove her to the restaurant on the Porsche Taycan, it was electric. They were both stoked about that. She was not so stoked about her so-called date. But she had promised Brainy that she would be open minded. She dressed up, one of her almost forgotten suits, it was a beautiful navy blue. She almost wore one of her classic stilettos but, truth be told, she didn't have the mental strength to go on a date with Veronica Sinclair and wear stilettos. Instead she went with ankle boots. She would hopefully be seated the whole time, anyways. She wore her hair straight down, bold red lips and a winged eyeliner. She was ready for the world. And yet… She was somehow both nervous and bored. 

    “You are fidgeting,” Brainy said, parking the car, close to the front of the restaurant. “There is no reason to be nervous. You already know her. Worst case scenario you don’t click and you come back home having eaten in a two Michelin star restaurant. Best case scenario, you release oxytocin.”

    “Don’t,” Lena looked at him exasperatedly. Still wringing her hands. “No more happy hormones talk. I’m not nervous about her. It’s more anxiety about being outside. It’s been a while.”

    “The place is very discreet.”

    “I know. Thank you.” She looked at the clock and smiled at him. “I’ll be going, you know I don’t like being late.”

    “Best of luck.” Brainy said, showing her his thumbs. 

    She smiled fondly at him and got out of the car. She approached the entrance, and was received by the maître d’. She politely asked for her table under the name Tess Mercer. She was guided to her table, and given the drinks menu. Lena glanced at it, feeling like drinking a few tumblers of good scotch, but feeling like the responsible thing to do was to drink a mild cup of wine. She was debating the merits of a glass of white versus one of rosé, when her date arrived. She politely smiled while standing up to greet her.

    “Hello, Veronica.” She said, trying to not look at the truly sinful cleavage that left nothing to the imagination, showing the myriad of tattoos that Veronica possessed. “Thank you for coming.”

    Veronica smiled, predatory, and waited for the host to retire, once they were both seated, before greeting her. “Lena Luthor, I see we are not so different as I thought we were, back in school.”

    “Well, time changes people.” She curtly said. “I’m actually surprised you remember me.”

    Veronica smirked. “I do remember secretly gifting you, your favorite scotch, for a few Metropolis galas back in the day. Your mom none the wiser”

    Lena hummed. “So you remember me trying to be a rebel.”

    “Rebel is kind of a big word.” Veronica laughed. Their waiter showed up and asked if they wanted anything to drink.

    “Do you prefer white or red wine?”

    “I actually prefer vodka.” 

    “Mhm. Do you prefer it straight or maybe something like a Martini?”

    Veronica bit her lip, hiding a smile. “Surprise me, maybe.”

    “Well, th— The lady will have a vodka martini. I’ll have the house white wine. And if you could, please bring Hackleback caviar as an entree, thank you very much.”

    As the waiter retired, Veronica made a big show of crossing her legs, even if Lena couldn’t actually see anything about the movement with the table in between them.

    “What have you been doing in these last years?”

    Lena looked discreetly around them. “You know, global domination and such. You?”

    “Ambitious,'' Veronica nodded, amused. “I’ve been coming and going. You know how it is. I opened a little pop up in Freeland a few months back. It has been doing quite well.”

    Lena hummed, the waiter bringing their drinks and their entrée. The little ‘pop up' was the illegal fighting ring she had across the country, for the entertainment of the rich and famous. Lena hated the mere principle of it, and she was already set on leaving without going anywhere further with this date. The blasé attitude of Veronica, not gelling well with her. The only thing was how she could exit the place as quickly as possible, without offending her companion. She took a gulp of her wine, it was quite dry, just looking at Veronica take a bite of the caviar smeared toast.

    “You have been doing well by yourself.” She commented, noncommittally.

    “So have you, last I heard. Quite the black sheep.”

    Lena hummed again, moving her glass of wine not even touching the food already on the table, when something dropped in the middle of it. A chunky platinum bracelet. She picked it up, with the cloth napkin, and looked up. There, dangling from the ceiling like ridiculous Christmas ornaments were at least six agents. There for her, no doubt. 

    “Are you fucking kidding me?” She breathed, reaching for the concealed gun under her suit jacket, pointing it up. In front of her, Veronica had also drawn a gun from somewhere in her minuscule dress. The odds weren’t particularly good. She heard a loud bang somewhere behind her and Veronica fired her gun. She immediately dropped under the table and crawled on the floor, seeking refuge behind the bar. Veronica, next to her. She was incredibly glad to be wearing a suit, instead of a dress. That just didn’t look comfortable. 

    “I guess that was the end of the date,” Veronica commented, once they were behind the bar, like she was talking about the weather. 

    “Yeah, I don’t think it was going to work, anyways.” 

    Veronica laughed. “I didn’t come with the idea of marriage, that’s simply not my thing. But I see we’re just looking for different stuff.” Somehow she had grabbed her long coat, she dropped a grenade over the bar, and just stood up and sauntered out of the place, once the thing detonated. Like there were not bullets flying fucking everywhere. Lena looked at where she disappeared from a few seconds in complete disbelief, before pulling herself together. She also had to move, and get to Brainy. She enjoyed her date for a grand total of 30 seconds, and then even the wine had gone to shit. She hid the gun in the back of her pants suit, and crawled close to a tall table, using it as a shield against the rain of bullets to cross the short distance between the bar and the service door a few meters to her left.

    Her ears were ringing with the sound of all the bullets that ricocheted from the metal table, glass breaking everywhere. She got to the service door, dropping the table. “Discreet my ass.” She grumbled under her breath trying to find the way to the parking lot in what appeared to be a frankly large cellar. 

    In the darkness, she saw a door, the opposite side from where she came from, that had to be her way out of this nightmare of a date.

     

     

    The first shot wasn’t even a shot, it was a sommelier opening a wine bottle, but Roulette fired in their general direction. And the rest of the teams just started firing at her and Luthor. She couldn’t believe how a simple reconnaissance mission went to shit so fast. All because of a stupid bracelet from her ex-boyfriend. She rappelled down to level floor, grabbing the gun from her tight holster, and approached Nia once she also dropped down. As far as she could tell, Argo One wasn’t firing. She couldn’t say the same for the rest of the agencies.

    “Where is she?” She heard Alex on comms.

    “No idea, lost her in between the people fleeing the scene.”

    “Heads up. By the bar.” Kelly’s voice cracked up. A gas grenade was deployed around the area, from behind the bar.

    “Roger that. Approaching target.”

    “Kara, don’t,'' Alex warned, but then Luthor jumped out using a table to deflect the bullets, and ran across the place. All four of them fired their guns. Glass was flying everywhere. She grimaced at the disaster, who knows which agency would end up with the bill to pay the damage.

    “B team go east to intercept,” Alex said on her ear piece “A team will pursue for the northwest entry. We need at least an idea of what exactly is she planning.”

    “Roger.” Three different voices said in unison. Kara could feel Nia next to her while she approached from the east. Once they crossed a door the earpiece fell silent for some reason. She couldn’t hear the movements from any of her teammates from the device, only Nia walking next to her. She stopped, looking around. It was a huge, dark cellar.

    “Nia, we need to split. I can’t hear the A team. Something must be interfering with the comms. Go back, warn them.”

    “I don’t think that’s a good idea.”

    “It could be Luthor. Please, follow orders, Agent Nal.”

    Nia looked at her, clearly not agreeing with her orders, but simply nodded and went back from where they came together. Kara took a deep breath and kept walking. Pointing her gun forward, looking up and down the place, with high shelves, and barrels. Liquor, she assumed, wine, whiskey that sort of thing. Sixty feet or so, ahead; next to a wall of barrels, she could see half a door. She quickly approached it, but right before getting to it, a body collided against her.

    She didn’t quite fall on the floor, but she lost her balance with the force of it.

    “I am so sorry,” a slightly accented voice said.

    “No prob—” She stopped talking when she saw no other than Lena Luthor, was the one that collided against her. She pointed her gun at her. Luthor did the same. They were too close to one another, a bullet would most likely be fatal at that distance. Even if they weren’t aiming directly at each other's heads. It was a stalemate, and they both knew it. 

    “Lena Luthor, you have the right to remain silent.”

    The brunette let out a disbelieving laugh. “Are you seriously reading me my Miranda rights?”

    “Anything you say can, and will be used against you.” Kara kept going, extending her free hand, palm up, for Luthor to surrender her gun.

    They both kept quiet for a few unnerving seconds. Then Luthor spoke up. “Okay, here's the thing...”

    “Yeah?”

    “I am really not up for dying today.”

    “Me neither,” Kara smiled despite herself.

    “So, I was thinking... Why don't you put your gun down.”

    “You put yours down. You're the criminal, and I'm the cop, I think I'm more trustworthy.”

    “I was minding my own business on a date, when you guys decided to rain shit all over me.” 

    “Wait,” Kara almost moved her gun out of the way, just from the sheer surprise. “You were on a date?”

    “Whatever.”

    “With wannabe Lisbeth Salander?”

    “She’s not that bad.” Lena grimaced. Silence fell for a few seconds, then; “What?” 

    “Nothing. I just—” Kara paused, slightly cocking her head to the side. “I didn't know you were into—”

    “Why would you know?” Luthor asked defensively.

    “That really torpedoes our profile of you.”

    “How exactly would that bit of information torpedo a whole psych profile?” Lena mocked.

    Kara kept quiet, a small smile dancing on her face. “It actually doesn’t but I guess it could get us a better understanding of you. Since you’re so hard to track down.”

    “Yeah, well good luck with that.”

    “I don’t need luck. You’re already under arrest.”

    “Am I?” Lena mocked again.

    Kara heard someone approaching from the northwest entry. “Alex, over here!” She made the terrible, stupid and rookie mistake of trying to see in that direction for half a second. Suddenly, a green smoke surrounded her. It looked nasty, and she used her free arm to try and block her mouth and nose, in case the thing was toxic.

    It dispersed really fast, less than a minute, but once it did, she found herself alone for a few seconds until the rest of the team got to her.

    “Fucking hell!”

    “What happened? Why did you ditch Nia?” Alex questioned. “And why don’t the comms work in here?”

    “I almost got her. She was right here.” Kara secured her gun into her tight holster again.

    “You would be dead if that were the case.” Kelly supplied, calmly.

    “Guys…” Nia said really low. When all three of them looked at her, she simply pointed her finger at the door, where green rocks were scattered on the floor. “I think she really was here.”

    Silence fell between them, Kara knelt down picking up a few of the rocks. Kryptonite, she figured.

    Then Alex spoke up. “Apparently you are not happy with just being the perfect score, but you had to go and be the sole survivor of an encounter with the one and only Lena Luthor.”

    Kara kept quiet, but she heard Kelly swearing under her breath. 

    Fuck, indeed.

    Chapter End Notes

    Everything Changes

    Chapter Summary

    Kara's night keeps getting better.

    Chapter Notes

    As soon as Lena climbed onto the Porsche, Brainy started his rant, while driving away from the restaurant, way too fast to not be considered suspicious.

    “What a complete and utter disaster. I am so sorry, Lena. Leave it to the government, you are in town for half a week and they are all over you, just inserting them—" He stopped talking when he noted that she wasn’t saying anything. “What?”

    Lena still kept quiet, commiting to memory the last 5 minutes. Felt like a lot more, for some reason.

    “Seriously, what? Are you hurt?” He asked again, a look of concern on his face.

    “No, I’m good.” She glanced at the window for a moment, before returning her gaze at Brainy. “So, this little outing wasn’t a complete disaster…” She commented with a little smile.

    “Aha! I knew it!” He laughed, knocking on the wheel a few times. “The tattoos were a little much but, it was simple statistics, with your similar stor-” 

    “What? No, Brainy, no. Veronica was a complete bust.” She interrupted, giving him a scandalized look. “And honestly, it is a little disconcerting that you think that we have similar stories…”

    Brainy looked at her, dumbfounded, like she had asked him the secret of the universe or something. “Then, who?”

    Lena smiled, remembering the name tag, stitched into a bulletproof vest. “What do you know of one K. Danvers?”

    He kept driving in silence, for a few minutes. “The guy from the smuggling team downtown?”

    “No. Not a he.”

    Brainy went quiet again, thinking of every fucking person in town, probably. Lena could literally pinpoint the moment he realized who she was talking about. “K. Danvers. Blonde hair, around 5’7”, quite muscular. Something plaid and blue on her person?” He asked under his breath.

    “Something like that, yeah.”

    He stomped so hard on the breaks, that the car skidded over. Luckily there was no one around them, as they were going to the warehouse over little crowded streets. “Sprock, Lena. She is a bloody DEO agent. She is part of the DEBS.” Somehow Lena was supposed to know what that last part was. It must have been clear on her face that she had no idea what it meant, because Brainy kept talking. “Unit Argo One of the DEO. They are an elite group. THE elite team, actually. They were the ones that arrested Lord, five months ago.”

    “The piece of shit had it coming. They just got to him first.”

    “She is their poster child, Lena.”

    She rolled her eyes and got out of the car. Brainy followed suit.

    “I do not think you are getting the bigger picture here.” He stood in front of her. “You know, this unit of the DEO recruits exclusively from a secret test on the SAT’s. She got the perfect score, which means the perfect agent, the perfect spy. They call her Supergirl for a reason, for god’s sake.”

    “Just give me the keys, Querl.”

    He scoffed, offended by the use of his actual name, but gave her the car key fob, anyways. She walked to the driver seat of the car, and was about to close the door when Brainy spoke again.

    “All the data we have suggests that she is straight.”

    Lena paused. Looked at him, sighed. “Let's go. Tell me what you know.”

     

     

    Argo One didn’t go to headquarters. Alex called J’onn directly and said they would go to him first thing in the morning and do a full debrief. She told him that Kara was upset about her encounter with Lena Luthor, which wasn’t quite the truth, but she appreciated the respite to think about the whole thing.

    Kara kept to herself that Alex probably felt more upset about it than her. She simply felt bummed out that they didn’t catch one Lena Luthor. Alex had done a quick physical check up on her as soon as they got home. The green gas had apparently done nothing to her, that had somewhat calmed her sister.

    It was 10:30 PM when, finally, the rest of the team left her apartment. Alex spent the last 25 minutes hugging her, clinging to her like a koala, both of them sitting on the couch, next to Kelly. 

    Kara still felt that restless energy from the restaurant. Not anxiety, more like anticipation, for some reason. When the clock on the wall said it was almost 11:20 PM and she still felt antsy she decided to go for a jog. She changed her clothes, and connected her earbuds to her phone before leaving her apartment through the fire escape, not to alert Alex of her outing. She was almost at ground level when her cellphone rang, James’ picture showing up on the screen. She almost didn’t answer, but she felt like she owed it to him.

    “Hi, James”

    “Hey. Are you okay? I heard what happened.”

    “Yeah. She didn’t shoot me, so that’s good. She is very sneaky, though.” She tried to joke. James kept quiet. “Hey, did you find your dad’s bracelet after the whole shoot out thing?”

    “Yes."

    Kara let out a sigh at his clipped tone. “Jimmy, listen, I know you want to talk about us, even if you don’t feel like it’s a good time. So, I will give you the answers, even if I know you won't like them.”

    “We don’t need to do that now.”

    “I prefer we do.” She said quickly, grimacing because there was no gentle way to say what she had to. “I broke up with you because I don’t love you. Not like that. There is no spark between us. We have everything in common and yet…”

    “I guess it is true what they say.” James said after a rather uncomfortable silence.

    “What?”

    “No one is good enough for miss perfect score. For Supergirl.”

    “Don’t be a jerk, James, come on. I do care about you.”

    “Just not enough, apparently.”

    “You know what, James? I’m gonna hang up, before any of us say something that can’t be taken back.” Kara said exasperated. “Because, in case you have forgotten, we will be in each other's lives, whether we like it or not until further notice. Because our sisters are in a relationship. So let’s not make things more awkward than they need to be. Take care.”

    She hung up and took a deep breath, closing her eyes trying to calm down. Telling herself that he was just hurt and angry, he didn’t really think what he was saying.

    “He sounds like an asshole.” An accented voice said behind her. Kara opened her eyes, because she recognized it and… fuck.

    “What are you doing here?” She asked, turning around, taking a defensive stand. Lena Luthor was standing there, as if she wasn’t a wanted criminal. “Came to finish the job?”

    Lena laughed. “Finish the job? What job exactly?”

    Kara was so stupid. Leaving her apartment at this hour, without telling Alex, or anyone. Without taking any weapon. And now she would die, and her sister would bring her back from the dead, just to kill her again for being so stupid.

    “To kill me, of course.”

    Lena kept smiling, looking at her with amusement in her eyes.

    “Or you came all this way to surrender? Given that I already read you your rights.” Kara continued.

    The brunette laughed again. “You’re funny.” She said putting both of her hands in her pockets. “None of that. Truth be told, I was actually thinking about our conversation. About my profile; and I found myself really curious about what exactly that file says. Then, I thought that we should go grab a drink and talk about it. I was gonna climb my way up, but since you’re already here...”

    Kara kept quiet. Incredulous about the nonchalance Lena was displaying. Two hours prior they both had been in a shootout, the brunette had at least 4 government agencies pursuing her, and here she was talking about having drinks.

    “Are you joking?”

    “No, not really.  Do you prefer wine or something stronger? Or maybe beer?” Lena took a step, not closer to her but to the side, and Kara used the opportunity to grab her by the lapels of her suit jacket and pin her to the nearest wall.

    Lena smirked, and showed her hands, in mock surrender. “Kinky. I like it.”

    “You’re under arrest.”

    “I don’t think so, no.” Lena was calm and collected, and that gave Kara pause. “You see, for all you know, I planted some bombs in this very building, enough to bring it completely down. My associate could have clear instructions to activate them should I not return to the car. Or it could be any other building in this street. Or there could be no bombs. But you wouldn’t know until it was too late.“

    Kara kept her eyes on Lena’s, no hesitation on her voice, no deviation on her sight line. It was true, she had no way to know if there were any bombs until she searched for them, and in the meantime if there were bombs, Luthor could detonate them in any moment. There were at least 30 people in her building alone. She thought about Alex, and Kelly and Nia. And Mrs. Stein and her cute english cocker spaniel, Luca. It wasn’t worth the risk.

    “So, Am I still under arrest, or are we going to get a drink?”

    Kara very slowly, let her hands drop. Letting Lena go. No idea how she would get out of this one alive. But at least she could minimize the casualties to just herself if she went with Lena. “Drinks, it seems.”

    “Kara?”

    Well, again, fuck. That was Nia, coming up out of the lobby.

    “I saw you coming down, I got worried.” Her mentee kept talking, ignorant of the danger they found themselves in.

    “It’s a party, then.” Lena said after a few silent moments, still calm, smiling at Kara, her eyes crinkling around the edges. Nia made a choked up sound, from the back of her throat, when she registered who was the owner of that voice.

    Kara looked at Nia, trying to keep a confident look, to not spook her even more. And even looking, if for some reason, the youngling was armed so they could try and get away somehow. She wasn’t.

    “We have to go with her. She has taken precautions.” She turned to Lena. “We are going, please lead the way.”

    Rather than go ahead of them, Lena sandwiched herself between the both of them and put her hands on their shoulders, guiding them to a silver 4-door sedan, a Porsche, Kara noticed, parked in the street.

    “Brainy if you could…” Lena said, opening the passenger door. Brainiac gave her an annoyed look and got out before going to the back seat. Kara supposed he wasn’t really happy that Lena went to look for them. Lena smiled at her, and she took that as an order to sit on the newly free spot. Lena closed the door once she was inside and Kara immediately looked in Nia's direction. She was okay, Lena also guided her to the other free seat in the car, next to Brainiac, in the back. She was almost polite, closing Nia’s door, telling her to mind her hands. Finally, she jumped on the driver seat, starting the car. It must be one of the electric ones because the engine was incredibly silent, that she wouldn’t think it was on, if it weren’t because she just saw Lena pushing the damn button.

    “I’m gonna need you both to give him your phones, lest someone dares to interrupt our night out.” 

    Kara got her phone from the pocket of her jacket, and gave it to Querl Dox. Nia did the same.

    “I don’t think I got your names,” Lena said a few minutes later, over the low indie rock music that the radio played.

    “Where are we going?” She asked instead.

    “I did promise drinks, I believe. And you didn’t answer either of my questions.”

    “Why are you asking her name?” Querl spoke up. “You know her name is Kara Danvers.”

    “Brainy…” Lena tried to talk, her companion kept going, undeterred.

    “And this is Miss Nia Nal. Youngest member of the Argo One, at 24. She graduated from Georgetown University, with a degree in International Relations. And was personally recommended to the team by Major General Grant.”

    Kara felt like she was going to vomit. How does this guy have all that information? Nia made a sound that was somehow between a gulp and a groan. And Lena let out a sigh.

    “I asked their names, because it is the polite thing to ask, Brainy. And I only knew Kara’s name because you told me. It’s a bit stalkerish, you knowing so much about them without them knowing you.” She explained to him calmly. 

    “Well, I take your security quite seriously. That means knowing everything about the people that wants to do you harm.” Brainiac retorted, defensively.

    “And I appreciate it, Brainy.” Lena smiled, before looking at Nia through the rear view mirror. “It’s nice to meet you, Miss Nal. I’m Lena Luthor. The little stalker next to you is Querl, he likes being called Brainy. We’re very sorry for his stalkerish ways.”

    Nia kept quiet, Kara could see through the side mirror next to her, that Brainiac was ramrod tense behind her, shooting glares at Lena.

    “Where are we going?” Kara asked again, having no idea where the tunnel they found themselves led to. 

    Lena shrugged, before answering. “To Oblivion. Don’t worry, it’s a very safe bar. No one will bother us there. You’ll like it. It even has arcade games, if you’re into that sort of thing.”

    Kara hummed. She was into that sort of thing, but this wasn’t the moment to play arcade games. She was still trying to think of a way to get home alive. Or to get Nia home safe, at the very least. The initial concrete tunnel had been replaced by another made of dirt after several turns; she was sure that the road didn’t exist in any city map. Come to think of it, she wasn’t even sure if they were still in the city. 

    The road changed from asphalt to gravel, and they parked among a wide variety of cars in front of a single storey building. Lena turned off the car, and opened her door. Brainiac did the same and they both got out. When neither Nia or Kara moved, Lena’s head peeked back into the car.

    “Those tunnels go for kilometers. You would end up lost for weeks. Come on, I just want to talk.”

    Kara didn’t move. Neither did Nia.

    Lena shrugged. “Okay, then. Suit yourself. But remember the precautions.” Lena closed the car door again, and went her merry way to the bar, next to Brainiac. They appeared to be arguing about something. A couple of minutes later, Nia opened her door, cowering at Kara’s glare.

    “Where are you going?” She hissed.

    “She’s right, It’s better that we go with her. And I need to pee, so…” Nia got out of the car, and followed their captors to the bar. Halfway through she turned around to look at Kara and made a gesture for her to go with her. Kara sighed, and got out of the car, too. Truth be told, it was better to stick together.

    The place was certainly packed. The music wasn’t too loud, but the people playing games, drinking and talking were. They didn’t attract many looks, but definitely attracted Querl and Lena’s.

    “I’m gonna find that bathroom.” She heard Nia say, but her eyes were stuck on the infamous Luthor, who smiled at her, and moved her head, signaling Kara to follow her. Kara did, like an idiot.

    Lena took a couple of beers from a busboy walking around and approached a booth already occupied by a group of people. They looked directly at Lena for maybe half a second before scrambling to get out of her way. She turned around, and smiled at Kara, waiting for her to sit.

    Kara waited a moment, but she sat down. The brunette put one beer in front of Kara, and sat next to her. “So, Kara,” Lena started, an amused smile on her face. “Who was the jerk you were talking to on the phone earlier?”

    She had to make a decision, how exactly was she gonna play this? She could keep quiet, the woman couldn’t possibly have a unlimited patience, she would get tired of her and get her back to her home. Or that could backfire and she would detonate the bombs just for shit and giggles. It was better to talk, and try to gain any information. Maybe she could make enough time for Nia to escape and… get lost in the tunnels. Maybe she could ask for a phone and call the team. Honestly, she was running on hope, not on strategy.

    “You’re gonna talk to me sooner or later.”

    “It was my boyfriend. Ex. My ex-boyfriend. Jimmy.” She finally answered. Lena, who was taking a sip of her own beer, nodded.

    “I see. I assume it wasn’t a friendly break up.”

    “It’s… complicated. It’s fairly recent. Actually, we broke up today. He’s not taking it so well because… Well, I guess you heard.”

    Lena nodded again. “You have everything in common, and yet…” She repeated almost word for word what Kara had said to James earlier.

    Kara’s lips twisted into a smile without permission. “Why do you care about it?”

    “I’m just curious about you.” Lena said, cocking her head to the side. Then quickly added; “Your team. What you do. About what you have on me.”

    Kara finally grabbed the beer bottle, and took a swig of it. Not really convinced about the answer the brunette gave her, but she was sure she wasn’t going to get anything else about it, so she kept quiet looking ahead.

    “How long have you been a DEB or whatever?” Lena asked. Kara immediately snorted.

    “No one calls us that. Not to our face, at least. It’s a dismissive nickname, because all the Argo teams are 100% female.”

    “I am so sorry.” Lena quickly said. Kara frowned. “I—Today was the first time I’ve ever heard of it. I didn’t know.”

    “It's okay.” Kara was a little lost, because this entire interaction was not something she would have thought a cruel criminal mastermind was capable of having. Apologizing for a sexist remark she had no idea about. “I think it stands for 'DEO’s Extremely Beautiful Squad.'” Kara says grimacing. Yeah, Alex would kill anyone that dared to say it to their faces.

    “Yikes.”

    Kara nodded. “Though, that begs the question, how exactly did you hear about it?”

    Lena laughed, amusement dancing on her green eyes. “Okay, I’ll bite. But to keep things fair, I’ll answer your questions if you answer mine.”

    Kara looked around her. People were none the wiser about them. She could actually see Nia playing air hockey with Brainiac. They shared a look, Nia didn’t seem comfortable but she seemed safe. She really had to play the long game, and try to get them both home.

    “Very well.” She finally said, looking at Lena again. “I accept the terms of the game.”

    Lena kept her gaze on her, her eyes still amused. She swallowed her beer before answering. “As Brainy said, he likes to know about the people that could harm me. He is very good at it. He probably knows more about you than your own mother. He told me about it, I guess that’s how you guys are known on the street.”

    Kara kept quiet for a few seconds, just looking at Luthor while she ordered more drinks. Guinness. She was not sure what to make of her. Even with the knowledge of her DEO profile. She wasn’t even armed for all she could see.

    “Your turn. You can pick and answer any of the questions I’ve asked tonight.”

    Kara considered her options for a few seconds, opting for the least offensive of all the questions; “I don't drink much. When I do, I prefer wine. But beer is good, too.” She finally said, finishing the drink.

    Lena bit her lip, trying to hide a smile. “I do prefer scotch, usually, but I appreciate a good wine.”

    “Oh man, I bet you’re one of the snobby ones that swirls the wine in the glass, and if you don’t like it you send it back.”

    “There is really no sending back. You have to pay for it, anyway. The swirly thing is just to taste if the wine is damaged.”

    “I wouldn’t know. I just get the cheap stuff from the grocery store. You did seem quite knowledgeable about it at Il Palazzo.”

    “Well. I am a Luthor. That’s the type of thing we grow up knowing.”

    “What else did you grow up knowing?” Kara asks, grabbing the new beer bottle.

    “Wouldn’t you like to know?” Lena teased, smirking again. “Lots of things. I’m a genius, after all.”

    “You’re also very humble, it seems.”

    “You don’t become the COO of one of the biggest technological companies of the world at 22 by being humble.” She ruefully said.

    Kara looked at her. Some of the amused shine of her eyes had left her. She drank from her beer bottle, like she was trying to forget something. “Do you miss it?”

    Lena smiled, this time mournful. “I do. And I didn’t even want that job in the first place. I just wanted to tinker in my R&D lab.” She looked at Kara, with that same sad smile. “I bet that wasn’t on that psych eval you have.”

    Kara was truly, completely lost, and part of her wanted to actually try to make the green eyed woman feel better.

    “If you must know, we don’t have much on you, actually. The central hypothesis of the eval is that; as a woman operating in a male-dominated field you feel the need to overcompensate, by being exponentially more ruthless and diabolical than your established male counterparts.”

    “I am not ruthless.” She said, somehow offended.

    “I think an argument could be made that you are, since we're here, because you wanted to blow up a building if I didn’t.”

    “That's—well, touché. Continue.”

    Kara kept quiet for a few seconds, trying to remember stuff that Kelly had said, not only in their latest brief, but in those months they spent studying Lena alone. “We think that these psychological forces combine to create a kind of emotional void in which you're incapable of loving or being loved.”

    “See, that is so not true.”

    Now she had really offended Lena. Great.

    “What? It’s the conclusion we got from the information we have on you.”

    “I'm open to love. I—I just—I got my heart broken, okay? And wasn’t ready to move on.” She said really, really low.

    “I'm sorry. That sucks.” Kara grimaces, a groan escaping from the back of her throat a few seconds later. “And I just did the exact same thing to my boyfriend. Ex. Ex-boyfriend.” 

    Lena hummed. “I don’t think so.” A few seconds of silence run between them before she asks, “Why did you do it?”

    “I don't know, it's just, it wasn't—” She paused and took a breath, centering herself. “I think love should be irresistible, like a drug. I think when it happens you should just not be able to help yourself. And I enjoyed my time with James, I did, but it was just like one does with friends. I just— I wanted more.”

    Lena kept her eyes trained on hers. A curious gleam shining in them. “See, not the same. My ex broke up with me because us being together wasn’t easy. Not because she didn’t love me, but because loving someone else would be easier than being with me.” She shrugged. “Anyways, that was two years ago. She moved on quite fast, I needed a little more time.”

    “Why aren’t you killing me?” She blurted out, after a minute or so of silence.

    “I am not a killer.” Lena calmly said.

    “But you killed those agents in Iceland.” Kara rebutted.

    “I didn’t even know there were agents in Iceland. I was watching the northern lights and studying the atmospheric climate change there. Brainy told me, a few days later, that they found them dead, there was a snowstorm around those days.”

    “And those DEA guys in Peru?” Kara asked, now really curious. Could they really have everything wrong about her? 

    “First of all, I don’t do or have anything to do with drugs. And second of all, I think it was ebola.”

    “But they found your rocks.” Kara said rather quickly.

    Lena laughed at that. “I was in the area. And those things, I accidentally drop them everywhere.” She searched her pockets and showed her hand full of the green stuff, “It’s mostly for my brother. He was obsessed with this myth when we were children. I created Kryptonite for him.” She pocketed the rocks again.

    “You're really not what I expected.”

    “Well, I think that's a good thing.” Lena quipped.

    “So, what's up with wannabe Lisbeth Salander?” Kara mocked.

    “Oh, God.” The dark-haired woman groaned, covering her face with both of her hands. “Brainy made me go, he thought we could hit it off because we’re both the black sheep of billionaire families. We met in boarding school, we didn’t get along then, don’t think we could go anywhere now, our priorities are opposite of each other.” She finished, dropping one of her arms along the back of the booth.

    Kara smiled. “Well, I think you could do better. Illegal fight clubs don’t seem like your style.”

    “Not at all. It lacks finesse. Besides the whole illegal thing.”

    Kara frunced her brows, a criminal reproaching the illegality of things was weird, but she said nothing about it, simply filed it to think about it later.

    “I forgot. You prefer the kind of fights where you can win a medal, like the Olympics.”

    “I prefer not to fight. But if there is no other option, then I prefer fights where there are a defined set of rules.”

    That was also weird. She really wasn’t anything like her crimes made her look like. She wasn’t violent, not only because she said she wasn’t, but because even when Kara had grabbed her, her first instinct was to raise her hands in surrender. She could have easily shot her right in the head back in the restaurant, but she didn’t. She didn’t like Roulette’s fight club. Could they have missed something about notorious criminal Lena Luthor?

    “Why didn’t you go to the Olympics?” Curiosity was really getting the better of her.

    “I was working on my mechanical engineering PhD at MIT. And I was taking an online MBA course. I didn’t have the time.” She shrugged.

    “You were only 19, I was a sophomore in university, going to frat parties and getting wasted.”

    Lena shrugs, again. “What did you study?”

    “Journalism. I—my older cousin is a Journalist. At the time, I wanted to live in Metropolis and be closer to him, because he’s my only blood relative still alive. And I wanted to tell the truth… but when I finished, I got offered the DEO job, and Clark and I never really had a close relationship. So I figured, I could choose my own family, and I chose my adoptive sister over my biological cousin. Can’t say I regret it.” She shrugged too, giving Lena a tiny smile.

    Lena looked in Brainiac and Nia's direction, a tiny smile dancing on her lips. “Yeah, I know what you mean.” She was looking at Querl, Kara deduced. Her chosen family. “My mom drowned, right in front of me. The Luthors, they—they adopted me when I was four. Lex was the only one that made me feel like I belonged. At least he did at the beginning. Then Lionel, our father, he,” She paused, looking for a good descriptor, furrowing her brows a little. ''He started favoring me, I guess. Lex didn’t like that much. We grew apart. Lionel died when I was 16. I didn’t know he was my biological father until I was in my twenties.” Her smile was a little sad. “At least that explained why my adoptive mother hated my guts, I suppose.”

    “I’m very sorry, that must have been very hard.” Kara said, her eyes on Lena’s profile. She seemed pensive. “My parents died in a house fire. I was thirteen. My cousin found himself in the same situation my father did when his parents died, too young to take care of some kid. He was in his early twenties. He knew my parents were friends, or friendly, I guess, with the Danvers, because I had never met them, and they were kind enough to adopt me. My sister, Alex, also hated my guts back then.”

    "What changed?" Lena asked.

    "Our father, Jeremiah, went MIA. They never found him. Mom found herself with two teenage daughters, only one job, and university prospects on the horizon. We had to get along, take care of each other. Wasn't easy, and it didn't happen overnight, but now I can't imagine my life without her in it. She's my best friend." She finished, a smile on her face.

    "That sounds nice.” Lena smiled back. “I can’t remember the last time I talked to my best friend. Not Brainy. She… I couldn’t bring her to this world. She has a daughter to take care of. What I’m doing… It’s dangerous. I can’t even bear the thought of anything happening to them because of me.”

    Kara was silent. So was Lena, pensive, drinking her beer. Kara wasn't sure she had realized the amount of information she had freely given. And if she did, It didn’t seem part of some diabolical plan. Even if it was, Kara found herself… enchanted by her. She felt like—like if it were any other circumstance, if Lena was anyone other than herself, she would love to invite her to hang out with her friends. To know more, to know everything about her.

    “Do you ever feel like nobody else ever fully understands you?” She asked instead, in a whisper.

    Lena let out a breathless chuckle. “All the time.”

    They both kept quiet. A thoughtful silence. Comfortable. Each sipping on their beers.

    “Did you satisfy your curiosity about what we have on you? I really should be sleeping” Kara said minutes later, thinking about how late actually was.

    “Not really, but I guess you will need some sleep. To work tomorrow in catching the bad guys and stuff.” Lena said, a flirtatious smile on her lips.

    “Will I see you again?” She blurted out. Didn’t even realize she wanted to ask that, until it was already out of her mouth.

    “Do you want to see me again?” Lena smiled.

    “I mean, I'm breaking, like, eight federal statutes,—being here with you right now.”

    “Yes, I know.” Lena said, looking directly at her eyes. Kara realized there was some blue on one of her green eyes.

    “I could get kicked out of the DEO for this.” She muttered, her eyes dropping to Lena’s lips, without her explicit consent. Like a moth to a flame.

    “For what?” Lena asked in a whisper, inching closer to her. They were very close, she could see a little scar next to Lena's right eye.

    “Oh my god.” 

    Kara practically jumped out of her skin at the scandalized voice of Nia. Her lips were so close to Lena’s she could almost feel their warmth. She drew away from her, sliding along the booth, getting up.

    “She was gonna kiss you.” Nia said, scandalized. “And you were gonna let her.”

    “You owe me 50 dollars.” Brainiac smugly said to Nia.

    “Kara…” Lena muttered.

    “Take me home.” It was the only thing she said, hugging herself, and walking away searching for the exit. She felt like an open wound. She was literally caught with her hand in the proverbial cookie jar. And she had no idea she wanted the damn cookie, until it was too late. And now that she realized it, she really wanted it, if it weren’t for the whole treason thing.

    She found her way out, and got close to the car, waiting for the other three. She didn’t say a word when Lena unlocked the car. She didn’t say anything when they left the parking lot or when they exited the tunnels. She kept quiet, looking through the passenger door window. Yet she could feel Lena’s eyes on her, while she was driving. And she could hear Nia and Brainiac speaking in hushed tones in the back seat.

    “Well, when can I see you again?” Lena finally asked in a muted tone, when she parked in front of their building.

    “You can't.” Kara answered with finality. Nia and Brainiac got out of the car, and she opened her door.

    “Come on,” Lena uttered, she went to grab Kara’s arm, but stopped at the last second.

    “I'm serious. We—You cannot do this again.”

    “Kara.” She beseeched her.

    “What about the bombs?” Kara asked instead.

    “There are no bombs.” Lena answered, Kara nodded, then; “Kara, when can I see you again?” she pleaded again. 

    “I have to go.” Kara finally got out of the car, catching up with Nia, closer to the apartment building.

    “I'm gonna come find you.” Lena loudly said, out of the car.

    Kara kept walking next to Nia, still holding herself. Nia gave her her phone, Brainiac must have given her both.

    “What just happened?” Nia asked in a low voice. While they climbed to the elevator.

    “Nothing happened.”

    “She was totally going to kiss you. You're so into her.” Nia accused her.

    “I am not!” She hissed. Jamming her finger on the button to her floor. 

    “You violated a prime directive. You're so busted.”

    “No, I'm not.” She turned to her mentee, once the elevator doors had closed. “You violated the section codes just as much as me. DEO Code Section 1, Article 2: ‘Agents will not consort with a known enemy, under penalty of treason.’”

    Nia gasped, offended. “I was not consorting.”

    “I saw you giving your phone number to Brainiac.”

    “That's not the same.” The youngling kept pushing.

    Kara had to bring out the big guns to make her drop the whole thing. “You got drunk and nearly got the chancellor of Bulgaria killed.”

    “That wasn't my fault. I thought the seltzer was non-alcoholic!” Nia gasped, again.

    “Remember how I covered for you? I never told anybody. If Alex or J’onn found out about that, they would demote you, and send you to the freakin’ desert.”

    “I can't believe you.”

    “Not a word. Nothing happened.” Kara said, as imposing as she could muster. Not her finest hour but, what was she supposed to do? Confess that she was certainly intrigued by the youngest Luthor? Yeah, not a chance. 

    She walked to her apartment door, Nia silent on her heels. She opened her door, exhausted by the whole ordeal, trying to relax, thinking of her bed.

    “Where the hell were you?” Alex’s voice screeched, as soon as she entered her place.

    Kara froze, this night just kept getting better and better. Goddamnit.

    Chapter End Notes
