    Red Herrings

    Chapter Notes

    “Jesus Christ, Alex. You scared the shit out of me,” Kara says, closing her door, once Nia got inside. Her sister and Kelly were sitting on her couch. Alex’s hair was sticking out in odd angles so she had been sleeping or racking her hand through it. Either way, she probably came in to check on her and didn’t find her. She would have been spiraling, hence Kelly and the cups of tea they both had in front of them. 

    “Well?” Alex prompted, looking both at Kara and Nia. Kara had to say something or Nia would throw her under the bus. Because Alex is fucking scary when she’s angry.

    “I—I was upset. James called and… He said some things.” Kara mumbled. It was true. Jimmy had called, and Jimmy had said some things, she hoped that would be enough to fool her sister.

    Alex looked at Kelly, who put a hand on her girlfriend's forearm. 

    “He did call me. He was… very upset.” Kelly said in a whisper, trying to hide a grimace.

    “Kara, you can’t go out alone, it’s dangerous. Luthor could be looking for you.” Alex uttered, after exchanging looks with her girlfriend.

    Nia snorted, behind her, but she covered it with a cough. “Well, that’s why I went with her.” She said after she calmed her badly disguised chuckle.

    “Yes,” Kara quickly added. “I didn’t wanna bother you, guys. Because he’s your family too.”

    Alex and Kelly shared a look. A few really charged seconds later, Alex sighed with relief, her shoulders visibly relaxing. “What did he say? Do I have to kick his ass?”

    Kara hesitated, James was Alex’s brother-in-law, even if Kara wasn’t really that upset about it, and the excuse was only an escape goat, Alex giving him an ass kicking wasn’t gonna bode well for the family reunions in the future. “I don’t think so. He’s just hurt.” 

    “Kara.”

    She flinched at Alex’s tone. Sighed, just to reply in a very small voice. “He said that, apparently, it was true what they said about me. That I believed that no one was enough for me, Supergirl. No one was good enough to miss perfect score.”

    Alex blinked really, really slow, like she was calibrating her brain, trying to gauge what was an appropriate response to the presented information. “Babe, I don’t think your brother will give you any nieces or nephews. Because I’m gonna kick him so hard, his balls are gonna shrink up, right up his body.”

    “Alex!” Kara scolded her sister.

    “Okay, we can talk about you castrating my brother in the morning. It’s really late, we should all be asleep. Come on, everyone to bed.” Kelly tried to calm Alex down, gently pushing her out of the apartment.

    She smiled at Kara, pushing her girlfriend back to her apartment. Kara shared a look with Nia, shaking her head imperceptibly, shutting up any sarcastic comment the youngling could have.

    “See you guys in the morning.” Kara said, gently pushing Nia out too.

    Once she found herself alone, she got her phone out and tried to search the GPS’ last location. It was her apartment. And before that, nothing. Brainiac must have deleted everything from it.

    What the hell was she supposed to do now? She had a debrief in… She looked at the clock on the cellphone screen, it was almost 2.00 AM. She had a debrief in 6 hours, how was she supposed to omit the fact that not only did she have a full on conversation with their suspect, but she also almost kissed her. And she actually liked her. And she wasn’t so sure they were on the right track with her.

    She opened her door as quietly as possible and goes down the stairs again. She had to check around the place, make sure that Lena was saying the truth and there were no bombs in the building. She didn’t have much time, so she went to the basement. The most logical place to plant explosives in her opinion, where luckily she only found cleaning supplies, tools and the boilers.

    No C4, no TNT, no weird chemical compound that goes boom.

    Kara got back to her apartment, carefully closing her door and went through the motions in automatic. Changed to her pajamas, cleaned her face, brushed her teeth, got her contact lenses out of her tired eyes, and went to bed, but sleep was avoiding her. She learned a lot about the youngest Luthor. And yet, she knew nothing about her goals. She mentioned studying atmospheric pressure in Iceland, and the northern lights, but… How does that relate to her crimes? She had also said something about tinkering in a Lab back in LuthorCorp. Was that a clue?

     

    Without having slept at all, she got up when her alarm started screaming at 6.30 am. She had no idea where the time had gone. She went through the motions again, in automatic. Brushed her teeth, showered, and dressed herself in her tactical suit, without the bulletproof vest. That went into a gym bag, along with the weapons and surveillance equipment she had in her possession. She was lacing her combat boots, when she remembered she was missing her glasses, they were back in the DEO’s locker room. She groaned, not really in the mood to fight with contacts when her eyes were sore from lack of sleep. The door opened to show Alex, already dressed.

    “Are you ready?” Her sister tilted her head to one side, she must have a dreadful face because Alex was already giving her worried eyes. “Are you okay? Did you sleep at all?”

    “Not really. I had too many things in my head. And I just realized my glasses are back in the DEO, so.” She got up, and grabbed the gym bag. “Let’s go, you’re driving.” She said, trying to lighten the mood. And It worked, because Alex immediately smiled.

    “I always drive. Come on, breakfast is on me today.”

    “This day is so much better, already.”

    Kelly was outside Alex’s door, waiting for them, they greeted each other with a smile. Alex and Kelly interlocked fingers and walked together to the elevator, Kara on their heels. The elevator door opened, and one Nia Nal, was trying to sleep on the corner closer to the buttons.

    “Bad Night?” Kelly asked, softly to the youngster.

    “Long Night. Full of nightmares, faceless assassins that turned out to be my friends. I really shouldn’t let Kara feed me before bed.”

    Kara glared at her, knowing that the entire thing was one dig at her, Alex snorted.

    “That is actually a good rule. She only eats trash.”

    “Hey!” Kara protested, albeit weakly, when they were exiting the main entrance of the building, a tiny smile on her lips, because of all the teasing. A green reflection immediately got her attention. Close to the fire escape stairs where she had pinned Lena to the wall. She pretended to kneel to re-tie her shoes, to grab the green stone.

    Lena was really not lying when she said she dropped those things everywhere. 

    “You okay?” Kelly questioned, looking at her from the side of the car.

    “Yeah, yeah”

     

    The trip to the DEO was a blur. She wasn’t even sure if she played the daily Nsync song. Her mind was overworking itself in trying to pierce together something that made sense about what she learned about Lena Luthor, last night. She stopped at the entrance of the Canteen, and turned to Alex. 

    “Um. Order me something, I need to get my glasses, I think I’m gonna get a headache.” She didn’t wait for an answer, she turned around and went to the locker room in a daze.

    She took a deep breath, she was already regretting not sleeping at all, and the day hadn’t even begun. She still hadn’t decided if she was gonna tell about her escapade with Lena Luthor. She put on her glasses, leaving her bag in the locker before leaving the room herself. She was on her way back to the canteen when another Argo team intercepted her. 

    “Is it true? Did you fight Lena Luthor?” One of them asked, so fast that the words almost melded together.

    “I heard you cut off her hand. Does she have her hand?” Another one questioned, also really fast.

    “Yes, she still has both of her hands. God.” Kara, somehow sidestepped them, and kept walking until she found herself in front of Nia.

    “Everybody's talking about it.” She remarked.

    “About what?” Kara inquired, her mouth suddenly very dry.

    “How you met Lena Luthor, and lived to tell about it. They're calling you a hero, when in reality you were three seconds away from getting laid.”

    “Oh, shut up.”

    “I didn’t even know you liked women.”

    “Well, that’s on you.” Kara quipped.

    “Major General Grant wants to see you.” Now it was Nia turn to sidestep her. She kept walking in direction to their deck. She could hear the mocking in the little shit’s voice. 

    “WHAT!? Nia!” Kara turned around, headache increasing exponentially, hunger completely forgotten, and followed Nia, like an over stressed puppy. “What's going on?” She asked, as soon as she got on deck, joining Nia, Kelly and Alex.

    “Major General Grant.” Alex said, as if that is answer enough.

    “She's coming down.” Kelly finished for her girlfriend.

    “Here? Why? She never comes here.” Kara voiced, the anxiety slipping away in her tone.

    She didn’t get an answer from her teammates, because the woman in question just entered the room. Everyone kept quiet for a few seconds, then Alex spoke up.

    “Major General, I would just like to say—”

    “Who are you?” The tiny blonde interrupted, with a hand movement. A no nonsense expression on her face.

    “Alex Danvers, Ma’am.”

    “Alex Danvers, my time is precious. You're wasting it.” She paused, then continued walking until she was in front of the big screen on the deck. “Where's the girl?”

    Another woman, a few inches taller than Major Grant, brunette, her assistant, Kara realized, called out. “Danvers comma Kara?”

    She swallowed, it felt like sand. She took a step forward, separating herself from her team.

    “You. Excellent.”

    Suddenly, Major Grant’s companion had a camera and was taking pictures of them. 

    “Well, take one over here. Right there. Excellent.” The tiny blonde kept moving Kara along the entire deck, taking pictures together. She wasn’t sure how many or how good they were but… it  was a lot. Finally they stopped, Cat Grant in front of her.

    “First....” She said, snapping her fingers at her assistant.

    “Kara.” The other woman supplied.

    “Kara. Let me just say how delighted I am to meet you. When I heard that one of our own girls had actually engaged Lena Luthor and lived to tell the tale. I mean, Wells, over at Central Intelligence, nearly peed his pants, he was so jealous. This stunt is really going to get the DEO a bigger budget.” Cat Grant expressed, poised, but brimming with enthusiasm.

    “Quentin Lance from Interpol called to extend his congratulations.” The brunette who was accompanying her, voiced.  

    Kara swears to God that Cat Freaking Grant giggled.

    “I'll bet. This is just too much fun! Now, Kiera-”

    “Kara.” the assistant corrected.

    “Kara, we are going to need a description of the encounter.”

    “The encounter?” Kara somehow squeaked. She wasn't sure how she wasn't spewing sand, her throat was like a fucking desert.

    “Everything that happened. No matter how small or seemingly irrelevant.” The Major responded, like she was talking to a toddler.

    “Why?”

    “To develop a new profile, of course. Nobody's ever been this close to criminal Lena Luthor before. You, for all intents and purposes, are our leading expert.”

    Somewhere in the room, Nia snickered, covering it up again with a fit of coughs.

    “I don't think I'm an expert at all.” Kara said instead. Because what the hell was she supposed to say?

    “You underestimate yourself. I took the liberty of accessing your private files.” Cat Grant said, moving her hand for her assistant, who gave her a black folder. She opened it, looking for something in it.

    “What?” Kara squeaked, flabbergasted at the blatant breach of privacy, but then again, they were basically spies.

    "It's a Man's World: Lena Luthor and the Psychology of Cultural Criminality. By Team Argo One." The Major read.

    “Oh, God.” Kara cringed. It was the profile that Kelly and herself were doing months ago. 

    "Lena Luthor is a genius, and a sociopath. Her crimes could be viewed as a desperate cry for help. The more she steals and destroys, the deeper her feeling of emptiness.” The woman made a dramatic pause, leaving the folder on the deck's surface. “Kyra—”

    “Kara.” Her assistant corrected, again.

    “I think you identify with Lena Luthor.” Major Grant declared, and Kara couldn’t stop the way she straightened up. Standing to her full height at the insinuation. “I think she sees your pathos. You've got some dangerous symbiosis going on.” Ms. Grant kept going. “What's that movie with Jodie and the dog falling in the well with the lotion?”

    “Silence of the Lambs.” The assistant supplied.

    “You're the lamb, Keira. We can use that to our advantage. I'm putting you in charge of this investigation.”

    “What?” Alex and Kara ask at the same time, astonished.

    “You are hereby promoted to Team Leader.”

    “No.” She heard herself and Alex say, even if her sister’s negative was nothing more than a whisper.

    “Is there a problem?” Major General Grant inquired.

    “Excuse me, ma'am, but I am the leader of this team.” Alex declared, taking a step forward.

    “Miss...?”

    “Danvers. Alex.” 

    “Danvers. Alex. There is a killer on the loose. There is no time for egos. This is not the Girl Scouts, this is a government agency, and you’re part of an elite team. We have a chance to do something here.” She shifted her gaze to Kara, again. “You have a chance to do something. Don't let us down. Ready your troops. And find out everything about Lena Luthor. I want to know everything about her. That’s all. Chop Chop.” Cat Grant finished clapping her hands, and then stalked out of the room, as if she hadn’t destroyed the professional relationship of the Danvers sisters.

    “Alex,” Kara breathed as soon as their blonde boss is out of the way.

    “What?” Her sister asked, cold and distant. Kara sighed.

    “I didn't mean for this to happen, I swear. I don't even wanna be Team Leader.”

    “That's the thing, Kara. You don't even want it. You get the perfect score, you don’t follow orders, and Major Grant makes you Team Captain.”

    “Alex…” 

    “Just—Whatever. It's fine. Just promise me one thing.” 

    “What? Whatever you want.”

    “You catch the bitch.” Alex demanded, fierce determination in her eyes. Kara didn’t think her mouth could be any dryer, she was wrong.

    “I… We’ll catch her.” She somehow promised.

     

    Kara had to turn around and avoid the eyes of everyone in the room. She rested the palm of her hands on the deck and tried to compose herself. She had a job to do, without revealing her little rendezvous with Lena Luthor the night before. It didn’t matter that she liked said criminal. (She had eyes, for Christ sake). But she was a ruthless and cruel criminal, right? She couldn’t ponder for much longer, because J’onn showed up. And the mission debrief began.

    She recounted almost everything, how they weren’t the only agency there, Jimmy, the bracelet drop, the wine cork, Roulette firing first. How the team splitted according to plan, why she ordered Nia to go back and find the others, why she continued alone and didn’t wait for backup. She mentioned how she read Lena Luthor her rights, how she didn’t shoot her because she was also aiming her gun at her head. How she got distracted for just a second to call for her teammates, when the target escaped, leaving only Kryptonite behind.

    She doesn’t mention how Lena was actually on a date. She doesn’t mention that they didn’t shoot each other because in reality they were bantering. She particularly doesn’t mention how notorious criminal mastermind Lena Luthor went to their building to seek her out. Or how they went for drinks together. She especially doesn't mention how they almost kissed. How she felt like she connected with her in a way she hasn’t connected with anyone in years. Or maybe ever.

    Nia didn’t say anything either. J’onn looked at her like he knew that she was hiding something.

    “Are you sure she didn’t say or did anything else?” He politely asked.

    “I… I don’t know. It all happened quite fast.”

    “Do we have an idea about what they were planning?” J’onn spoke again.

    Kara kept quiet, she could feel Nia’s drilling gaze on her, it was her that said something. Surprisingly not to accuse her, but to save her ass.

    “Well, she did mention global domination to Roulette. And we thought that red wine, white wine and vodka were codes for something.”

    “Yes,” Kara automatically said, even if she knew that information was useless, because they were only flirting. That thought for some reason, gave her a full body shudder. “It could be.”

    “We can start with that.” Alex agreed. 

    “Very well,” J’onn said as he walked to the door. “Get to work and keep me posted.”

    “Yes, sir.” Alex waits until J’onn was out of earshot to add, “Sorry, our breakfast got sidetracked.”

    “Doesn’t matter. We can eat later.” Kara answered. She should really tell Alex about Lena Luthor’s visit. She really should. There was no reason for her to hide it. Other than cute expressive green eyes, a killer jawline, a knowing smirk, and magnetic personality, but that was it. “I’ll go get us coffee, and then, let the work begin.”

     

    And work they did, when she came back with enough coffee for the four of them. They worked relentlessly, almost for the entire week. Foolishly, to be honest. Because Kara knew they would get nothing, because it was all bullshit.

    Kelly downloaded all the pictures she took in the restaurant. Nia did the same with the audio recording, and they listened to it incessantly. If Kara held her breath every time she heard Lena’s voice, no one had to know.

    Alex and Kara got back to Il Palazzo, showing their fake FBI badges to enter the place. They asked about wines, what brand was Lena drinking, what vodka was Roulette’s martini made with, where was the caviar from. They retraced their steps since their drop from the ceiling. And yet, Kara kept her mouth shut about the entire thing being a waste of time, because wine was just wine, and Lena Luthor was just on a date, not planning to bend the world to her will.

    Kara didn’t sleep anymore, she just crashed briefly, dreaming of heterochromatic green eyes, whenever her body needed to recharge. And yet, she had this constant restless energy that made her jittery, like there was electricity coursing through her veins. So much so that she needed to go to the gym in the middle of her work day, just to try and calm herself. (And no, the amount of coffee she was consuming didn’t have anything to do with it, thank you very much). She didn’t eat as much as normal, something Alex kept repeating, like it was some sort of worrisome occurrence. 

    She was using her time alone in her apartment to research Lena Luthor prior to her criminal activities. Nia had somehow gotten in touch with Mount Helena’s and had convinced them to give her Lena’s personal file of her time at boarding school. Kara had taken a copy back to her apartment. Kelly was almost taking her degree again, pouring over her old psychology books, their previous profile of Lena always next to her, adding things to its margins and crossing out other parts. Kara tried to memorize everything she could see about Kelly’s annotations, and then google about it in the privacy of her home. She even purchased one of the most fancy and expensive VPN’s to do it. The only actual wall in her bedroom (since the others were a window, a door to the bathroom, and a cute curtain to separate it from the living room), had become a murder board, with everything she could find about Lena Luthor, and her theories about her. 

    On their fourth day, late in the afternoon, Alex had joined the team on the DEO deck and announced that she found out that Lena was adopted. Of course, Kara already knew that, and she kept to herself that while adopted, she was Lionel Luthor’s biological daughter. Instead, she congratulated her sister on her findings, as she went to Kelly to add to their new profile how ‘because Lena was adopted, she was determined in proving that she was worthy of the Luthor name just as much as her genius brother, even if it meant being a notorious criminal.’ or something along those lines. 

    While the adoption paperwork was under annoyingly dense red tape, Alex had been able to get a couple of things out of it. Like Lena’s age at the time of her adoption, four. Her mother's name, Elizabeth Walsh. Her birthplace, Ireland. And how there was no father name listed. 

    Kelly and Alex, kept the rest of that day trying to find anything about Elizabeth Walsh and exactly why Lena was actually listed as American everywhere, instead of Irish. Kara had to remind her sister that she wasn’t American by birth either, but she was still legally American. Kelly concluded that going to Ireland to boarding school was not only out of the prestige of the institution but likely motivated by the emotional connection to the place. 

    Kara filled all the information on her mind, to unpack later over the investigation board on the intimacy of her bedroom.

     

    That same night, Nia showed up at her apartment, a murderous shine on her eyes.

    “How long are you gonna let us go on this wild-goose chase?” She demanded as soon as the door closed behind her. Her voice was more of a whisper-shout than anything else. Probably not to alert Alex or Kelly.

    “I don’t know, Nia.” Kara sat down on her couch, cradling her head in her hands, repentant. “What am I supposed to do? I feel like there is something I’m missing.”

    “Missing something? You’re not missing anything, Kara. You’re just wasting time, not only yours but your entire team’s, resources, government money, and for what? A pretty face?” Nia kept the same volume, but her tone was clearly exasperated. “She’s one of the most wanted criminals, she’s on at least three different lists, it’s our job to bring her to justice. We don’t even have to mention that she came here, simply tell us something useful to find her. And we can put this entire thing behind us.”

    Kara remained quiet. It was true, except she didn’t exactly have much. Part of her was waiting for Lena Luthor to actually climb the stairs of her fire escape and… she wasn’t sure yet. So, she stopped that train of thought and just vacantly looked at Nia.

    “Why didn’t you say anything?” She questioned, instead in a defeated voice.

    “Are you seri-? Kara, you said you were going to rat me out, about the drunken incident with Bulgaria’s Chancellor. And believe it or not, I don’t want you to be court martialed, either.” Nia, sighed. “Please, just give me something. Meet me halfway.”

    Kara massaged her forehead, the persistent dull headache not abating, and sighed as well. “Did you know that the agents in Peru were never given an autopsy?”

    “What? What agents in Peru?”

    “Yes, the DEA agents. They found them, along with Kryptonite, and just like that, their deaths were attributed to her. Same thing with the people in Iceland.” Kara stood up and walked to her room, opening the curtain and showing her board to Nia. 

    “So, what? Are you saying that she didn’t kill them?”

    Kara waited a moment before answering. “She said as much.”

    “Kara, she’s a criminal. Of course she would say she didn’t do it. You cannot be that naïve.”

    “I am not naïve. I investigated the thing myself. None of those agents received an autopsy. The people in Iceland were found under a ton of snow, there was a storm when they were sent there, I checked. I don’t think she killed them. She didn’t kill me, and she had plenty of opportunities to do so. Same with you.” She explained, pointing at the board, and giving Nia printed reports.

    “Okay, she didn’t kill anyone. But she’s still a cyber terrorist. A thief at the very least.” Nia was looking at the paperwork.

    “Maybe, but she has no motivation to. She’s a genius, she’s a billionaire, she had a fantastic job that even without her family money she could live more than comfortably. So why has she resorted to crime?”

    “Ego, probably. She will always live under the shadow of her brother. He’s also a genius, one that has contributed to the betterment of humanity, you know, a good guy.”

    Kara pursed her lips, Nia’s conclusion was sound, a logical explanation. And she had looked into Lex Luthor’s past. Spotless, of course, maybe a little too spotless, which to her is a red flag, but a hunch was not enough when the alternative, his sister, had the same opportunity, motive, means, and had, as far as confessions go, admitted to it. Not when her only motivation to find that Lena was innocent of it all was, realistically, a pretty face. Exactly as Nia had said.

    “She told me that she originally made kryptonite for Lex. She said that he was obsessed with a myth about it. I did some research. It's a tale of a man from a planet named Krypton, who was sent to Earth when his planet died. He had super-powers because of the sun, it was different from the one on his planet, his only weakness was this green substance, that were the remnants of his planet, it made him weak. That’s kryptonite.”

    “Get to the point, Kara. That has nothing to do with our target.”

    Kara nodded. She was well aware about how deranged everything seemed, how deranged she sounded.

    “It just made me curious, you know, why would her brother be obsessed with it? At first, I thought he identified with the alien. But it made no sense, why obsess over the thing that made him weak, right? Then, I realized that he probably identifies with someone who gets power with the kryptonite.” Nia kept looking at her, unimpressed. “Sebastian Wilde. He’s the archnemesis of the alien. He’s a genius, he’s a millionaire, he’s seen as the villain, but he thinks he is right, that humanity’s saviour should be human not alien.”

    “This still says nothing of value to us about Lena. Kara, look at yourself.” Nia was clearly done with her shit. “You don’t eat. Don’t sleep, and now you’re talking about myths and magical powers. We need to catch her, or you will be accused of treason and court martialed, and you’ll drag me with you. There is no other option. Please, Kara, I’m begging you, tell me you have something that can help us catch her.”

    “She said she got her heart broken two years ago,” Kara mumbled after a long silence, biting her bottom lip. “That her ex-girlfriend ended things because it was easier to love someone else. I assume that the break up was because she turned to crime.” She crossed to her board, and pointed at a picture. “I think the girlfriend was Andrea Rojas. Obsidian North’s CEO. She also went to Mount Helena’s.” She took another deep breath. “They were friends since childhood, then had some sort of fall out, then they befriended each other again, I think this is actually the start of their relationship. That was around the time that Lena started working remotely for LuthorCorp, she reported to the R&D department, she was barely 20 years old, still at college.”

    “Why do you think is her?” Nia asked. Part of Kara was proud at her questioning, it was a valid concern, especially with how erratic she had been acting.

    “Well, there were rumours at the time that they were together. You know, gossip rags. They were seen vacationing in Athens, Costa Rica, Paris, St. Tropez, and Buenos Aires, where Andrea’s originally from. Lena mentioned how her ex moved on quite quickly after their break up, and a few months after Lena committed her first crime, Andrea started dating Russell Rogers; they're now engaged.”

    “Okay, that’s something. Maybe Andrea knows about where she could be hiding. We can go visit her tomorrow. Obsidian North has its headquarters here in National City. Anything else?” Nia pondered.

    “She mentioned she has a best friend, who she misses. She said her friend has a daughter. I cross referenced everything we knew about her. And I think she was talking about Samantha Arias. She works at LuthorCorp, in finances. She has a 16 years old daughter, Ruby Arias. I don’t think they’re actually in contact, but I do think she would protect them. If this thing extends long enough I think that if we plan a sting operation, we can make Brainiac and Lena think that Miss Arias is in danger, she would come out. But that plan has so many variables, it would be dangerous. It would be my absolute last resort.”

    Nia was silent for a few moments, looking at her board, then at Kara.

    “Can I ask you something?”

    “I think you just did?” Kara smiled, feeling tired. Then again, she couldn’t even remember the last time she slept more than four hours. “What do you wanna know?”

    “Why are you so reluctant to catch her? This could be the biggest catch of your career. And Kelly told me you guys spent months coming up with a profile when she first disappeared, after her first wave of crimes. What's changed?”

    Kara pondered about it for a second. 

    “I met her.” She simply says.

    “Just like that?”

    “I don’t know. I felt like I connected with her. Not even when she came here, but at the restaurant, in the cellar. I didn’t lie.” She quickly adds. “She was aiming her gun at my head, and so was I, but she was on a date, she was pissed that we had ruined her night out. It made me laugh, for some reason.”

    Kara sat down on her bed, raking her hand through her hair, pensive.

    “And then she came here and— well, you met her. She's charming, she cares about important stuff, we didn’t talk much but I got that impression of her. I mean, what criminal has an electric car? It's an expensive car, but eco-friendly, nonetheless. We talked about being adopted. And how both of us were seeking the approval of someone that would probably never give it to us.”

    “And she’s attractive.” Nia posited.

    Kara glared at her. “I am not that blind. Yes, she is attractive. But she’s also smart. And she could have killed both of us. She could have wired this entire building with C4 if she had wanted to, that night and every night since then. She knows that at least 2 of us live here. So, why hasn’t she done anything? Do you see why I feel like I'm missing something?”

    “I get it.” The brunette nodded. “But my priority right now is not being charged with treason, not the possible innocence of a wanted criminal.” Nia put her hands on her own hips, and looks directly at her. “So, tomorrow, you’ll tell the team about Andrea Rojas. You’ll tell them you found the rumors about their romance and think it is a promising lead.  Hopefully it won't raise any suspicions about how exactly you got to that conclusion. I will try to find any digital evidence of her friendship with Samantha Arias, now that I know what I’m looking for. If we do this right, we can catch Lena Luthor without implicating ourselves. Understood?” 

    Kara wanted to smirk at the way that Nia had talked, and the pose she had adopted, because it was clearly an Alex move. But some part of her brain was disappointed that she would not see Lena Luthor out and about again.

    “Yes, ma’am.” She said instead.

     

    The next day, Kara didn’t say anything after breakfast, or after lunch, in any case. It didn't matter how many times Nia had looked at her, willing her to open her mouth with murderous looks. Finally, almost at 15:00 hours, she approached the main deck joining Alex and Kelly who were reading one of the thesis Lena had written in college, ready to talk just like Nia had told her, when J’onn entered the room.

    “You need to move out, now. Luthor was just spotted in one of Lord Technologies’ labs.”

    “What?” Kara screeched. Lord Tech? That made no sense, they had raided the place months ago. Why would Lena go there?

    “Now, Argo One.” J’onn ordered, severe. “The van is waiting for you. Kara you’re leading.”

    They moved to the unmarked black van, full of tactical gear at their complete disposal. Kara put on her bulletproof vest and holstered various weapons to her person in a daze. It didn’t make sense, it didn’t follow Lena’s modus operandi. The DEO had raided all of Lord Tech’s labs, anything that could be of value was in their possession. Had they missed something there? She had the suspicion that they had missed a lot with Lena, but Lord was a close case, right?

    “It feels like a trap.” Alex voiced, like she was able to read Kara’s mind. “There is nothing of value in any of Max’s labs, anymore.”

    “We’ll be careful. Maybe we missed something the first time.” Kara was quick to respond. “Maybe your boyfriend hid something there.” Kelly immediately guffawed.

    “I am armed,” Alex glared at her for the comment. 

    “Good. I don’t think she will be so forgiving this time around,” Kara said, looking at Nia.

    “What’s the plan?” Nia asked while quickly braiding her hair, to get it out of the way.

    “ETA 2 minutes.” The driver piped up.

    “Right. We don’t have a layout of the place, so we will have to wing it for the most part. Same teams as the last time, but we will only separate if absolutely necessary.” She pushed her glasses up her nose. She didn't have the time to put on contacts, and that could be a problem in hand to hand, but that was a problem for later. “Channel 2 for comms, in case of separation.” She said when the van stopped. “Let's go.”

    She took out her gun, and got closer to the main entrance of the lab. Nia behind her, then Kelly, then Alex. She nodded and Nia opened the door. Kara went in first. The place was completely void of people. Except, she could feel like they were being watched, the hairs at the back of her head standing, goosebumps along her neck. 

    “I don’t like this.”  Alex mumbled, her back to Kelly, looking behind them. 

    “I don’t like it either.” Kara declared, her own back to Nia. “I think we shou—” She stopped talking when in the corner of her eyes she saw a somewhat familiar figure, but when she turned around to see again, there was nothing.

    “Kara?” Kelly called, next to her.

    “Yes, I think we should start from the bottom up. Moving as quickly as possible. I don’t like it here, we’re too exposed.”

    “Agree. Let’s move on.” Alex answered, before they start to move as a unit, in very practiced steps. They went down a flight of stairs, and checked all the doors. All were locked except one, right at the end, that door was wide open. “Trap.” Alex simply commented. They were far enough from the door that no one in that room could hear them talk.

    Kara shook her head. “Doesn’t matter. We’re four, she’s only one. Brainiac may be with her, but I’m sure we can take them. We can catch her.”

    “Do you think that’s wise? There is not much room to escape, if it’s indeed a trap, we’re not even sure if she’s really alone.” Kelly remarked.

    “Kara.” Nia muttered her name, clearly in warning.

    At the same time, Alex started, “If you're gonna do the job, do the job right. There were creepy shadows upstairs, there are no bad guys anywhere, the only way is an enclosed space. Trap.”

    “It’s an order, agents.” Kara asserted, walking with her gun still aimed in front of her. Nia was right, they had to catch Lena Luthor and put all of it behind them. She entered the room without waiting for the rest of the team. Sadly, the place was completely empty. “Goddamnit.” She stomped the ground like a child, and put her gun back on her tight holster, while looking around.

    “Do you think the tip was fake?” Kelly questioned, holstering her gun as well.

    Kara caught the green glimmer on the ground, and smiled. “No. She was here alright.” She walked up to the green rocks that were under a really weird grey metallic arch. “Has the lab found anything useful about these things?” She asked, kneeling to pick up the kryptonite.

    She doesn’t hear an answer. There was a loud hissing sound, and a purple light enveloped her. Her instinct was to reach for her gun again, and aim it in front of her. She didn’t feel like she had moved, but when the purple light vanished, she wasn't with her team. She was in a completely different room. Upstairs, she realized, one of the big offices. She felt like she wanted to throw up, and her skin was prickly and a little sweaty.

    “Take a breath,” the now familiar accented voice said, and that was somewhat both a gut punch and a soothing balm to Kara. “That portal is a one time use, and it’s in the rough stage. You must be feeling a little dizzy.”

    When Kara stopped seeing black spots in front of her, she looked directly at Lena, lowering her weapon. “You can’t keep doing this.”

    “Doing what?” Lena smiled, like she was the picture of innocence.

    “Kidnapping me!”

    “I have not done such a thing. We’re still at Lord Tech. You came looking for me.” Lena kept smirking.

    Kara pursed her lips, trying not to smile. “What are you doing here?”

    “I came to apologize for the other night. I was out of line, when I tried to kiss you.”

    Kara was gobsmacked. All that trouble just to apologize? She looked at Lena, trying to figure out if she was lying, but the only conclusion she drew was that she looked soft. She was wearing blue jeans, and a green sweater that made her eyes pop more, with long sleeves that covered half her hands. She had white tennis shoes, and her hair was down, very straight and parted in the middle. Involuntarily, Kara took a step closer to her. Lena’s lack of heels made Kara realize that she was shorter than she thought the last time they saw each other. And that, for some reason, makes Kara's face break into a smile, and her chest fill with a strange lightness.

    “This is getting out of hand,” Kara said, in a whisper. “You are gonna get me in trouble. I can’t be here alone with you.”

    “Sorry.” Lena responded, it was clear in her face and voice that she wasn’t.

    “I mean, I really like you. But you're still a criminal. And I am still a government agent tasked to capture you.”

    Lena narrowed her eyes, “Right.”

    “I could get court martialed.” Kara insisted.

    “Yeah, I gathered. That's why I brought you here, so we could clear the air between us. You know, set boundaries.” Lena stated.

    Kara observed her for a few seconds. “It's really sweet of you to go to all this trouble for that. The purple thing and all. But my friends are gonna wonder where I am.”

    “No, they're not.” She quickly assured her.

    It was Kara’s turn to narrow her eyes to look at her. “Why?”

    “No reason.”

    “Lena! Oh my god.”

    “Come on. It's not like they're gonna die or anything.” The green eyed monster was clearly way more amused than she should be. And Kara is not as worried as she should.

    “Let them out.” Kara ordered her. “I'm serious.”

    “Okay. Jesus.” Lena picked up a tablet from a nearby desk and typed something in it. “Booby trap deactivated. Happy?”

    “That wasn't funny.” Kara mumbled, trying very, very hard not to smile. 

    “It was kind of funny.”

    Lena kept smiling like a Cheshire cat, and Kara felt...something. It made the lightness in her chest vanish. Guilt, she figures. She was really enjoying the cat and mouse game they were doing, but Nia was right, it was their job to capture her.

    “I have to—,” the words are: arrest you , but she’s incapable of voice them out loud, “I—I gotta go.” She said instead.

    “No, no,” Lena shook her head. “I'll go. I understand this whole thing isn't gonna work out between us because, obviously, the criminal and the cop thing.” Lena’s smile was different now. It was mellow, and disarming. “But, I just want you to know that, that night was the most alive I've felt in a while.” Her eyes were fixed on Kara’s lips for a second before going up to her eyes again. “So I guess, I'll see you around. Same Bat-time or whatever.” She turned around, with a rueful smile.

    Kara didn’t really register that she moved the two steps it took to grab Lena’s hand. Doesn’t notice the way her own hand travels along Lena’s arm and her fingers tangle into the brunette’s hair, at the nape of her neck. The next thing she actually knew is that she was kissing Lena Luthor.

    She was kissing her, like her lips were the first breath of air after being underwater for too long. Lena froze for half a second, Kara swiped her tongue on Lena’s bottom lip, seeking access, the brunette granted it immediately, responding enthusiastically to the kiss. Lena grabbed her by the front of her bulletproof vest with both her hands. The kiss was not gentle, it was hungry, filthy, full of teeth and tongue, and it’s like the restless energy she has felt the entire week, now has somewhere to go. Kara can taste coffee in Lena’s tongue, and maybe a dash of whiskey. When she felt a little lightheaded by the lack of oxygen, she pulled apart, biting Lena’s bottom lip, gently tugging at it for a second before releasing it.

    She felt breathless, and looking at the rise and fall of Lena’s chest, she wasn't the only one. Kara touched her own forehead with Lena’s, both trying to catch their breath.

    “Come with me.” the criminal said, “Come on. What do you have to lose?” She asked, looking at her eyes.

    Kara felt like she was being pulled in two different directions. Her work, her integrity, her morals, versus the crackling connection she felt with the woman in front of her. She decided to answer Lena’s question as honestly as she can. 

    “Everything.”

    Chapter End Notes

    They can prey Irish!Lena from my cold dead hands.

    Interlude: Nightmare

    Chapter Notes

    Hi, sorry for the delay on this chapter, it was a very difficult one to write. This one is very light on Kara and Lena, they're barely in it. It's mostly an Alex and Nia's chapter, and those minor Dansen, Minor Brainia tags. If that's not your cup of tea, that's totally okay. If you decide to read you get a little Sam and Andrea cameos as a treat. It was originally two chapters (one for dansen and other for brainia) but this is first and foremost a supercorp fic, so I had to start from the beginning. Anyways, enjoy.

    Something was wrong with her sister. Alex wasn’t sure if it was her break up with James, or if her encounter with Lena Luthor had affected more than she let on. It has been four days since both events, and Alex was worried. Maybe it was something else entirely that was afflicting her sister, but it reminded her of Kara’s attitude from when she was first adopted. Timid, quiet, and little, trying to not take up much space. 

    She wasn’t eating as much, or with her usual cheeriness, she spent the time moving the food around the plate and looking vacantly at the air in front of her. She didn’t sing at the top of her lungs in the car anymore. There had been no game night this week. And she knew for fact that Kara wasn’t sleeping much, it was evident by the large black spots under her eyes. So, yes, she was very worried about Kara.

    “If I have to read something else about polyatomic anions, I think my eyes are gonna fall off.” Kelly said, as she exited the bathroom, her hair up in a yellow headscarf, dressed in one of Alex’s ratty t-shirt, ready to sleep. “I don’t even understand over half of the things she was talking about in that thesis.”   

    Alex looked at her girlfriend with a tiny smile, asking her to get closer to her with a movement of her hands. “Yeah, I don’t know if we are truly the most capable people to be reading those papers. Even with my background in bioengineering. We’re not really that kind of doctors.” She confessed, once Kelly had climbed the bed and lied half on top of her. “But we still got a few more to get through tomorrow, so get ready for it.” Alex hugged Kelly to her chest and sighed looking distractedly at the ceiling. She was content, and yet, still preoccupied about her sister.

    “Kara, again?” Her girlfriend simply asked, after a few minutes of comfortable silence.

    “Yeah. She’s acting like she’s thirteen again, traumatized about the death of her parents. She’s closing herself off, and I’m not sure she even realizes she’s doing it.”  Alex was drawing tiny circles in the small of Kelly’s back, distractedly. It was soothing to her. “Actually, I think that's what scares me the most, the fact that she’s not aware of it.”

    Kelly hummed, nodding into her chest, “A psychologist's work truly is never-ending,” she jokes, looking at her. “It’s completely normal for you to be worried about your sister, but you have to give her a little more time to process everything. And even then, she might not tell you anything.” Her girlfriend caressed her cheek, and Alex knew she was looking for a reaction, “We know Kara doesn’t like change. And in the last three days a lot has changed. She broke up with Jimmy, something we both know she has been thinking about for months. She survived an encounter with a ruthless killer, something she’s had to relieve multiple times since then. She got a promotion she didn’t want, at her sister’s expense. Something, said sister, didn’t take so well…”

    “When you put it like that…” Alex sighed. “In my defense, it was just surprising. Kara never wanted to lead the team, or any team. At least, she never told me she'd want to.”

    “General Grant didn’t really give her much of a choice, did she?” Kelly continued caressing Alex’s jawline, really close to her mouth. It was very distracting. “And you didn’t need to get so offended by it. J’onn had literally offered you his job, less than 48 hour prior. Something you still haven’t told your sister.”

    “Because I’m worried about her. Did you see her eyes today? She’s wasting away and I don’t know why. It’s very frustrating.” 

    “I know, babe. But I’m giving you my opinion, both as Kara’s friend and as a professional. Give her a little time. She will get better, and she will tell you what you need to know once she’s ready. In the meantime, you keep trail mix around her, so she eats something. I’ll do my best to keep her hydrated, and hopefully she gets some hours of sleep soon.”

    Alex sighed again. Kelly was right, she needed to give Kara time to grieve her relationship. Nevermind her actual friendship with James, even if breaking up was her decision. She had to let Kara know that she wasn’t angry at her because she was chosen team leader, because her initial reaction really wasn’t good. And they had to catch one cyberterrorist. Then everything would be okay.

    “Thank you for calming me down.” She smiled, taking Kelly’s hand that was lovingly touching her jaw, and kissed the palm of it. “I’m very lucky to have you.”

    “Yeah, you are.” 

    Kelly smiled in that way that always makes Alex want to simultaneously melt into a puddle, and pin her against the nearest surface. Instead, she goes for a kiss, because that’s a good middle ground. She tasted like cinnamon, smelled like lavender, and felt like sunshine, and Alex couldn’t think of something better than kissing Kelly Olsen. Well, she can, but it was really late.

    “Love where this is going…” Her girlfriend mumbled into her lips, “but I’m really tired. One of my superior officers is a pain in the ass.”

    “Yeah, Kara’s the worst.” Alex joked, stretching herself to turn off the night table light without letting Kelly go.

    “You were worried about her, literally three seconds ago.”

    “Well, my amazing girlfriend told me to not worry too much.” She said, mirth lacing her words, before kissing her girlfriend’s forehead. “‘Night, babe. Love you.”

    “You’re insufferable. I love you, too. Sweet dreams.”

     

    The next day came too quickly, in Alex’s opinion, but they stuck to their routine. She woke up first, put on the coffee maker, and went to the bathroom to take a shower. When she got out, half dressed, Kelly was sleepily sipping on her coffee. She kissed her girlfriend on the temple as a good morning, and got her own cup. Once Kelly was a little more awake, she hit the shower, while Alex served up two cups of coffee to go, (Kelly’s with oat milk and sugar. Hers black with no sugar), and cleaned up the dishes.

    As everyday, she went to Kara’s apartment to make sure she was already up. Her sister slowly nodded, and kept getting ready. She went back to her own apartment, where Kelly was almost ready to go, and finished dressing up for a new day of work.

    The day went on, as the previous four days. Kara didn’t sing in the car, Nia got a few more minutes of sleep on the back. They ate breakfast together, Kara didn’t order her usual abundance, and never finished her omelet, but ordered an extra coffee. Alex kept repeating Kelly’s words to herself, and looking at her girlfriend pleadingly. She made sure to buy a few packs of trail mix in the canteen, to give to Kara through the day, for her to munch on. 

    At mid morning, Kelly was once again with a thick psychology book that had the appearance of being from 1739, on one hand and a tablet with press profiles of Lena Luthor on the other. Alex thought she looked adorable, with her little concentration wrinkle in the middle of her forehead, and a pencil between her fingers dancing over the book, ready to scribble up anything that could be useful.

    “Look at you all smitten and stuff,” Somehow Kara had approached her, and she hadn’t realized. “It’s curious how you basically spent 24 hours together and you still look at her with so much…”

    “What?” She asked, somehow defensive and insecure, about her sister’s opinion but she took the opportunity to offer Kara some trail mix. She internally cheered when her sister distractedly took the pack.

    “Longing.” the blonde finally said. “Like the day is not long enough to be with her. I’ve never seen you like this, not even with Mag—” Kara had, in that precise moment, the urge to chomp the entire pack of trail mix into her mouth. Apparently.

    “You can say her name, Kara.” Alex smiled at her sister, “It’s okay, it’s been a long time. Maggie and I wanted different things. It was something neither of us was willing to compromise on. I now realize that maybe Maggie was my first love, but not my happily ever after.” She commented, her gaze still fixated on Kelly.

    “I think she’s really good to you, for you. Just like you’re good for her.” Kara remarked, once she was finally able to swallow the dried fruits and nuts. “And I think you make a better couple, actually. She compliments you in a way Maggie was never capable of, you were both great in the field when we had the opportunity to work with her, and she was fun, but behind closed doors you were too alike.” Alex could feel her sister's eyes trained on her. And she felt a sudden nervousness roving through her. “Too stubborn, too set in your own ways, and I know it was hard at the time, but you did the right thing. If you didn’t have kids because of her, you would’ve resented her, and if you did have kids, she would have resented you. That decision brought you to Kelly. Who’s kind, brilliant, and clearly makes you so, so happy. Someone you can actually disagree with, without it escalating into full on fights, and she sees your perspective and you see hers.”

    “Is there something you want to actually say?” She questioned, suspicious.

    “Well, not really.” Kara shrugged. Alex could feel Nia skulking around the both of them. “I’m really just trying to have a conversation with you. I meant every word I said, but I’m honestly just trying to see if you’re still mad at me.”

    She was gonna answer, when Nia cleared her throat behind them.

    “Is there a problem, Agent Nal?” Alex asked.

    “Not really, boss. It’s just, I’m not exactly sure of what else to do. We’ve researched everything.” Nia was looking directly at Kara, with a really weird intensity, then she remembered that Kara was their immediate superior officer now. 

    Kara’s lips were thin and white when she looked at Nia, and they really had a really weird vibe today.

    “Look into LuthorCorp’s public records of anything that she was doing prior to her first hacking, maybe we missed something. Look if she was working constantly with other people, maybe she’s still in contact with someone.” Kara ordered in a monotone voice. She and Nia have some sort of silent conversation, only looking at each other, until Nia simply turned around and left to do as Kara had ordered.

    “Okay, what the hell was that?” She wondered out loud, trying to cut a laugh that had climbed up her throat.

    Kara shrugged, “I think she’s frustrated.”

    “Yeah, I understand the feeling” Alex touched her neck, a little uncomfortable, “In any case I wanted to apologize for the other day.” Predictably, Kara’s adorable crinkle made its appearance. “I didn’t react very well when you got promoted. It wasn’t because of your capability to lead, or even mine,” She kept talking, not letting Kara interrupt, “I’m just used to it, and you have never given me any indication of wanting to lead a team, it just took me by surprise. And it doesn’t even matter because, um, J’onn tells me he’s retiring soon and he—he offered his job to me.”

    “Alex! That’s so great!” Kara's smile was blinding, jokingly punching her shoulder. “You’ll be fantastic in that job.”

    “I— Thank you.” It’s all Alex was capable of saying, for some reason. Kara opened her mouth like she wanted to say something, but decided not to at the last second, and instead smiled looking in Kelly’s direction again. 

    “So, no more working with Kelly, then?”

    “They say that with absence the heart grows fonder, or something like that,” Alex shrugged. “She’s applying for another job, actually… We have plans, and they— it would be easier if either of us is not in constant danger.”

    Kara looked like she was calculating a very complex math equation for a few seconds, until it clicks, “Oh! Already?”

    “Well, no, not immediately, of course. But we’re not getting any younger.”

    “Of course, of course. Cool, cool, cool, yeah, so cool.”

    “Are you okay?” Alex asked, frowning at Kara’s response.

    “Yeah! I’m gonna hit the gym. See you for lunch.” She spoke while she was walking backwards to the door. Alex wasn’t exactly sure of what had happened, but at least Kara was talking about lunch, and she had eaten the dried fruit, so she took it all as a win.

     

    Kara entered the canteen, almost at 13:00 hours, way later than usual and when the rest of the team was almost done. With the ends of her hair, still a little wet from the shower, she looked a little pale, and was still aloof while eating the burger she ordered. Alex was still very worried, and decided to let her act all weird until the end of the week, then she would intervene. They got back to their deck, seemingly in good mood, Nia furiously typing on her computer, Kelly went back to her books, and Alex went back to another Lena Luthor thesis, they were hitting wall after wall. Kara didn’t stand in place for more than 5 minutes at a time, her restless energy overtaking her, she kept pacing with a tablet in her hand. Alex had to bite her tongue to not lash at her, and tell her to stay put.

     

    It was close to 15:00 hours, when apparently her sister was ready to say something. She took a breath and approached Kelly and herself. Then J’onn showed up.

    “You need to move out, now. Luthor was just spotted in one of Lord Technologies’ labs.”

    “What?” Kara confusedly asked.

    “Now, Argo One.” J’onn ordered. “The van is waiting for you. Kara you’re leading.”

    The tactical gear was ready for them when they got in the black van, it was a very unusual place for Lena Luthor to go when Lord Tech Labs had been raided a few months earlier by the DEO, after their tortuous sting operation. 

    “It feels like a trap.” She said. “There is nothing of value in any of Max’s labs.”

    “We’ll be careful. Maybe we missed something the first time. Maybe your boyfriend hid something there.” Kara mocked her.

    Kelly let out a cackle next to her.

    “I am armed.” Alex glared at her sister and then looked at her girlfriend like she had betrayed her.

    “Good. I don’t think she will be so forgiving this time around.” Kara let out, still smiling.

    “What’s the plan?” Nia questioned distractedly, while braiding her hair. 

    “ETA 2 minutes.” The driver announced.

    “Right.” Kara sobered up “We don’t have a layout of the place, so we will have to wing it for the most part. Same teams as the last time, but we will only separate if absolutely necessary. Channel 2 for comms, in case of separation. Let's go.”

    They got out the van in formation, they moved in perfectly coordinated steps, every angle covered by any of the four. Once inside the lab, the place was solitary, no one in sight, and yet she felt like they were being observed. Ominous shadows that came from seemingly nowhere.

    “I don’t like this.”  She mumbled, her back to Kelly, protective.

    “I don’t like it either.” Kara concedes. “I think we shou—” She interrupts herself, leaving her words unfinished, without reason.

    “Kara?” Kelly prompted.

    “Yes, I think we should start from the bottom up. Moving as quickly as possible. I don’t like it here, we’re too exposed.”

    “Agree. Let’s move on.” She agreed, before they start to move again. They went down a flight of stairs. All the doors were locked except for one, right at the end. “Trap.” Alex simply commented.

    Kara shook her head. “Doesn’t matter. We’re four, she’s only one. Brainiac may be with her, but I’m sure we can take them. We can catch her.”

    “Do you think that’s wise? There is not much room to escape, if it’s indeed a trap, we’re not even sure if she’s really alone.” Kelly, always the voice of reason, said.

    “If you're gonna do the job, do the job right. There were creepy shadows upstairs, there are no bad guys anywhere, the only way is an enclosed space. Trap.” Alex exploded, hissing like a high pressure valve.

    “Kara.” Nia muttered like some sort of warning.

    “It’s an order, agents.” Kara ordered and, stupidly, started walking with her gun still aimed at nothing. 

    Luckily the place was empty. “Goddamnit.” Kara stomped her feet like a two-years-old child, being denied candy, and then holstering her gun.

    “Do you think the tip was fake?” Kelly asked.

    “No. She was here alright. Has the lab found anything useful about these things?” Kara walked under a metallic arch and picked up some Kryptonite rocks. 

    “Not re—” The answer died in her throat when the gray metallic arch Kara was under came to life with a whirring sound and a purple light consumed her. Her heart dropped out of her ribcage, “KARA!” She screamed at the same time as both Nia and Kelly, moving to try and catch her sister, but it was too late. When she got close enough to the apparatus, whatever the hell that was, it was like Kara had never been there. “Kara.” She calls on her earpiece, and only gets silence. “Kara, please, do you copy?”

    No answer. She looked around the arch, looking for any control panel, or mechanism, or whatever to undo whatever it had done. “Come on, Kara…” She could feel both Kelly and Nia doing something similar, for a few seconds, until Kelly tried to get out of the room. The door closed with force, and immediately green smoke started to fill the place.

    “Fuck!” her girlfriend said. 

    Nia was apparently so dumbfounded she let out a strangled laugh, before covering her nose and mouth with the crook of her elbow. Kelly and Alex did the same. Alex got closer to the door and tried to get it open with her free hand, using her leg as leverage. It wouldn’t budge.

    “Babe, don’t tire yourself, we don’t know how much oxygen we have left,” Kelly said in confidence. She was right, of course, but Alex was also desperate. 

    Desperate because she didn’t know where Kara was. If Kara was okay, if Kara was hurt, what had that machine done? What does the purple light do? All these questions were crowding her head, while she kept trying to open that damn, really heavy, door. And the green gas was steadily filling up the room. She felt drowsy but she kept trying to open the fucking door, for who knows how long.

    “Babe… stop.” At some point, Kelly had sat down with her back to the wall, close to the door. Or maybe she slided down because she also looked and sounded sleepy. Alex turned around to look for Nia, she found her slumped next to a high metal table, also close to the door. Now Alex was overcome with worry, not only for Kara, but the rest of them, as well. Whatever that green gas was, it was at the bare minimum, to put them to sleep. And with Lena Luthor as the suspected mastermind behind it, they could never wake up. Now it was imperative that she opened the door, she unholstered her gun and aimed at the only point of entry.

    “Wait…” The lethargic voice of Nia stopped her. “That door is over two inches of steel. The bullet would rebound and kill you. Or us.”

    “If we don’t get out of here we will die anyways.”

    “You don’t know that.” It was Kelly who spoke. “It could be a simple sedative. If you fire that gun, and it ricochets into any of us, we would bleed to death, because we would be too sleepy to put any pressure on it. You’re the doctor, am I wrong?” Kelly’s voice was a little raspy, and she spoke very slowly, just like when she was falling asleep at home.

    “No. You… you are right.” Alex didn’t holster the gun again, but she sat next to her girlfriend. All three of them had abandoned the illusion of trying to not breathe in the gas. It was everywhere, giving the place a faux neon green aspect, it didn’t smell, but she was unaware if the room was still being pumped of it. “What do you think it was the purple thing?” She asked her girlfriend, Nia had already passed out on the table.

    “Remember the polyatomic anions? And the quantum entanglement theory?” Kelly replied, with her groggy voice, “That was the basis for a transmatter portal. Her idea was to use it for humanitarian efforts…”

    “So, I guess she finished it.” Alex says before looking at her girlfriend, she had passed out too. She grabbed Kelly’s hand with her free one and interlocked their fingers. “I really hope you’re right and this is just a dumb knockout gas, because I really would hate not marrying you, Kelly Olsen.” She rasped, just to immediately lose consciousness.

     

    She awoke an undetermined time later. “Babe,” she drowsily called Kelly. “Come on, wake up, so you can say how right you were...”

    “I was so right,” Kelly said, both sleepy and mocking. “We have to find Kara.” And in that moment Alex knew, (or more like confirmed, again) that Kelly Olsen was the one. 

    “That we do.” She pushed herself to stand up, shaking her head to wake up. She helped her girlfriend to her feet, and then walked up to Nia, “Hey, sleepy head, time to wake up.” Alex called, shaking up the kid.

    “‘m up, ‘m up.”

    “Sure you are, kid. Come on, we have to find Kara.”

    The door was easy to open, this time. Whatever force was keeping it shut was gone. Alex tried to call Kara in comms, but it was met with silence. Again. They got out of the room, in wedge formation, Alex in front, guns drawn. They were going blind, no idea where Kara could be, the best was to do an inverse sweep of how they got to the room in the first place.

    The lab floor was completely empty, except for them, and the more they searched for Kara and didn’t find her, the more antsy Alex became. The sweep of the floor where they passed out was clear, and so was the entry floor. There was only one floor left, and she was losing hope in finding her sister there. 

    She opened the door of what she knew was Maxwell Lord’s office back when he was the owner of the lab, and her heart dropped out of her body when she saw an arch identical to the one two floors down. On Max’s desk it was Kara’s necktie, surrounded by kryptonite, next to a handwritten note, that simply said:

     

    'I’ve got the girl'

     

    Kelly immediately got her phone out, she heard Nia say something, but in all honesty, Alex felt like she was under water. She had lost her little sister, the one she had vowed to protect since teenagehood. They didn’t get along at first, but once Alex got over herself and her resentment over having a sister she didn’t want, and saw a girl who had lost everything in one swoop, she allowed herself to love Kara wholeheartedly. She was her best friend, and she couldn’t protect her. Because Lena Luthor may have spared Kara once, but she was sure that they wouldn’t get as lucky twice.

    Alex had to use Max’s desk to support herself, when her knees were about to fail her.

    “I lost her, Kelly.” She heard herself say to her girlfriend, who immediately got closer to her, stopping whatever the hell she was doing. Kelly hugged her.

    “Alex, listen to me. We are going to find Kara. We will have the whole DEO searching for her. We will get your sister back. Okay?”

    She didn’t believe it, but she would try, if this were happening to her, Kara would try. “Okay”

     

     

    As soon as they got to the office that had a second metallic arch, Nia knew that she would not like whatever they would find there. Once she saw the green kryptonite, the tie and the note, she knew they weren’t looking for someone who had been taken against her will.

    “You’ve got to be kidding me…” She mumbled to herself. Okay, she was like 90% sure Kara had gone willingly, and she wanted to laugh about it, because how is she supposed to explain that, without condemning Kara to be court martialed? Or implicate herself? There was no way. She looked at Kelly calling J’onn at his personal line. Quickly explaining how the mission had gone awry, how they lost Kara. They had not much of a timeline because they had no idea for how long they were passed out, and honestly, she didn’t even remember at what time they were dropped at the lab. 

    Nia still felt a little groggy, whatever that green smoke was, she was sure she would be still sleeping if it weren't for Alex waking her up. Twenty minutes later, J'onn arrived with Winn and Doctor Hamilton in tow, plus 10 other agents. Winn went directly to the gray arch to try and figure out what it was, (Nia was 99% sure that even if he could figure out what it was and the theory on how it worked, that thing was forever toasted.  Lena Luthor was too much of a genius to leave her functional tech around). J'onn was taking their statements about what exactly had transpired, while Dr. Hamilton was making sure they were actually okay and not slowly dying without them noticing. 

    "I will need to do some blood work, but everything seems fine." The Doctor said to J'onn, then looked at the team. "If you feel nausea, or headaches, please contact me immediately."

    Kelly and herself mumbled something akin to acceptance. Alex still looked lost, like the proverbial rug had been pulled from under her. She had no idea her sister was probably just flirting away with the enemy.

    "Let's get you back to headquarters and then home. You all need to rest." J'onn said, with clear concern in his voice.

    "Rest?" That pulled Alex out of her numbness, "We need to start a search squad. She can't be that far. Kara is smart, there has to be something here. A—A, I don't know a trail of crumbs, for us to follow. A lead, something."

    "Alex…" J'onn tried to calm her.

    "No! I have to do something. You can't ask me to sit this one out. That's my little sister."

    "I'm not asking you to, but you got injured. You all need to recover. It's protocol, Alex you know this. And the less you fight me on this, the quicker you can join the rescue efforts." 

    Alex shuts up after that (Not that she stops grumbling and assassinating anyone who comes near her with her eyes, but is progress), Kelly’s hand interlocked with hers trying to placate the redhead.

    They went back to headquarters in a black van, identical to the one that dropped them, and they went directly to the med bay. The place was in complete chaos. Agents moving around. The big screens played a video of Cat Grant, addressing the situation, informing of Kara’s abduction, giving carte blanche to search for her, whatever means necessary. In the med bay, Dr. Hamilton took their blood samples and did a more complete check up, before letting them go with instructions to eat and hidratate. J’onn took Kara’s car keys and personally drove them home. No one said a word. The tension could be cut with a very dull knife. 

    “Hey, Nia. You should stay with us.” Kelly said once they were in the lobby of their building.

    “What?”

    “If anything happens to you, or you don’t feel okay, you would be alone in your apartment, it’s dangerous. Stay with us.”

    Nia was dumbfounded, Kelly was looking at her expectant, and Alex simply kept walking forward to the elevator. 

    “We’re staying at Kara’s” She said, not an invitation, an order.

    Nia nodded. Then a new voice called for Kelly, at the same time that Nia’s phone vibrated in her pocket. James had arrived, hugged his sister and nodded to his sister-in-law, telling how Winn had called him to tell him what had happened.

    Nia checked her phone, it was from an unknown number. But she had a mild suspicion that was confirmed not three seconds later, with a second message.

     

    Unknown number [06:12 p.m]: She is safe.

    Unknown number [06:12 p.m]: This is Brainy, by the way.

    [06:13 p.m]: Could you please tell her to call me?

     

    She answered immediately, and saved the number. The four of them climbed out of the elevator, and went in complete silence to Kara's apartment. Kelly went in search of food and tea in the kitchen. Alex plopped herself into the old sofa. Nia and James awkwardly sat in the individual chairs in front of her. 

    They kept quiet until Kelly came with tea and cookies, which she was sure were just the absolute last resort on the extremely long list of Kara's snacks. 

    "Thank you, Kelly. Feeling okay?" She asked, if only to break the silence.

    "A light headache, but I'm okay." Kelly smiled, as kind as ever, softly caressing her girlfriends back in a soothing manner.

    Suddenly, Alex remembers how to move and snaps at James. "You have gall coming here after what you told Kara." 

    "I was angry. That doesn't mean I want her in danger," James threw back at her.

    Nia's cell vibrates again. That caught Alex's attention.

    "Is it J'onn?"

     

    Brainy [06:21 p.m]: If I see her, I shall communicate your message. She is… otherwise occupied, at the moment.  

     

    Nia has to stifle a laugh because WOW. Kara really didn't waste any time. "No," she easily answered Alex, but at the curious look in Kelly's eyes she added, "I had a date tonight. I'm telling them I can't make it." The lie slipped easily from her lips.

    "I'm sorry, Nia. That sucks," Kelly mumbled, sympathetically.

    "It's okay. Maybe it's for the better, more time to know them better without the pressure of the first face to face," She smiled at Kelly, and finally replied to the text.

     

    [06:24 p.m]: Are you serious? It's barely been what? Two hours?

    Brainy [06:25 p.m]: Positive. They arrived at our meetpoint two hours, thirteen minutes ago, yes. 

    Brainy [06:26 p.m]: Not that I am counting the minutes. I am just very good at numbers. They are my thing, as they say.

    Brainy [06:27 p.m]: In any case, I have not seen them since.

    [06:28 p.m]: I'm aware that you're good with numbers and code, Querl. 

    I know a lot about you now too.

     

    Conversation flowed easily with Brainy, she wasn’t actually that much sure if he was flirting, because his writing was very formal, no contractions, and with words like affirmative, and statistically, peppered in so casually in conversation. Then she remembered he was exactly the same face to face. 

    At some point, she vaguely heard Kelly sending James home, because apparently he’s antagonizing Alex with his mere presence. Kelly returned to sit next to her girlfriend and tried to calm her undercurrent rage and impotence with soothing words and caresses'.

    Nia smiled, still chatting with Brainy and learning a lot about him. How he’s not really on good terms with his family, even before going underground with Lena, how he considers Lena his family. How he prefers tea over coffee, and that he solves the crosswords everyday. How he copes with food when stressed. In exchange, Nia lets him know about losing her mother recently, and how her mother leaving her a family heirloom and a substantial inheritance, has put a wedge in the relationship with her sister Maeve. She doesn’t mention work, but does mention Alex, Kara and Kelly, they’re her best friends after all.

     

    She doesn’t know when she actually fell asleep, in that really uncomfortable chair, but she wakes up hearing Alex’s loud voice, and Kelly trying to calm her down, again. At least it was morning already.

    “She has a fucking detective board, Kelly. It has to be this, this is why Lena Luthor took her.”

    Nia cringed before standing up, trying to alleviate her sore body.

    “Maybe. But getting frustrated over it doesn’t benefit us in finding your sister.” Kelly tried to calm Alex, again. “You know Kara better than anyone, why didn’t she say anything about her investigation?”

    Nia held her breath, waiting for Alex’s response.

    “It’s her first case, being the leader. It’s a lot of pressure. Especially if she had Grant breathing down her neck, and time against us.”

    “There you go. Now, relax. Let’s study this, figure out what Kara found out, and go from there. If this was the reason for her abduction, then we have somewhere to start.” Kelly was in front of her girlfriend, holding her neck to keep her eyes on hers. It felt like a very intimate moment so Nia cleared her throat to announce her presence.

    “That sounds like a plan.” She said, smiling. “Is there coffee? If not, I can go and find some in my apartment.”

    Alex nodded and smiled at her, taking a step back from her girlfriend. "I made a pot. But for someone who eats the way Kara does, she has no food here"

    "But we can order something." Kelly quickly added.

    Nia goes for coffee, Kelly ordered food, and Alex contemplated her sister's thought process. Nia does her best to subtly guide her in the right direction, without giving away that both Kara and herself had actually met and talked to their target.

    Truth, Lies and Consequences

    Chapter Notes

    So sorry this one took so much time. Writer's block was a thing.
    If you see any mistake, please let me know. Enjoy.

    Kara stepped out of the swirling purple portal, with a knot on her throat. She felt like walking to her own execution. Which was not entirely false, It was the end of her career. It could very well be the end of her life as a free citizen, too. 

    It took her a moment to figure out that she was a little over a block from her apartment. Definitely closer to it than 10 seconds ago, but not as close as Lena intended, she was sure. Kara walked the block to her building in a few minutes, her mind still a little foggy, still thinking on what exactly was she going to say to her team, to the agency, to her sister.     She stood in front of her own door, only to remember that her keys were still on her locker back at the DEO. By instinct, (childish instinct, but still) she twisted the doorknob, anyway and to her surprise, the door opened. Just enough for her to hear the voice of someone who she never, in a million years, thought would be at her home.

    "Are you kidding me?" For someone her height, Cat Grant was terrifying when she approached one with murder in her eyes. "We conduct a nationwide manhunt for you, and you're boning the suspect?"
