Why would she leave everything behind, just to rob a few companies, upload their blueprint on the dark web and let everyone know it was her? How are all these companies connected? And why was the government so hellbent in catching her when there are literally hundreds of way more dangerous and ruthless criminals? Had no one, but her, figured out that Lena didn't kill anyone? Not even those agents that several government agencies accused her of killing? And then, there was the way Lena referred to what she was doing, as a job, something important. Where did Brainy fit in all that? Everything she knew about him, he was a model citizen. And he believed wholeheartedly in what they were doing. Which was… What, exactly?

    "Kara?" Kelly's voice brought her back to reality, for the way her sister-in-law was looking at her, it wasn't the first time she'd called her name.

    "Sorry, I was thinking about dinner. What's up?" 

    Kelly searched something on her face, Kara's not sure what or if she found it at the end, but Kelly continued talking.

    "We're going dress shopping for endgame, tomorrow. Unless you have something to wear?"

    Kara bit her bottom lip, last time she went shopping it was with Lena, their first day together. And they tried all kinds of clothes, including formal wear, even when both of them knew they could never use something like that together.

    Kara shook her head, trying to dispel the memories, and focus. "Um, sure. Just you and me, I assume? Sorry you have been assigned as my babysitter." She whispered, giving Kelly a tiny smile.

    Kelly, once again, simply looked at her for a few seconds, then sighed. "They're pissed at you, Kara." She said softly. "Alex was distraught, for almost two weeks thinking that you were in mortal danger. She didn't eat, or sleep, she almost shot an innocent woman. I had to sedate her a few times so she could rest." Kelly then looked at the papers Kara had been glossing over, (an old case, since she's been completely grounded) Kara followed Kelly's glance and was surprised to see that she had absentmindedly sketched Lena's face on a corner of the paper. She covered the sketch with her hand, for some reason, even if she was sure Kelly had already seen it.

    Thankfully, her sister-in-law ignored it like a champ. At least for now, and continued, "Nia is pissed that you made her an accomplice, and as a result she has lost the team’s trust, and James… Well Jimmy got his ego bruised, I suppose, but he was worried about you too." 

    Kara tried to swallow the knot stuck on her throat, it was easier to ignore it all when she didn't have to face them all, and their disappointed expressions.

    "What about you?" She asked in a murmur. Kelly kept watching her face, and Kara's intelligent enough to know that she was assessing her. 

    "I'm pissed too. You almost broke my girlfriend in a way I'm not sure I could have ever put her back together." Kelly said, almost casually, her voice as kind as ever. "You broke my brother's heart. You betrayed Nia, Alex and me, this entire agency, to play house with a criminal. So yes, I'm pretty fucking pissed too." Kara felt like she needed to look away. Kelly barreled through, anyways. "But I was also worried about you, I still am. I'm not sure if you have any idea what you got yourself into. I'm still trying to figure out what made you take the decisions you made."

    How could she even begin to explain that one? She had an answer, of course. But it was not logical, or one that would be easily accepted. She kept quiet instead, only nodded, and tried to go back to the original topic.

    "I don't have anything to wear. I think tomorrow is a good day for shopping."

    "I bet it is, KD, I bet it is."

     

     

    Dress shopping was not an enjoyable experience when your friend is clearly not happy with you, but is trying to not be a dick about it. Kelly was polite, and it's clear that she was trying to be neutral about the whole thing, but Kara knew her well enough to know that she'd rather be anywhere else than babysitting her.

    "I'm sure I can find something in my closet to wear." She said after almost two hours of going in circles, she really had no idea what to wear to this gala, something that didn't translate to, 'I'm the guest of honor only to keep appearances because otherwise I would be in Siberia for treason'.  

    "Kara, you already told me you had nothing to wear, come on." Kelly kept looking at the different racks with formalwear, all different types of dresses, puffy and colorful with glitter and sequin, and black and austere, with gold details. She had already gone to the dressing room several times, and hated every single dress she'd put on.

    "Does Alex know what she'll wear? She always helps me with this type of thing, I'm terrible at it." 

    Kelly sighed, even if a smile was dancing on her lips, at the mention of Alex.

    "Yes, she… picked it up yesterday, she'll wear a suit. She told me to pick my dress and find her a pocket square and tie to match." 

    "Of course she is." Kara turned to look at the variety of dresses in front of her, once again. Kelly had already picked a beautiful yellow dress that fitted her like an elegant glove. "I wish I could wear a suit. But I don't think James would appreciate it." The last part she said more to herself, not wanting to offend Kelly.

    She heard her, anyway.

    "Yeah, it's just male ego. They feel emasculated with a woman in a suit. But given the ordeal, James feeling like that, is somehow, the least of your problems." Kelly took a second, then turned to another wall of dresses. Kara followed her like a lost child. "But maybe we can give you a jumpsuit."

    "Isn't that basically the same thing?" 

    "Not to the straight male identifying people in the crowd. It's less intimidating to them." Kelly smiled, this time sincere. "Alex told me to remind you that blue is your color because it brings out your eyes. Do you like anything here?" She pointed to the new wall of dresses, and Kara smiled, thinking that maybe her sister won't be mad at her forever.

     



    Endgame was just a couple of days away. The team was still giving her the cold shoulder, and Major Grant had given them minor missions, (to test her loyalty, she supposed). They were surveilling a man named Josh Vartox, a black-market weapon seller, when James dropped from the ceiling next to her, both of them dangling from tactical rope, Kara could see her sister stand to attention from the other side of the building. She turned to James, instead.

    "Is this your thing now? Show up unannounced in the middle of my OPs?"

    "Maybe," He shrugged his shoulders, and then gave her that boyish smile that had made her knees turn to jelly, once upon a time. "So, are we going to Endgame together?" 

    It was a stupid question, and they both know she will. They are going together. It's not like she had any other option.

    "Yeah. I got my dress ready. It's blue." Kara smiled, just because it was something she should do. "You know, If you want us to match."

    James smiled again, and to Kara it felt bittersweet because it's a charming smile, but she couldn't help but think that his smile is not the one she wanted to see. And it's not him who she wanted to go to the stupid ball with. 

    "Yeah, I can match with your blue dress." He said, not watching their target at all. Kara tried to look down at Vartox, but James started talking again, this time even lower. "I still love you, and I know that deep down, you still love me too."

    She felt like screaming. Yes, she loved him, but not like that, she never loved him like that.

    Her brain kinda stops at the thought. It wasn't a realization, but acceptance. She never loved him like a partner, as much as she wanted to, but she didn't want to lose him as a friend either, so she kept dating him, even when she knew that their relationship was going nowhere. 

    “I got you something,” he said, unaware of her realization, searching for something in the pocket of his tactical vest. It’s his father's bracelet, again. For a second, while she extended her hand so he could put it on her wrist, Kara asks herself why was he so insistent on her to wear something that wasn’t even his to begin with? Then she froze. She had given her necklace to Lena, the only thing she had of her mother, and she didn’t take it back when she returned home. She hadn’t even missed it, as if it was right where it belonged. The thought was chilling, giving something so personal, so meaningful to someone who had been in her life for barely a month, someone who no one in her life approved of. 

    "See you for Endgame, babe." She heard James say, she nodded absentmindedly as he returned to his original post, closer to the ceiling than her. 

    She didn’t even remember that she didn’t have her mother’s necklace until this very moment. Was it that the necklace didn’t mean as much anymore? Or was that Lena meant so much to her already?

    “Are you okay there, KD?” Kelly's voice cracked on her ear piece.

    “Yeah, he—he was just confirming that we were going together to Endgame?” 

    “You sure?” Alex’s voice was the one cracking on her ear this time. It was the first time she had talked to Kara for something that wasn’t about work, and Kara’s thoughts about Lena faltered. Was Lena worth losing Alex?

    “Yeah.” She said softly. “He gave me his dad's bracelet again.”

    "I still don't know why he's so attached to that old trinket." Kelly commented in a whisper. "Dad didn't even like it that much."

    Kara was going to say something when Nia's voice crackled on her earpiece.

    "We got movement."

    Things were all work from then on. Vartox made a sale, Argo One took pictures of the exchange and then raided the place. James was nowhere to be found, neither was anyone from Homeland Security. Arrests were made, evidence was collected and then they went back to headquarters.

    She was storing her equipment when Alex stood next to her. 

    "You did the right thing accepting James' wristband." She said in a murmur. “Once we’re past this test Major Grant is obviously putting us through, we should be back to normal.”

    Kara tried her best not to scoff at that, she doesn’t even know what normal is, anymore.

    “Did you write your speech?”

    “What speech?” Kara asked, confused.

    “I knew you wouldn’t remember it. Kara, you’ll be DEO Agent of the Year, you need to give an acceptance speech.” Alex said, with a little tease on her voice. It almost felt like when they were teens, but her sister’s voice was still a little strained.

    “I don’t know if I can do that, Alex.”

    “Come on. You have a degree in journalism, you’re good with words.”

    Kara’s shoulders were tense, and she didn't know how to relax with this conversation. There is so much at stake, her entire career, her relationship with her sister, her friends. Even her freedom and yet… 

    “I don’t like to lie, Alex.”

    “You might be in the wrong business, then.” 

     

    Alex promised to write something simple for her, because she has the tendency to ramble when she’s nervous. And she knows she would be nervous speaking in public.

     

     

    When she got home that night, there was… something different than when she left in the morning. She wasn't quite sure of what it was, but she noticed it immediately after opening the door. She left her bag on the floor, her eyes examining her apartment, everything looked the same, but there was something… in the air maybe. She tried to dispel the feeling while walking to her room, and then stopped in her tracks when she saw her. In complete peace, sitting at the foot of her bed, reading the few files she still had of her in her apartment.

    “Lena,” the name escaped her lips as a whisper, as a prayer. 

    “If I didn’t know any better, all these files and the investigation board would be considered a little stalker-y. Don’t you think?” Lena commented, as if going into the apartment of someone whose sole job was to arrest you, was a completely normal occurrence.

    “How did you get in?” She asked instead. Lena smiled and Kara felt her knees going a little weak, because she missed her, and Lena was beautiful. 

    “For being the home of the agent of a big government intelligence agency, your security is shit. Plus, I’m a genius, you know this.” She answered, leaving the files on the bed, next to her.

    “You shouldn’t be here.” Kara forced herself to say. Lena kept a serene smile, without moving from the bed, and Kara had to fight the urge to kiss her and send everything else to hell. Lena was quiet for a second or six, and then stood up.

    “I know.” She said, walking towards her. Kara took a step back, when Lena saw her, she stopped moving towards her.  “I just wanted to give this back to you.” She whispered, taking something out of her pocket, then opened her hand to show her, her mother’s necklace. “I reckon it's important to you. Sorry it took me a few days to bring it back. I had to make sure that the DEO wasn’t on my tail.”

    Kara moved her hand slowly, to retrieve the necklace. Lena didn't move, only letting the necklace fall into Kara's hand.

    "It was my mothers." Kara mumbled, and the silence is both charged and serene.

    "Why did you make me wear it?" Lena pressed, a few seconds later, in the same low voice and Kara didn't have an answer. She didn't know why it was important at the time, to her that Lena wore the thing for the painting. But it didn't really matter that she didn't answer, because Alex opened her door, her hand hovering over the gun still strapped to her leg holster.

    "You didn't reactivate the alarm." Her sister chastised, entering her home, right before registering Lena's presence. Her gun was drawn immediately, and trained on Lena.

    "She was here already," Kara defended herself, like a child folding over. Lena on her part, kept being as cool as a cucumber, didn't even raise her hands in surrender at the gun pointed at her.

    "Hello, Alex. Nice to see you again." She greeted Alex, instead.

    "Alex—" Kara tried to intervene. But her sister barreled through.

    "Lena Luthor, you're under arrest."

    "Am I?" She questioned, smirking.

    "You have the right to remain silent. Anything you say can be used against you in court. You have—"

    "Alex—" Kara wasn't even sure what she was going to say, but Alex just shut her up with a side look, still talking, maybe even a little louder.

    "The right to talk to a lawyer for advice before we ask you any questi—"

    It was extremely quick, the way Lena touched her wristwatch, and the purple swirl of the transmatter portal showed up behind her. Alex fired her gun, but by the time the bullet lodged itself on the wall of Kara's bedroom, Lena was already gone, purple portal and all.

    "Jesuschrist, Alex. You could've killed someone!"

    "She's a criminal, Kara!"

    Kelly came in then, gun aiming at the nothingness in front of her.

    "She's still a person." Kara argued. "And I'm not talking just about her, you know how paper thin these walls are."

    "The hell just happened? Why did you fire your gun in here?" Kelly asked her girlfriend. 

    "Luthor was here," Alex said, finally holstering her weapon, looking at her girlfriend, before turning to Kara again. "Why? What did she want?"

    Kara swallowed, because if someone knows the importance of the necklace, it's Alex. She took a deep breath and raised her hand to show her sister the piece of jewelry.

    "She brought this back. She was already here when I got in." She mumbled. Alex kept quiet for a moment. 

    "And why did she have your mother's necklace?" Alex wondered aloud, slowly and with suspicion, and Kara knew she couldn't tell her the truth.

    She could not tell her sister that she'd given Lena Luthor her most prized possession because she felt connected to her. Because she wanted to paint her, only wearing something that was hers. She wanted to draw the two of them together, because they were important to her, and they belonged together. Even if only for a moment in time. Just like Lena and herself. She lied instead, and it was the easiest lie she had ever said.

    Even when she hated doing it.

    "Must have dropped from my pocket. I hadn't even noticed that I didn't have it."

    Alex eyed her like she was evaluating if she was telling the truth, and it must have been convincing enough because she looked at her girlfriend, who almost imperceptibly nodded, like confirming it. Kara had to actively fight the urge to sigh in relief.

    "We'll need to up the security in the apartment, then. But she really got that portal thing now. Doesn't even need the big Lord Tech contraption." Alex commented to Kelly. 

    "Maybe we should move Kara. It seems like she has an obsession with her, if she's seeking her this much."

    "Don't talk about me like I'm not in the room. I don't want to move." Kara put her proverbial foot down. "And she's not obsessed with me. We just have a few things in common and she wants to connect with someone. She misses her freedom."

    Alex gave her a severe look. Seemingly defending the terrorist was a step back from the lie. 

    "We'll see," Kelly, always calm, countered. She turned to her girlfriend. "She will not return right now. It would be stupid. Now she knows you're close by and she would need time to get close to Kara again."

    "Fine." Alex mumbled before turning around and leaving the apartment. Kelly stayed behind, now looking at her, and Kara had the suspicion that maybe she didn't really fool her sister-in-law, as she previously thought.

    "If she comes back, you better arrest her," Kelly whispered. "Because you're not protecting anyone by letting her escape and do whatever the hell she's doing. It's your job. And you're letting everyone down, but especially your sister."

    Kara nodded, then looked at her feet. Kelly was right. It was her job. 

    Except she wasn't sure that Lena deserved to be arrested. 

    She pondered once she was alone again. What exactly was Lena doing? She tried to rack her brain about stuff she heard while she was with her. Tried to remember if she'd heard Brainy mention something, anything about what they were doing. Then she looked at her investigation board again. She was good piercing stories together, she had pretty much every piece available right there. 

    She had to find something, because if she didn't she might be in love with a criminal and if that was the case, then, what exactly did that say about her?

     

     

    Not even two hours later, Alex opened her apartment door and told her to get her tactical gear on.

    "Luthor raided Acre Lee Chemicals, like the criminal she is."

    “What?” Kara asked incredulously, Alex didn't answer, she didn’t even wait for an answer. Kara put on her boots, took the bulletproof vest and followed her sister, still trying to wrap her head around the information. Acre Lee Chemicals was a tiny company, they made fertilizers, pesticides and cleaning compounds, nothing out of the ordinary at simple glance. They waited 5 minutes outside their building for their non-descriptive black van to arrive, and then J’onn was on a tablet communicating long distance with them. Long story short, an alarm went off, and local police called them. Apparently it was clear that it had been Lena.

    When they arrived, only a patrol car was waiting for them. Alex talked with the officers. She came back a few minutes later and talked to the team, and J’onn.

    “They say that the place is trashed. There is Kryptonite, but way more than usual right on the entrance. They didn’t want to compromise the crime scene too much for us.” 

    “Okay, go in. Alex, you're the team leader. Tomorrow you will debrief. Be careful, Argo One.”

    And with that, J’onn disconnected.

    Alex looked at them all. Nia had yet to say a single word to Kara since she gave her a piece of her mind when she came back. Kelly was still looking at the patrol car, pensive and Kara was just… waiting for Alex instructions.

    "We'll go in." Her sister started, "but it wouldn't be the first time she tricks us just to get to Kara. The ultimate goal is to protect Kara. We move as one, no one goes anywhere alone."

     

     

    The place was deserted once they got in, but the police officers were right about it all being Lena's doing. There were papers all over the floor. Computers and glass too. And not even 500 foot from the door, a mountain of kryptonite. Once they got past the reception, and got into the offices, the trail of green rocks diminished, but it was still a steady flow. 

    Not only Lena was making sure they knew it was her, but she was guiding them somewhere. In any case, if any of her teammates thought the same, none said anything. 

    The green rocks trail ended right in front of what seemed to be the biggest office in the building. The door didn't even have a name. 

    "Be on high alert." Alex warned just before opening the door. The office was big, if a little tacky, but it was impeccable in comparison to the rest of the place.

    Ominously, in the middle of the desk, with a big red bow was a box. It was one of those card boxes used to store files. The team didn't stray from one another more than a foot when they enter the office. Alex was the one that got closer to the box.

    "It's for Kara."

    "Of course it is." Nia sardonically said.

    Alex opened the box and furrowed her brow. 

    "It's a bunch of files. Blueprints. And a sample of something." She informed them.

    "How do you want us to proceed?" Kelly asked her girlfriend. Kara didn't need to hear Alex to know what's in her sister's mind. 

    "Let's go another round, make sure that there are no more surprises and then go home. We'll send forensics tomorrow." She looked directly at Kara. "Until I personally examine this, you will not touch this box, understood?"

    "Yes, ma'am." 

     

     

    She couldn't sleep that night. Clearly Lena wanted to tell her something, for her to find something, but Alex didn't let her even look at the box. There was seemingly nothing else in Acre Lee Chemicals, but a forensics team was called in the next day, anyway. 

    Past midday Alex put the box in front of her.

    "I could not find anything out of the ordinary. It's all files from Acre Lee." Alex stated. "The sample was a chemical compound used to clean pools that never made it to market. There are a few newspaper clips about lead poisoning in the Heights, from a few months back. Then there are some financial paper trails that go nowhere so far. Any idea why she would want to give you that?" Alex asked, she could feel Kelly and Nia's gaze on her.

    "I guess she wants to tell me something." Kara answered, sincerely. "There is something in that box that she thinks I can figure out."

    "Then you and I will focus on this box. Nia and Kelly will keep an eye on everything else, should this be a trap." Her sister ordered, then points at the box. "Shall we?"

     

    Her and Alex spend the rest of the day trying to pierce together the content of the box. The blueprints were more of a map of the entire city than anything else. Electric lines, pipelines, garbage disposal routes.  The financials were huge sums of money for the sale of Acre Lee Chemicals. The newspapers were about a low income apartment complex in the Heights that had it habitants displaced because of lead found in the painting. It didn't make much sense. 

     

    Kara entered her apartment with a sense of defeat, of having accomplished nothing. Lena wanted her to see something and she couldn't. Maybe she was too tired to see whatever she was supposed to see, so she tried to sleep as soon as she got home. 

    The box was for her, Lena must have put something just for her to see or something only she would look for. 

     

     

    Endgame day rolled around without Kara paying any attention to it. She woke up with the alarm blaring by her head, thinking she had just gone to sleep. But now she had an idea. As soon as she got to the DEO, she went to the fluorescent flashlights and then used the big red bow as a filter, over the blueprints. There were a series of 'X's over the city, the one on the water pipelines map caught her attention. It's right over the Heights.

    She could feel everyone flitting behind her but didn't pay any attention to them. 

    She dropped the flashlight and went to the newspaper clips. Read them again. And again. And again.

    "Alex, who owns Acree Lee?" She asked, distractedly.

    "It was a closed sale five weeks ago," Alex replied, after looking at some papers. "It actually looks like they are in a liquidation process."

    Kara looked back at the maps with the flashlight and the red bow, then opened her computer. And she dived in. She had bits and pieces, but there was a big piece missing, and she was not sure she had the time to figure it out. 

    It's almost time to go when she called Winn.

    "Hey, can you tell me what exactly was the pool thingy?"

    Winn seemingly looked for it in the computer, by the clackity on the other side of the line.

    "Um, it's an advanced hydromorphic carbon nitrate compound."

    "And hypothetically speaking," she smiled to herself, because maybe she had something here. If she remembered her science correctly. "If this synthetic compound is combined with water, can It exhibit the exact same properties as lead poisoning?"

    "With certainty."

     



    J'onn was assigned as her babysitter while she dressed, as Alex, Kelly and Nia were doing the same.

    She was still ruminating on her time with Lena, trying to figure out her motivations, and leaving the box at Acree Lee. She was racking her brain, still trying to pierce everything together. But there was no use in trying to get answers at that very moment. Endgame would be in an hour and a half, and she had to talk in public, and be the perfect girlfriend and perfect agent. The perfect score.

    "Hey, J'onn?" She tentatively asked, fixing an earring while walking to her living room, where he was sitting. "Can I ask you something?" It was a dumb conversation with Lena, really. About her choosing to work for the DEO instead of continuing her career path in Journalism. Kara mentioned her perfect score, and how good she had been in training and working with Alex.

    Then Lena, of course, asked something she had no answer for.

    "Of course." J'onn said, standing up. "You look beautiful, by the way."

    "Thank you, J'onn." She smiled nervously and took a deep breath. "Nia is excellent at computers and with people. Kelly is an ex-army soldier with a psychology degree. Alex is a doctor with a history of partying and slight alcoholism that almost prevented her from graduating." She paced in her living room, in front of him, who stood stoic, as always. "I'm an orphan with severe trauma who prefers to smile and tried for a long time to be worthy of her cousin to the point of studying the same as him so she could spend time with him."

    "Are you going to ask something, Kara?" J'onn looked at her with something really close to pity and Kara hated it.

    "Why am I the perfect score? There is nothing that makes me better than my team. Or anyone on the other Argo teams."

    He examined her face, that pitying look still there, and then sighed.

    "Officially? The SAT determines your ability to lie. It's the thing that makes a spy a spy." J'onn commented. "The reality it's that it's all a lie. You have to be nominated by someone in the DEO to be part of the Argo Teams." He sat again and sighed. "It really is Cat's project, she wanted more women, exceptional women. Argo One is the more promising, of course. Cat wanted mysticism, drama and to stand out from other agencies, so she created the story. Don't get me wrong, everyone in the Argo Teams is vetted and trained to perfection. But the perfect score story is only to create a myth, someone to beat, a face to the Teams."

    She didn't feel deceived, knowing Cat Grant, this truth checked out.

    "Why me?" She pondered, instead.

    J'onn shrugged again. "I proposed Alex to be the face. She needed a win after Med School, and Jeremiah's death. Cat chose you because you, and I quote, 'reminded her of herself'."

    Kara laughed. "Of course she did."

    And just like that, it was like a weight had been lifted from her chest.

     

    The pressure of perfection going away.

     

     

    The place was packed. Not surprising with all the intelligence agencies in attendance.

    Nia looked beautiful in a turquoise and silvery-white dress, on the arm of a very muscly, tuxedoed man; a friend of James. Kara was clinging to James' arm, his suit jacket was white, with a royal blue lapel, same as his pants. It was the exact same blue of her jumpsuit, with her faux skirt pooling around her feet. She figured that was along the lines of 'less intimidating to the straight male identifying people' that Kelly mentioned. Kelly, who was really glowing in her yellow dress next to Alex, in a Cerulean suit with a yellow tie and pocket square to match her girlfriend's outfit.

    As it was, Argo One presented as a really handsome foursome, which was good, for a first impression, because as soon as they stepped out of their limousine outside the hotel, all eyes were on them. It was nerve-wracking and unsettling. Kara was already dreading having to stand in front of everyone and read the speech that Alex wrote for her. 

    James dragged her around the place, talking with people. James held the bulk of the conversation, as the night dragged on, they even danced a little. But in all honesty, she just wanted the night to end. She grabbed a champagne flute from a waiter and tried to blend into the crowd, escaping her companion while he was occupied rubbing elbows with some big names from the DoD. Kara stopped next to a big round column, trying to not call too much attention to herself, and was so withdrawn in her mind that she startled when she heard a very familiar voice, at the other side of the column. 

    "You're burying your head in the sand, it's a very real possibility, Alex. And you know it." Kelly argued. Her words hushed, probably hiding from the crowd, just like herself. 

    "It's not, babe. It was just a mistake, she was vulnerable after the break up, that's all."

    "She broke up with James." Kelly hissed, as Kara realized she was the topic of conversation. "Kara is not heartbroken because she broke up with my brother. She's heartbroken because the girl she likes is, as far as we can tell, a criminal. She's distracted and miserable both at work and at home." Kelly had been observing her, and Kara was not sure why she's surprised, she had been suspecting as much, but she thought she'd been great at fooling her sister-in-law. Kara was both curious and worried, it was the first time she has ever heard them this close to fighting. Kelly kept talking. "The most she has actually worked is on that box, only because she's sure there is some kind of message from Lena there. She's not eating or sleeping, again. Alex, she might actually feel something for Lena Luthor."

    Kara is frozen, Kelly had been reading her like a book, since she came back, and yet she was not sure why her and Alex were talking about that, right this moment. 

    "I just think that you, making Kara say that in her speech is unnecessarily cruel." Kelly said, in an even lower voice. "You, not wanting her to do it, doesn't mean that her feelings are not real or valid."

    "It doesn't matter, Kelly. She has to say stuff like this, unless she wants to be also considered a suspect. And I'll not let my sister do such stupidity." Alex finished, and then silence. No reply from Kelly, no extra comment from Alex, only the blaring of the music from the speakers.

     

     

    Her sister found her 10 minutes later, still standing in the same spot. The champagne in her hand, hot and flat. Thoughts of Lena, her own feelings, the box and the missing pieces invading her mind, since she had overheard her sister's discussion with Kelly.

    "Okay, so I just wrote down a couple words explaining our version of what happened," Alex distractedly said, giving her a couple of pages. "And expressing your gratitude at being honored." Her sister made a pause, looking at her, clearly uncomfortable. Then, she sighed. "I'm sorry that we've been fighting. It's just I really want what's best for you."

    "I know." She said, because out of everything, of that she has no doubts. And she knew that Alex was right. Whatever her feelings about Lena, tonight she had to play a part. For her own good, for her team's good, and maybe in the long run, for Lena's sake too.

    Alex smiled at her, and then the voice of Cat Grant, standing in a podium, replaced the music on the speakers.

    "Once in a blue moon an exceptional young lady comes along who truly exemplifies what it means to be an Argo Agent. Discipline. Energy…." Kara sort of drifted off, focusing on preparing herself for whatever she would read. She knew it would probably feel like a stab wound, considering that her time with Lena Luthor was the happiest she had been in a long while. She came back when she heard her own name from Cat's loud voice. "Kara Danvers survived one of the worst tragedies that could happen to anyone." The General was poised, and made all the dramatic pauses she could to keep the public engaged. Kara really should have seen the lie behind her perfect score ages ago. "Kidnapped, held hostage for endless days." Kara had to bite down a smirk that threatened to escape her, the fantastic irony of that line. She felt freer with Lena than now. Ms Grant kept going, "What fears she may have known, none of us can fully appreciate. But she has agreed to tell us part of what she learned on her journey. It gives me great pleasure tonight to give our greatest honor, the Binder-Plastino award, to DEO Agent of the Year, to our very own perfect score, Kara Danvers."

    Alex subtly pushed her toward the stage, Kara did her best to smile at Cat Grant. She stood in front of the podium and looked at the pages her sister gave her, for the first time.

    "Courage. It's a big word. I did not know what courage was to me until I endured twelve agonizing days in captivity, captured and held hostage by a madwoman." The words tasted so much like acid, that she had to take a pause. "It was a routine mission gone terribly wrong. I was separated from my squad and knocked unconscious. I awoke in the arms of the enemy." Her knuckles were so tight at the podium's ends that they had gone white. "Just the sound of my own desperate thoughts to keep me company." She continued, looking at Alex, Kelly and Nia from time to time. "I learnt that, if there is corruption in this city, Lena Luthor is behind it. If there is indecency to be found, she is the root." She lifted her head, because now she understood why Kelly was worried about the speech. And then, the worst happened.

    Up in the mezzanine, standing in an all black tracksuit, hair in a ponytail, was Lena, stoic and beautiful. Kara forced herself to keep reading, trying to not let her voice waver.

    What the hell was Lena thinking? Showing up in a place with hundreds of people dying to capture her.

    "It's not until moments like this when you are forced to take stock of yourself that you see what you are made of. It's not until moments like this when you have to remember what's right and true because it's moments like this when you discover what it truly means to be part of the DEO's Argo Team."

    Applause broke out and Kara looked up again, looked at Lena, only to see her still standing there looking directly at her, but unaware of the gun aimed at her head, by none other than James. 

    Lena must have seen something reflected on her face, because she turned around. However, Kara couldn't actually see whatever happened after because Cat Grant was putting a medal-like thing on her neck. But there was no gunshot, so perhaps Lena was okay.

    She left the stage, in a daze, with the single thought of finding Lena and getting her the hell out of this place. She took off the medal and put it on Alex's hand.

    "I—I have to go to the bathroom." 

    "Are you okay?" Kelly asked.

    Kara didn't stop and just kept walking, "yes, of course." She said, not even taking the time to turn around and look at her sister-in-law.

    She kept walking, but risked a look at the mezzanine again. No sign of Lena or James in there. 

    If Kara was panicking, that was between her and her God. Lena stupidly going to a Hotel where she knew every agent in town would be in high alert and looking for her, made no sense, why would she take such a risk?

    Mentally she traced a route up to the floor she saw her in, and followed that path as fast as possible. She found James unconscious on the floor. No gun in sight, but no blood either so that was good news. She took James' pulse, steady, and couldn't help but smirk. He will be so pissed off when he wakes up an realizes that 5.5ft Lena Luthor knocked his ass down. A problem for later, she thought as she stands up. She had to find Lena before any of the other hundreds of agents did. 

    She went in blind, opening up doors, and crossing hallways with no destination in mind, but just following her instinct. Lena needed a quiet place to use the portal watch. It felt as if an hour has gone by, but it had been less than 10 minutes when she finds herself in an eerily quiet cellar, with boxes and tables and barrels. She walked as quickly as she could without running, her heart drumming in her ears, she turned around making sure she was not being followed when she crashed against something… someone.

    Instinct took over, she drew out her pistol and aimed at whoever she smashed into. When she finally, in that fraction of a second, saw past the barrel of her gun she noticed green eyes, one of them with a drop of blue.

    "We really need to stop meeting like this," Lena whispered, lowering her own gun, and smiling.

    "I didn't mean any of that." Kara blurted out, because her priorities are clearly in order. "I didn't write it."

    "I know, Kara," Lena said, and then she's kissing her and Kara felt like she could breathe again, when she hadn't even realized that she couldn't.

    She hugged Lena, her free hand at her waist, still kissing her as if they were running out of time.

    It hits Kara that they are.

    "What are you doing here?" She questioned, pulling away from Lena's lips. "For being a genius, It was such a stupid move, Lena."

    "I know." Lena replied, with her characteristic cool demeanor, cupping Kara's face with her free hands. "But it turns out that this girl is just consuming all of my thoughts, so much so that I can't focus on work."

    Kara had no other choice than to smile, like an idiot.

    "And coming here was a really rookie mistake," Alex's voice rang close. Kara was so focused on Lena she didn't hear her sister approach, and now Lena had a beretta pointing at her head. Again.

    "Alex, wait." Kara put herself between her sister with her gun, and Lena, it was useless anyways, because Kelly and Nia were both flanking them, with their firearms out too.

    "Kara, for Gods' sake, she's a criminal, we're cops. What did you think was going happen?" Alex angrily asked.

    "But I am not," Lena states. Loud, clear, secure of herself. "At least, I think you'll be more interested in what I am actually trying to prevent."

    Kara furrowed her brow, she could see that the declaration also gave pause to her sister.

    "Explain," Alex conceded.

    "But first I need to get out of here, I guess your boyfriend must be waking up right about now so," Lena commented to Kara, and Kelly tensed up.

    "He's okay," Kara quickly added. "She just knocked him out. Let's go to my apartment. We can talk there."

    "You can't come with me, Kara." Lena replied. "He's gonna tell everyone that he saw me, and they will look for you. Your sister can come with me. She will feel better if she can keep her eyes on me." Lena smiled, again like she was talking about the weather. "But only us girls. Then, if you're not satisfied with what I have to say, then you can arrest me. I promise."

    "And what exactly do you have to say?" Alex asked, as sardonic as ever. 

    Lena leveled her with sure eyes.

    "The truth."

    Chapter End Notes

    Did you really think I would make Lena Luthor evil?

    Veritas Liberabit

    Chapter Notes

    Hello! Very sorry about the enormous delay in between this chapter and the previous. I got too in my head about it, and then I got a massive writers block, and then I got too in my head again, but it got finally done.
    I hope you like it. There are some warnings on the end notes, as they are spoilery, please check them out.
    Many thanks to Lara, for cheering me on the entire time and helping me edit this chapter.
    And thank you for sticking with me on this journey. Happy Holidays!

     

    Lena saw as Kara, Nia and Kelly retreated and went back to their party. She kept her neutral expression, even when she knew that Kara's sister would have some words for her. Alex had yet to holster her gun, and as soon as her friends were out of sight she stabbed the barrel of it on Lena's lower ribs. Predictably.

    "Have you been given the order to kill me, Agent Danvers?" She asked, not a drop of fear in her voice. Even if she was actually shitting her pants; but the redhead didn't need to know that. 

    "I have not been ordered to bring you alive," Alex seethed. "What did you do to my sister?"

    Lena tilted her head involuntarily, she didn't quite understand the question. And her sincere confusion must show up on her face, because Alex's firearm stopped pushing up on her side. Her face contorted into a sort of surprised expression.

    "What do you think I could have possibly done to her?"

    "You have experimented with Q-Waves," Alex responded in a grumble. While she finally holstered her gun and dragged her by the elbow, looking for the exit. 

    Lena couldn't stop the snort that escaped her lips. "The Q-Waves idea is completely theoretical." She commented, letting the woman guide her. "But good on you for somewhat understanding applied electromagnetism, Agent Danvers."

    "Shut up," Alex mumbled.

    They walked quickly, taking the back exit, and never going into the hotel lobby. Alex then used her phone to call one of those ridesharing cars. A silver sedan was quick to arrive and they climbed into the back. Alex sat unusually close to her until Lena could feel the barrel of the gun on her ribs again. 

    "Is that really necessary?"

    "Well, I am a little pissed off that I'm here, instead of dancing with my girlfriend with whom I had plans. And I have you to blame, so… Yes."

    "Fair enough."

     

    The trip to Kara's apartment was quick. Brainy's assessment and Kara's loose comments about the fierceness of the oldest Danvers were apparently really on point. The woman was inscrutable, one singular expression. She felt like a child being scolded only by the power of her stare. And yet, Lena could see some similarities between her and Kara. Even with their seemingly polar opposite personalities.

    Lena observed as Alex ditched her suit jacket and hung it by the door, and loosened the tight Windsor knot of her tie.

    "Do you want something to drink?" Alex asked, not unkind, but not really amiable either.

    "I would love a glass of scotch, but it's not likely to find that here if it depends on Kara." Lena commented, sitting on the couch. It was the comfiest furniture she'd ever sat on. It was clearly old and well loved.

    Alex stopped in the kitchen to look at her, studying without a word. Then, very slowly she turned to one of the cabinets, open it and from the very back picked up a half empty whiskey bottle.

    "I stand corrected, then." Lena conceded while she watched the older Danvers sister pour a finger or two of the liquor into two mugs. They are mismatched, with childish cartoon drawings in them. And so undeniable Kara that it was ridiculous how Lena was even attracted to this woman.

    "To be fair, this is mine." Alex mumbled, giving her one of the mugs and sitting in the chair in front of her. "I don't think she even knows it's there." 

    Lena chuckled and took a sip of whiskey. It's not bad, she'd definitely tasted worse while studying with people almost twice her age, back when she was completing her last PhD. She knew Alex just changed tactics. Anger and pointing a gun didn't work on her, and from there forward, instead of the stick, she would try the carrot. 

    Two could definitely play that game. "Kara wanted us to play pool together." Lena confessed, letting herself melt into the couch. "Like, in teams. Us, against you and your girlfriend. She thought we could win."

    "As if," Alex snorted, and Lena was very aware that she was watching her every expression. "Kara is pretty terrible at pool, you would have to be freaking extraordinary to win against Kelly and I."

    Lena arched an eyebrow, looking at Alex. "Is that a challenge?"

    Alex kept quiet, for a moment and Lena knew this was when the balance would shift. To which side, it was still to be determined.

    "No, not a challenge." The redhead murmured. "But, maybe there will never be a way to find out, since you'll probably end up in a supermax prison."

    Lena hummed, her gaze staying on Alex's face. She could still play the aloof, nonchalant  façade she'd been playing for half her life. However, not only was her freedom in the long run at stake, but also Kara's. 

    Any future with Kara was dependent on the woman in front of her. For all her confidence and rebelliousness, Kara would never go against her big sister for the long haul. 

    "I'm hoping my good intentions will sweeten the deal." She finally conceded. "I was ready to accept that faith… until I met your sister." Lena took a tentative sip of her drink, opening up had never been easy for her. And opening up to this woman, it was just almost unbearable. "Which is preposterous because we barely know each other, but…" A sigh escaped her, unconsciously, without her approval. "She makes me feel seen, understood. She makes me laugh. And I think I may do the same for her."

    Silence enveloped them, and Lena could feel the blush climbing up her neck and her cheeks. She felt like an open wound, completely exposed. And the more time the silence extended, the more attractive the idea of jumping from the window seemed.

    Finally, Alex groaned in annoyance, standing up.

    "That idiot could not have a crush on the good kid from IT," Alex walked back to the kitchen. "No, she had to go for the genius criminal. Like a fucking cliché." 

    Lena observed the agent for a moment, while she poured more alcohol onto her mug. Drank it all, and then poured some more. She reminded Lena of Sam, whom she missed very much.

    "I hope that you understand, once I explain everything," Lena mumbled. "I just felt like I had no other option."

    "Going full criminal was your only option? I can't believe that." Alex spited out, sitting again and leaving the bottle in the coffee table in the middle of them.

    Lena shrugged. "The quickest way from point A to point B, is a straight line. Especially when corners are full of bureaucracy and people with other interests."

    The agent snorted. "You're mixing your metaphors."

    Lena took the bottle and poured the amber liquid onto her own mug. "Maybe," she whispered, just as the door opened abruptly, showing a very worried Kara.

    Kelly with Nia hot on her heels. The worry didn't immediately leaves the blonde's face. Lena saw the moment Kara registered that she wasn't in any imminent danger, her entire frame slightly melting on the spot. 

    Lena turned to Alex again. "But it's the truth." She said, ready to tell her tale, and convince these people that she was not the bad guy. But that the enemy was closer than they suspected.



     

     

    Kara was trying really hard to keep her cool, but she felt jittery and off-kilter. She grabbed one of the champagne flutes from one of the servers going around and almost drank it in its entirety, if it weren't for Nia's glare.

    "You are acting unhinged, calm down." Nia mumbled. "And you look like you've seen a ghost, act normal. You're both spies, for heaven's sake." She whispered to Kelly.

    Kara looked at her sister-in-law who looked even more worried than her.

    "Are you sure that she didn't hurt James? What if she does something to Alex?" Kelly quickly mumbled, in a very tiny voice.

    Kara touched Kelly's shoulder, trying to calm her down, "James is okay, I promise. And Lena would not hurt Alex." Kara tried to smile. "Even if Lena wanted to, Alex is too stubborn to let her." She kept to herself that it was more likely that Alex would do something to Lena, than the other way around. Kelly nodded and sighed, they only needed to wait for Jimmy to find them and then stop his paranoia from reaching anyone else, or something along those lines. 

    "Let's go dance," Nia offered. "We are supposed to be having a good time, come on." She didn't wait for a response, she simply grabbed them and dragged both Kara and Kelly to the middle of the dance floor and started dancing around them. Well, maybe dancing was too generous of a word, her moves were so ridiculous that Kara couldn't blame Kelly when a full laugh broke out of her. But Nia's silliness paid off almost immediately, both relaxing into silly dancing with each other.

     

     

    It had barely been two minutes when James approached them, looking desperate and grabbing Kara by the arm.

    "Hey, watch out!"

    "Are you okay?" He asked, an edge on his voice. 

    "Why wouldn't I be?" She acted as if she were confused by his behavior. "Are you okay? You look like you're about to pass out."

    "Yeah, listen to me, Lena Luthor is here. We need to get you to a safe house." He whispered. 

    His hand on her arm squeezed with more force than she would have predicted, and Kara could not avoid wincing at it. Thankfully, Nia saw it and pulled her closer to her, making James' hand drop.

    "What do you mean Lena Luthor is here?" Her friend questioned James in a hiss, who was looking around them as if they were surrounded, behind enemy lines.

    "She's here. She was on the balcony while you were doing your speech." He explained urgently, not raising his voice. 

    Nia made a show of looking around them. Kelly approached him with the calm that characterized her, putting her hand on his shoulder, and asking, "Are you sure?"

    "Yes I'm sure, she gave me a shiner." He whisper-shout, pointing to his cheek, where very faintly Kara could see a slight inflammation, and she had to bite her lip to not smile. 

    "We can tell Winn to assemble a squad to search for her," Kelly offered, and Kara's mood changed drastically. That could take hours, the entire DEO searching for every possible hideout. She wanted to go to her apartment, make sure both Alex and Lena were okay, and not ready to kill each other.

    Thanks God or the sun or the stars or whatever deity that actually exists, James seemed to agree with her as he shook his head no.

    "It would take too much time, and she has a time advantage, because I don't know how much time I was out." He grumbled while taking a step towards her, like he was going to grab Kara's arm again, but thought it better, and changed this mind at the last second. "It's better to take you to a safe place. Where is Alex?"

    Nia, was the one who answered, barely a second to think of an excuse. "She was planning to make it a night with Kelly, so…" She looked at the younger Olsen, shrugging. "I had to keep you distracted so she could prepare everything." Kara supposed there is some nugget of truth in there when Kelly's cheeks actually flush red, but Nia kept talking like nothing. "I will call her, we'll meet and then figure out a plan, okay?"

    Kara realized, that the less people knew about Lena showing up the better. "Jimmy, don't ruin the night for the rest of the people. I think you'll do better if people don't know about it, and they don't panic." 

    James considered for a moment and then nodded, "You're right, but you need to go now."

    "Already called a car, let's go." Nia lifted her phone showing the screen with a rideshare app and then pulled Kara towards the exit, while Kelly said goodbye to her brother. 

    Sure enough, by the time they got outside, after shouldering the sea of people on the dance floor, a white small SUV was parking close to the entryway, ready for them.

     

     

    None of them said anything while they made the relatively short trip to their building, but Kara's leg was bouncing up and down the entire time.

    She almost ditched her heels once she got into their apartment building and went for the stairs, but it didn't seem like a possibility with Kelly and Nia's respective dresses. The lift was even slower than usual, but when they got to her floor she had her key ready and in one fluid movement she opened the door.

    She didn't know what to expect when she entered her apartment, but it certainly wasn't Alex and Lena drinking a bottle of whisky, that she had no idea where they'd got from, out of her Disney mugs. In any case, seeing the both of them okay and not on the verge of shooting each other, relaxed her instantly.

    "But it's the truth." She heard Lena say to her sister, and it was like watching her put on an armor.

    Alex stood up, searching her girlfriend's eyes, "Did you have any problem getting away?"

    "No, I texted Winn to let me know if anything turns up, and to check up on Jimmy." Kelly shrugged, even if a little conflicted. "We shouldn't have any issues."

    Alex nodded then looked at Lena, a serious expression on her face. "Well, time to fulfill that promise of truth. And if I am not satisfied with it, I'll arrest you. I promise you that."

    Kara's eyes are fixed on Lena, who looked at her in return and had the audacity to give her a tiny smile. A smile that was supposed to say that she's got it, that Kara had nothing to worry about. Instead it feels like Kara's heart could very possibly jump out of her chest at any moment. 

    Lena took the Winnie the Pooh mug and stood up, walking closer to the TV and faced them all, "I assume you got my package from Acre Lee?"

    "We did." Alex answered immediately, sitting next to Kelly and Nia on the couch. Kara finally took off her damn heels, keeping her eyes trained on Lena, not daring to even sit down.

    "What did you learn from it?" Lena questioned, and she was looking directly at Kara too, with her pretty green-blue eyes.

    It was like she was back in their warehouse and the rest of her team, including her sister, were not in her own living room.

    "They are apparently in a liquidation process, we were not able to find out who bought them last." Kara commented.

    "The sample?" Lena questioned her.

    "A pool cleaner; never made it to market," Alex answered for Kara.

    "It's a…" Kara forced herself to remember Winn's words, she was tired and not sure she got them all, but she hoped that Lena would fill in the blanks. "Carbon nitrate compound, but that if mixed with water, it can mimic lead poisoning."

    The corner of Lena's lips quirked in a very subtle smile and Kara couldn't help but smile too. It had been a while since she enjoyed any sciencey conversation.

    "A hydromorphic carbon nitrate compound, yes. Did you figure it out then?" Lena baited her and she would really, really like to wipe her smile from her face. 

    Kara was not sure if she wanted to do it by being right or by kissing her. "I matched the map to the paper clippings. Are you implying that The Heights poisoning wasn't accidental?"

    "It wasn't accidental. There was no lead paint anywhere in the area." Lena answered, succinct and without pause. "Did you find anything else? What about the financial records?" 

    Finally, Kara peeled her eyes off of Lena, and looked at her sister. 

    Alex rolled her eyes, "I could not find anything, only someone named Sebastian Wilde, CEO of a 'Thunder Corporation'."

    Kara and Nia perked up at the exact same time.

    "Sebastian Wilde?"

    "Thunder Corporation?"

    Alex's brows puckered in the middle of her forehead. "Nia?"

    "Winn told me about this investigation he's spearheading, a 'Thunder Corporation' has been doing a lot of acquisitions and liquidations, too quickly. It's suspicious, but he has not been able to find anything illegal yet. Neither could the Treasury Department."

    "Okay. Kara?" Alex prompted, and Kara felt like she'd just got 800 extra puzzle pieces, on a 500 piece puzzle. 

    "Is your brother, isn't it?" She asked Lena. "Sebastian Wilde, it's the myth he's obsessed with."

    Lena cocked her head to the side, looking at Kara for a moment and then nodded. "Yes. Lex is behind it all. The poisoning is not random either. It's a very thought out plan that has been years in the making. I actually think it was my father who started it."

    "Your brother? Who was nominated for a Nobel Peace prize?" Kelly challenged, skeptical. Kara can't really blame her, Lex Luthor's reputation was impeccable. "You really want us to believe that?"

    Lena lifted the mug still in her hand and took a swig, and Kara knew that whatever she was going to say, it was something that she would be taking to her grave in any other circumstance. Lena left the mug back on the coffee table and crossed her arms over her chest, armor back in place.

    "The Luthor's are old money, but the real fortune came up in the American south, revolutionary times." Lena commented, her eyes dancing through the four of them. "When LuthorCorp's parent company was founded, LMart, grandfather Lachlan realized that it was better for the business if he played both sides. Publicly he did everything he could to establish a neutral position in every possible topic." Lena tried to gloss over it, but Kara could see how tense she was. "My father, Lionel, took it a step further when he took over and founded LuthorCorp. Making public remarks here and there, benevolent, fair, progressive. Everyone quotes him saying, 'It doesn't matter where you come from, if you work hard, you can make it.'"

    She let out a sardonic laugh, bordering on a scoff. "He was playing the crowd, of course, because at the manor, he used to speak more candidly on the topic, especially if he had something to drink. My father was sexist, racist and classist. He was xenophobic and homophobic. And so are my mother and brother, but they know how to play a crowd. Being open about it all is bad for our business, thus they resort to machinations and manipulation." Lena shrugged and finally uncrossed her arms. She was not relaxed but the way she was holding herself was less tight and Kara took a moment to connect the dots on her mind. 

    "And while awful, it wouldn't be as terrible as it is, if it was something they only did behind closed doors, with no consequences. If they did not have such a massive influence. But they do," Lena said, with barely contained ire, and to Kara it was like watching a completely different person. "Now the political climate is changing again, for the worse. And my brother is successfully executing his magnum opus, and no one even knows about it."

    Alex cleared her throat, her own arms crossed, her face inscrutable. "Even if your brother is all that, sadly none of it is illegal. You on the other hand have done plenty of illegal stuff."

    "My only crimes are a hacking that happened over a decade ago; and stand in the way of my brother's plan." Lena rebuffed, lifting her head defiantly. 

    "You still have not said what this supposed plan is." Kelly pointed out.

    Lena sighed, and rubbed at her face. "It's a lot of moving pieces, and I'm not even sure I have the entire picture figured out."

    Kara may have an idea about it. "He is poisoning low income neighborhoods. With the Acre Lee compound, isn't he?"

    "Yes. Not only low income, but immigrant neighborhoods too." Lena nodded. "It's not his only plan, but it's the one of the most promising, because with long exposure it can lead to death."

    "At that scale, it's genocidal." Kara mumbled.

    "National City is not the only testing ground." Lena continued, wringing her hands together in a nervous tic. "He is working with very powerful people, who have very similar goals. And I'm pretty sure he's using other people to advance his agenda, too. The earthquake at The Glades in Star City. The explosion of Star Labs in Central City. The people suffering psychotic illusions in Gotham. Lex has his hands in all of it."

    "Do you have proof of this?" Alex questioned.

    Lena very discreetly cringed at the query, "I have pieces. Nothing too incriminating. I would have brushed past it if it weren't because I know my brother. And my mother." She bit her bottom lip, like thinking if it was important to reveal the next nugget of information. "I wasn't even the one to find the first clue. That was Querl."

    Nia's breath hitched, close to Kara.

    Lena kept talking unaware of it. "He was one of our leaders in IT. He found a file buried in the metadata of the servers. Thought it was malware or something. He decrypted it and he came to me, he did not understand what it meant because of Lex's reputation. He calls the entire thing Project Cadmus."

    "Hold on, that's a government program." Kelly chimed in, frowning. "Something about genetic enhancement for soldiers. Jimmy went to a meeting about it."

    "I remember that, it was like four months ago, the Army thing. He didn't get selected for the next phase." Kara confirmed.

    "Like I said, it's a plan with many moving pieces." Lena muttered, "Doesn't surprise me that genetic enhancement is something he's considering. But I can tell you that James would never be part of the program, other than maybe as a base line. He doesn't fit Lex's very specific criteria." Lena said darkly, and there was no need to elaborate, the meaning was crystal clear. "In any case, I found out pretty quickly that I could not go to the authorities for this. Not when many of them were in it too."

    And just like that, Kara finally got her answer. She finally knew why Lena had gone rogue. Because she had no one to trust, not when the government was involved in the crime too.

    "Holy shit, that's why they wanted us to arrest you." Kara breathed out. "That's why they pinned all of those deaths on you."

    "What?" Kelly and Alex asked at the same time, her sister more loud and scandalized than her own girlfriend.

    "It was the reason why I started our research in the first place." Kara said to Kelly, with a tiny smile. "I found it very… odd that all agencies were ordered to capture a 'Cyber-Terrorist'," she made air quotes with her hands. "Especially when the supposed targets were all private companies. And then she was accused of killing the DEA agents in Peru, and the joint force agents in Iceland. Did you know that none of them had autopsies?"

    Nia groaned, hiding her face on her hands. "You were right with your murder board…"

    "Why didn't you say anything earlier?" Kelly asked, looking at Kara.

    She shrugged, "I had nothing solid. I didn't find out about the autopsies until after we met." She pointed to Lena with a movement of her head. "Now that she's telling us more, I'm connecting the dots." She finished smiling at Lena. 

    "Everything is very convenient, isn't it?" Alex drawls. "I still have no concrete evidence. I only have your word and, excuse my frankness, that means shit to me."

    "I know it's not much, but I can give you the initial file we found on LuthorCorp's server and any other document that could be relevant." Lena agreed, looking at Alex. 

    "I still don't believe you. In my eyes Lex Luthor is an upstanding citizen, meanwhile you're the criminal, you admitted to it." Alex coldly declared.

    "Alex, come on, it makes perfect sense." Kara snapped.

    "Does it? Because to me it is just she said, he said." Alex stood up, pointing to Lena. "She says he's xenophobic and racist, but everything we know about him points to the contrary, and he's a very prominent figure." She exploded, wildly moving her arms. 

    Lena nodded and accepted the judgment, and Kara wanted to scream at her, to use that big brain she boasts so much about and convince Alex. Apparently Lena heard her, even though she did not open her mouth.

    "I assume you're an outstanding team. You're the pride and joy of the DEO, I'm guessing it's not because of your pretty faces." Lena started pacing in front of the TV, her hands clasped at her back. "May I ask, agent Danvers, how did the DEO, the CIA, FBI, Homeland Security, and every other agency know that it was me who was raiding Faolan Shipping?"

    Silence fell on the living room, none of them had an answer. They acted like soldiers, they got the info from their superiors and that was it.

    "What about Lockwood Family Steel? Schaffenberger Chemicals?" Lena continued, and the silence kept stretching and Kara breathed because she got them. They cannot answer. Lena kept asking. "Did you even know I went to Daxam Industries? Or Merlyn Global? Queen Consolidated? LexOil? AmerTek?" 

    "Get to the point," Alex seethed.

    "My point, Agent Danvers, is that there is someone high enough in your bureaucracy who feeds you the information. Filters it, purges it, even." Lena had stopped pacing. Her hands, still at her back. Looking at them as if they were her public and she was giving a lecture. "My point is, that you had no idea about me until it was written on a memo directed at all security agencies. An order to capture me."

    It was like the air had been sucked out of the room. Lena was not implying that the supposed whistleblower was any Joe Schmoe, but someone who could influence the entire Intelligence Community.

    "They pinned everything on me because, supposedly, they found my digital signature in the new hackings, from something I did when I was a child, right?"

    "Yes," Alex confirmed.

    "There is a fundamental flaw in that deduction. Because I am not the one doing the hacking." Lena remarked, and Kara was reminded of the computer that resembled a mountain in the first warehouse.

    "Brainy does the hacking," she said aloud.

    Lena nodded. "He's much better at it than me." 

    "Okay, let's pretend we believe you, what is your brother's goal? Kill minorities?" Kelly pondered aloud.

    Lena shrugged, with an uncertain look on her eyes. "I don't know if that's the goal or just a step on the plan. Whatever the case, innocent lives will be lost, and I am not willing to sit idly and let it happen." 

    Kara had never been so attracted to anyone as much as she was attracted to Lena in that particular moment. 

    "Stop it." Nia told her, kicking Kara on her shin.

    "Auch! Stop what?"

    "I can hear your thoughts, they are very loud. Keep it in your pants, this is serious." She mumbled, but there was not enough space in her living room, and everyone heard her anyway. 

    Alex seethed in place, and Lena blushed a pretty color, but Kelly kept the conversation going. "Why are you talking to us now, then? If you're so sure you cannot trust the government."

    "Because I met Kara, and I trust her. She trusts you, and that's good enough for me." Lena replied with ease, looking at her. Kara smiled back, and the only reason she did not get closer to her and kissed her, was because her team was present. "I also suspect that Lex knows about it and he will accelerate his plans."

    "Why do you think that?" Nia questioned. 

    Lena was quiet for a moment, then turned to the youngest of them all, "When Kara was with me, how did you guys find the warehouse?"

    Kelly was the one who answered, in a low voice, "James. He… Lucy told him she got a tip."

    "Who is Lucy?"

    "She's a lawyer from the Army, she worked with us at the DEO in a few cases, Lucy Lane." Kara explained to her.

    "Any relationship with General Sam Lane?"

    "His daughter…"

    Lena let out a sarcastic snigger, and turned to Alex. "Hell of a coincidence, don't you think?"

     

     

    Alex, of course, required more proof. Lena asked Kara for her laptop and procured an USB drive from one of her pockets. Then, she completely disconnected the internet on the entire apartment and asked them to put their phones in airplane mode.

    She opened files upon files, upon files on the laptop. It seemed interminable. Kara knew some names, high ranking officers in all government military areas, including but not limited to: Major General Sam Lane, General Hank Henshaw, General Wade Eiling, Colonel Anthony Miller. Then she recognized a few DEO names, people that had worked with them: Jason McGill, Raymond Jensen, Marcus Taylor. A few people she knew from past Endgames, military people of different ranks in different agencies: T.O. Morrow, Dean Petrocelli, Carl Draper, Martin Proctor, Robert Todd, Colonel James Harper.

    Then there were entrepreneurial names, like: Malcolm Merlyn, Kenneth Bender, Moira Queen, Rhea Matthews, Maxwell Lord, Morgan Edge, Eobard Thawne, Benjamin Lockwood, Mercy and Otis Graves, Gemma Cooper and, of course, Lex and Lillian Luthor. 

    There were project names like: Project DNA-Cadmus. Project Undertaking. Project Red Progeny. Project Scarecrow, and Project Medusa B.

    It's all incriminating enough to, at least, guarantee an investigation with the proper authorities, but at that point Kara truly understood why Lena ran away and tried to stop everything from the shadows herself. She couldn't trust anyone. Especially, and most chillingly, because of the name at the end of the list. Phil Baker. The fucking President of the United States of America.

    The silence was chilling, after Lena disconnected the USB and let them turn on their phones again. Kara reconnected everything, mulling over all the new information.

    Kelly immediately got a barrage of messages, she read them quickly and typed a reply with a light scowl. "Winn says they found no evidence of Lena at the hotel. Jimmy was apparently drinking heavily and they think he imagined the whole thing."

    Kara cringed, and she see Lena doing something similar, but with a touch of guilt. Kara couldn't help it, she simply tried to free her of any remorse. "Hey, you had to knock him out. He had his gun on your head."

    "I know. It's just… he did nothing wrong, he was just trying to protect you." 

    "Yes, but now that we know the truth," Kara said, pointing to herself and the team. "It's safer for him and us that they think that you weren't there. We don't know if any of his superiors are involved or not." She finished looking at her laptop, and then turned to her sister. "Right?"

    Alex took a moment to order her thoughts. It seemed to Kara that she was a little in denial. Her sister, finally, sighed. "I need to confirm at least… something, anything of what you're saying. Because if what you're implying it's true then… It's very bad."

    "Also, I would like to ask some questions," Kelly added, her eyes set on Lena. "Some names… don't quite fit the profile you're painting."

    "Of course, I will answer to the best of my abilities." Lena had a tiny smile, like she couldn't believe she managed to convince them. Kara can relate to that.

    Kelly asked about Max Lord first, somewhere next to her, Alex shuddered as if someone had dumped ice water on her. They had studied him and Alex had been one on one with him several times and other than, scamming people of their hard earned money, he was a pretty okay dude. 

    "I don't think he knew he was helping Lex. Don't get me wrong," Lena sat on one of the individual chairs. "Max is a little misogynistic and slightly homophobic, but he's for the most part pretty inoffensive. But… have you paid attention to who he was scamming?" 

    Nia was the one that actually answered in a whisper, after a brief silence, "Middle class immigrants."

    They all seemed to accept the answer and Kelly moved on. "What about Rhea Matthews? She's not even American."

    It was no secret, it was part of her success story. She was the wife of a rich man in a tiny European town in Georgia; Valor. Her husband died, and she came to America to live with her then 20-years-old son and founded Daxam Industries, specialists in all things construction. The company grew at a fast pace because of a few, gigantic government contracts and now the company was the leader in construction on the west coast. 

    Lena took her time to answer, a pensive look on her face. "I don't think she knows she's being played. I do have a theory as to why she's working with my brother." She looked directly at Kara. "And more importantly why my brother sought her out. You gave me the idea."

    "Me?"

    "You reminded me about the radiation flood on Kandor." Lena said, with a gentle voice.

    Another set of the puzzle pieces found their places in Kara's mind, then. Valor was next to Kandor, a part of it was also affected by the radiation; her husband had died because of it. "You think Lex wanted to know about it…" She stopped herself, while having an epiphany. "Project Medusa… 'проект горгона' It's the Acre Lee product."

    Lena nodded, "Yes, that's his version of Project Gorgon, one of them at least. I think Lex promised her retribution against Kaznia, a link to President Baker. I believe it's unlikely they will deliver, they most likely will pin whatever atrocity they commit on her." Lena shrugged, and Kara agreed, by the mere fact that Lena knew the real Lex and not his public persona.

    She looked at Nia, challenging her. "I told you he was too squeaky clean to be real."

    "You were a lousy source," Nia quipped back. "This one at least has first hand experience." 

    Lena rolled her eyes and then looked at Kelly, to finish her deduction. She was ruthless and succinct. "As far as I can tell, Merlyn is still pissed about the delinquents that killed his wife, that's his motivation. He convinced the Queen's to join him. Thawne has a vendetta against all low-income people because his city used taxpayer money to fund low-income housing instead of funding his Physical Tachyon research pipedream. Lockwood is openly racist and xenophobic, just like Mercy Graves. Otis and Edge just roll to whoever pays them the best. Sam Lane it's a die-hard nationalist, and the President… well, he's lining his pockets and thinking himself the hero of the story."

    Silence stretched for a minute or so, and Kara could see that Alex and Kelly were silently debating if they believed Lena or not. And Nia was looking forlornly at her phone.

    "Okay." Kelly conceded.

    "What do you need us to do?" Alex asked, apparently reaching a mental consensus with her girlfriend.

    Lena's surprise was clear in her eyes, at least it was to Kara. She smiled at Lena and nodded, encouraging her to talk.

    "Well," Lena started. "First we would need to find at least one of your superior officers that is not involved in this. So you four are not accused of insubordination. Anyone come to mind?"

    The response was not only automatic, but also unanimous, "Major Grant." 

    Lena tilted her head to the side. "If you are sure, I am willing to sit with her." She declared, sure of herself.

     

     

    Alex asked for them to bring J'onn into the fold, as well. She sent him a message, for him and Cat Grant to be at her apartment first thing in the morning. Of course, she was still suspicious of Lena, and reluctant to leave her alone with Kara. Kelly proposed that she and Kara went to her apartment to sleep. And Lena to stay in Kara's with Alex. Kara said goodbye just with a look, not wanting to antagonize her sister, more than the necessary. It had been a long night.

     

     

    Cat and J'onn were punctual as ever. Both of them produced weapons to aim at Lena as soon as they saw her, weapons Kara had no idea they carried, Alex managed to defuse the situation. And thus, the explaining ordeal once again had begun, and Lena didn't change her story, it was clear to Kara that it wasn't rehearsed, she just hoped that it was enough to convince everyone in the room about Lena's intentions.

    Alex and Kara usually did the bad cop, good cop routine, and Alex never pulled any punches; but watching Cat interrogate Lena was nothing short of brutal. There were no moody lights, double mirrors or uncomfortable handcuffs. Still, the short blonde was a tour de force, burrowing into the skin of Lena's deepest insecurities. Kara hung her head in shame when stuff she had unearthed, before actually getting to know Lena or in her polygraph interrogation, gets brought up. 

    Cat mentioned Lena's biological mother, and she went stiff. It didn't get any better when Cat mentioned her relationship with Andrea Rojas and how she was now engaged, and 'way better without her'. But it was Cat bringing up Brainy that pushed Lena to stand up, like a tightly coiled spring.

    "Do not talk about him like you know him," She declared. "Attack me all you want, but leave Querl Dox out of this."

    "Seems to me like he's deep in this. You say he's the one hacking away." Cat commented, impassible. "He's the one having contact with other criminals, like Veronica Sinclair."

    Lena flushed a red color and mumbled, "It was a date. Brainy thought I— I needed to go out. After Andrea. It was… affecting our mission."

    "Mission?" Major Grant asked, clear mocking on her voice. "You mean your criminal activity?"

    "You may not approve of my methods, but I am the only reason The Heights episode has not been repeated anywhere else." Lena said, with certainty. "I am the reason Lex has not done another Star City Glades scenario, and why Cadmus DNA experiments have been halted."

    "Aren't you full of yourself? No one asked you to do anything in the first place. And even if all that were true," the Major made a pause, Kara had no doubts it was only for dramatic effect. "I suspect you probably just made everything worse."

    Lena took that like it was a physical blow, Kara saw her almost take a step back. Instead she clenched her jaw and glared at Cat Grant, in a way that she probably had never been glared like. "I had to do something, since going to the authorities was out of the question." Her voice was low, threatening even, her gaze focused on the shorter woman, defiantly. "I gambled my reputation, and my personal relationships because in the grand scheme of things, it was the right thing to do. And, at the time, it didn't really affect me, but who is to say that won't change in the future?" She stabbed her thumb on her own chest. "I am an immigrant too and not straight. But I'm white, technically rich, and have no connection with my heritage, but what about those who are not in the same position?" Lena kept going, impassionedly.

    "My ex-girlfriend is a Latina woman, but she's also rich and has blue eyes. She passes well enough but, what of those who don't?
