My best friend is also Latina, so is her daughter, born and raised here, one day that might not be enough. Brainy is Indian-Canadian, all his papers in order, he pays his taxes, and he is brilliant, but his skin color constantly puts him at the bottom of the barrel. And those are only people that I know." She looked around them. "What if five years from now Morgan Edge decides that Miss Olsen should not have her military rank because not only is she a woman of color, but also queer? Or, that you, sir," Lena pointed at J'onn with her hand. "Cannot be the boss of them because of your skin color. Or that Miss Nal cannot be part of Argo teams?" She took a deep breath, as if to center herself, Kara could tell it didn't work.

    "Lobbying it's not the issue, rich people will always do that, it's the lengths they are going to get their way. Poisoning innocent people, leveling an entire neighborhood, human experimentation disregarding the consequences. I had to do something to stop them."

    Silence reigned in the living room. Lena was breathy for all the rage she had been containing probably for at least two years. Almost all the eyes in the room were trained on Lena. Except Alex's, that were on Kara, who was struggling to not smile. Finally, the entire portrait of who Lena was, the one she got to know over almost two weeks, was similar to her criminal persona. Her reasons, while maybe misguided, were born out of her desire to do good.

    "Why did it have to be you?" Nia wondered, just loud enough for all of them to hear, because she'd become a fantastic agent. Kara felt proud of her apprentice's instincts and her pointed questions. But right now she was simply engulfed in Lena, and in how vindicated she felt about not being wrong about her character. 

    "Because my family, my brother, is the one orchestrating it. He's behind it all, I am the only one who could figure out their plan, or at least part of it." Lena replied, crossing her arms over her chest. "And because I was the one that could possibly get away with it."

     

     

    Cat didn't believe Lena right away, but she instilled enough doubt in the Major for her to order Argo One to conduct a covert investigation into the matter. Lena, staying in Kara's apartment, was then put on round-the-clock surveillance, as either Nia, Kelly, Alex, or J'onn were always with her. Kara was not allowed to be with her alone, instead she had the bulk of the investigation to prove that Lena was right.

    That's how Kara found herself accidentally crashing into Michael Matthews. And flirting with Michael Matthews, and somehow trying any excuse in the book to not have sex with the guy, who was very insistent. Especially in trying to bed someone he'd met less than 2-hours ago.

    Kara promised herself she wouldn't make fun of Alex and her dates with Max Lord anymore, because this guy was even more slimy than he was. But it paid off, when 3 days later, she found herself in a high end restaurant dining with him and his mother showed up out of the blue.

    "This is your conquest of the week, then?" Rhea Matthews, cut a striking figure, almost regal. She didn't wait for an invitation, she simply sat down in between them. Part of Kara was relieved that she didn't have to avoid Mike's wandering hand, trying to climb up her leg anymore, but another part of her was even more nervous about Rhea somehow figuring her out.

    "Mother." Kara almost laughed from the way Mike tensed when he registered his mother's presence. "This is Kate… Ka—"

    She intervened when it was clear he was still very much a frat boy, "Kara Kent, very nice to meet you." 

    "Pleasure." Rhea Matthews' face was obviously saying the total opposite. She turned to her son again, ignoring Kara completely once again, "I've been trying to contact you all day. I need you to free your morning tomorrow, we have a meeting with Mr. Luthor. Big news apparently."

    "Couldn't you leave a message with my secretary? I'm obviously busy." Michael said, trying to save face. His mother had other plans.

    "I would have, but it turns out you have not replaced the last one I had to fire, since she had so many unsavory things to say about us." Rhea retorted, looking at his son like her words should mean something different to him. "Besides, you tend to work better when I order you directly."

    Mike sighed and Kara feigned looking at the drinks menu again. Rhea stood up, taking her leave. "The pertinent information it's already in your Email. Please don't be late, I would hate to track you down again, I would not be as pleasant."

    Kara had the impression that a dentist appointment was more pleasant than Rhea Matthews on a good day. But she smiled, all dumb and blonde like Kelly recommended because he was superficial and just asked Mike about drinks, until he relaxed a bit more. She had to play her hand with patience, to get the information. His ego needed a win, which sadly for her, meant she needed to flirt.

    It took a few touches on his bicep, laughing as if he was the funniest man on the face of the earth, but by the end of their dessert, Kara had what she needed. 

    The next day, 1000 hours, LuthorCorp.

     

     

     

    It went to shit when Major Grant didn't approve of a recon mission on LuthorCorp, because apparently two companies having a meeting was not illegal; and fitted into place, when less than six hours after the meeting between Lex and the Matthews, National City Children's Hospital, went up all in flames. Kara wouldn’t have thought much of it, if it weren’t because right there, smiling at all the cameras, pledging money was Lex. And in the background both Rhea and Mike with hard-hats on their heads and blueprints in their hands.

    Less than a minute later her phone was ringing with Alex’s name on display.

    “I saw,” Kara mumbled as a greeting. “I’m convinced they planned it.”

    “You are not the only one who thinks that. Most Wanted is convinced it’s her fault. She’s freaking out,” Alex commented, and besides the new nickname she had granted Lena, Kara who had lived half a life with her sister, detected some worry in her words, as cynical as they were. 

    Kara tried not to gloat about it, and focused on the important details. “Why? She’s been with you this entire time. Or J’onn, or Nia.”

    “Martyr Complex, what do I know?”

    “You can just ask her, Alex.”

    “I still don’t trust anything she says,” Alex grumbled, and Kara could see her shrugging in her mind's eye.

    “Let me talk to her,” she whispered to her sister. “I do trust her.”

    “That’s the problem, Kara,” Alex sighed but still put her on speaker, something that surprised Kara. “She can hear you.”

    She hadn't seen or talked with Lena since the day after Endgame. It’d been almost a week. She cleared her throat. “Hey, Lena. Are you okay?”

    “It’s my fault. He’s punishing me.” Lena’s voice was a little more than a mumble, and Kara had to force herself to not leave the DEO and go back to her apartment, and comfort Lena.

    “Why do you think that?”

    “Because I gave the money to that Hospital,” Lena said, and she could tell she was not really paying attention to her. Looking at the news, perhaps.

    “What money?” Alex was the one that asked, and Lena let out a laugh that lacks humor.

    “Brainy and I hypothesized that you would arrest me, once I showed up at your fancy party,” Lena narrated. “We planned accordingly. We figured that we could hack one of Lex's accounts, one he was financing experimental weapons with. We thought the money could be used to do some good…"

    "Why didn't you tell me?" Kara asked, but kept to herself that she didn't think that would've changed anything. 

    "I didn't think about it, to be honest."

    "Well, no one got seriously hurt, the news said. That's all that matters. And I'm working on figuring out your brother. We will stop him, Lena."

    "Kara," Alex’s tone was a warning, 'do not commit to something you cannot deliver', it said.

    Instead, she doubled down, "I promise."

     

     

    Cat Grant simply wouldn't budge. 

    "You know, Kiera, if sometime you decide to use up that journalism degree, let me know, I can make some calls. You are incredibly annoying." She said, but did not approve of any mission to investigate Lex directly, and Kara started having suspicions. But the voice at the back of her head that sounded a lot like Alex's didn't let her entertain the idea for more than a few seconds. 

    In any case Kara couldn't wait anymore and she told her sister exactly that. Right in front of Lena, when she showed up at her own apartment for the first time in almost a week.

    "You cannot be serious, Kara, that's reckless," Alex practically hissed. "We have no evidence..."

    "Yes we do. You guys just don't trust it because it comes from Lena," Kara scoffed. "Alex, he set a children's hospital on fire, because Lena donated his money there. Do you really think that's something a good guy just does?"

    "We don't know if he did it. It can be a big coincidence."

    "You have never believed in coincidences, Alex. You have always told me to investigate, to follow the evidence. I want to keep digging but you and Cat won't let me."

    Lena was incredibly quiet and her wringing hands were the only evidence that she was even listening to them.

    Kara sighed, "I'm not asking for you or the team to go with me. Just do not tell Cat or J'onn about it."

    "No," Alex was adamant. "Argo One sticks together. You know this."

    "I want to go to LuthorCorp, then," Kara explained. "There has to be something incriminating on there, right?" She said. "We can find it and we will have all the evidence we need."

    "Not necessarily," Lena interrupt them. "He most likely won't have anything there, since I left. He knows I found all about it from the LuthorCorp servers." Lena took a moment to think, like debating with herself. "But I think I know where he will have all the evidence we need."

    "How convenient," Alex sarcastically let out.

    Kara completely ignored her sister and nodded, "That works for me just the same."

    "But I have to contact Brainy," Lena countered.

    "Consider it done," Kara immediately promised.

     

     

    Alex was not happy about it. She was very not happy, at all. 

    Lena gave them a phone number to call Brainy, with a message that made no sense to either of them. But Alex made the call, nonetheless, giving him a rendezvous location and a time. Not trusting either of them to not pull a fast one on them.

    Argo One jumped into Kara's blue Chevrolet, after briefing the rest of the team, and raiding Alex's apartment for any weapons she had stashed out. 

     

    "I don't like this," Alex kept mumbling, every few minutes, just for Kara, sitting shotgun next to her, to hear.

    Lena was sitting in the back, between Nia and Kelly in silence the entire trip to the rendezvous point.

    Kara waited for Lena and Nia to be out of the car, the youngest agent with the muzzle of her firearm on Lena’s ribs, while they were making contact with Brainy for whatever info he would provide Lena, to talk to Alex.

    "Listen, I know you don't trust her, but I do. I feel it in my gut that she's telling the truth," she looked at her sister, pleadingly. "If we prove she's right, we can do our job and this all goes away. We throw another bad guy behind bars and we keep living our lives." Kara turned to look at Lena's direction. "If she's wrong, or lying, you get to arrest her, and keep the credit. Plus, you get to tell me I told you so. I wouldn't hold it against you."

    "I don't think you understand how dangerous this half-cooked plan of yours is, Kara," Alex retorted, after a moment in silence. "Lex Luthor is one of the most powerful men in the country, regardless of what Most Wanted says. This is a non-sanctioned mission. We are rogue, if we go in, find nothing and Cat Grant finds out about it we can all kiss our careers goodbye," Alex kept looking at Lena, Nia and Brainy while talking. "At the very least. If she figures it out because Lex tells her we are gallivanting around with his sister, we will be court-martialed, because she would never tell anyone whatever Kool-Aid Lena told you to convince you she's telling the truth, even if she believed in it for a second."

    Silence was incredibly loud on the car. Kara understood her sister's apprehension, but she believed Lena. And she trusted her team. 

    "You really don't believe in anything she has told us? With all the things she has shown us? The evidence of stuff Lex has done?" Kara asked in a whisper.

    "I'm worried about you," Alex answered instead. "She has you wrapped around her finger and I don't know if I can trust her with my little sister's heart."

    Kara didn't say anything, Alex just kept looking ahead of them. 

    "She's not lying," Kelly offered, and Kara had forgotten that her sister-in-law was listening to them, but now she felt like hugging Kelly, in gratitude. "She wholeheartedly believes she's doing the right thing. And babe," she got closer to the front seat, and whispered. "I also believe her."

    “You cannot be serious,” Alex mumbled. Kara almost fist-bumped the air.

    “No, listen to me, babe.” Kelly pleaded. “I’ve been studying her brother, I went through all I could find; newspaper archives, I called his schools, I talked with some people who know him first-hand, besides Lena, I watched some of his interviews and his TED talks, and of course, talked with Lena herself. He hides it really well, but I think he’s an egomaniac and a narcissist at best.” Alex fully just turned around in her seat to look at her girlfriend like she had lost her mind. Kelly didn't move from the edge of the backseat, looking at Alex, convinced. “He's a sociopath at worst.”

    "You have never met the guy," Alex countered.

    "How many times have I been in the same room as any of our suspects, babe? You still trust my assessments," Kelly challenged. "You have a bias, we all have, because we have been on the heels of Lena for years. But now I have new information from her. She was a neglected child, she’s used to being in the shadow of her brother. Her accomplishments often got buried under and compared to Lex’s.” Kara silently agreed, and then looked at Alex. 

    They had similar problems growing up, except that Eliza and Jeremiah never really compared one to the other. But she knew that Alex struggled for a while, because sometimes Kara needed more from them, when she still had nightmares about the fire, and couldn’t deal with science at school. 

    Kelly kept talking, looking at Alex, “She has a strong sense of morality. You asked me if I thought she had some Martyr complex. I don’t. But she is a touch masochist, and she carries her own guilt around. Because she was adopted by a rich family and has, in her mind, unfair advantages because of it. That’s why she told us she was the only one that could get away with her crusade. Besides the fact that it’s her family behind it all, she feels guilty because she didn’t see it sooner, because she still loves her brother even with all his faults. Some part of her also feels shame because she’s not straight, we all know how that feels like, our families supported us, hers doesn’t. She still carries that shame, and maybe even as a response to PTSD.”

    Kara looked through the windshield at Lena. She couldn’t really imagine how different her own life could have been if Eliza, or her biological parents for that matter, were not supportive of her sexuality. Let alone if they were as vitriolic about it like Lena’s family, going to the lengths they are going to eradicate them, their lives.

    Lena abruptly hugged Brainy in the distance, and she could tell it surprised them both, and Nia. 

    Alex sighed, rubbing her face with her hand for a second. “I hope you’re both right. Because if she’s wrong, we will all end up behind bars.”

     

     

    Nia and Lena returned to the car, and by the look they gave around the almost claustrophobic space, they noted the tension in the air.

    “Okay, what did he give you?” Alex asked, her team leader mask back on.

    Nia answered first, with a crazy smile on her face, “Brainy gave us a tablet with all the info he– they had on Lex. Like it's only missing the last time he went to the bathroom.”

    Lena made a displeased face, “I wouldn’t go that far.” She confessed while she swiped, and typed on the screen of the tablet. “It’s all the info I have on him, as promised. It also has all of his known aliases, and locations of interest. His bank account numbers, some contacts, and so on.” She typed some more. “But for now the important part is this,” She showed them a blueprint. “This is his private lab, it's more of a bunker, really. He took me there once, before I went to college. I think he was trying to see if I thought the same way as he did. He was testing me.” She shrugged her shoulders. “He went to great lengths to hide it from the list of LuthorCorp’s properties. He told me he started all of his personal projects there. If that’s still true, I’m sure we can find something incriminating here.”

    It’s an underground floor on a farming property, Lena explained, In the outskirts of the city. And it's gigantic. “It’s under a barn, we used to vacation there when we were children. At the time, it was owned by a friend of our father.” Lena commented, “This was drawn from my memory, and it’s possible that the layout could have changed since. Brainy investigated it, and it’s now his property under one of his many aliases.” Lena freely gave the tablet to Alex, for her to examine. “I don’t know the security status, but Lex likes strategy games, I suppose there could be some kind of booby trap.” She shrugged again.

    Alex looked at the screen for a moment, and then looked at Kara, “What do you want to do? Do you want us to wait, or are we going guns blazing?”

    She took a moment to think, looking at the upside down screen too. “I know we’re ill-prepared for it, and that you guys are not sure this is the right move, but we are already breaking our orders. This guy torched a hospital because he’s greedy, I think we should go in, and find as much as we can to bring him down.”

    There were a few seconds of silence, and Kara waited, almost holding her breath.

    “Okay,” Nia said, nodding. “What’s the plan, then?”

     

     

    Alex drove them up to the farm, while they planned the raid. Kara was surprised when her sister told them that Lena would go into the property.

    "She's not trained," Kara protested immediately. "It makes more sense for her to stay with Kelly in the car, they can plan a getaway route, and she wouldn't be in danger."

    "She knows Lex the best," Alex responded." If we want to go in and out as fast as possible, she needs to guide us. Plus I need to keep an eye on her."

    Kara opened her mouth to disagree again, but Lena interrupted her, touching her shoulder. "Kara, she's right. I just told you he likes games, who knows what we could find in there," she shrugged. Kara looked at her with her mouth still agape. "The fastest we do this, the better."

    "She doesn't have gear," Kara weakly protested again. They had only managed to find two tactical vests. 

    "I'll be fine." Lena reassured her with a tiny smile, that did nothing to calm Kara down.

     

     

    Kara was not happy about it. But she went with the plan anyway, because going there was her idea in the first place. The entire property was deserted, no security whatsoever, except for a chain at the entrance. Kelly was the getaway driver, she stayed in the car half a mile away from the farm. Nia stayed in the property, but outside the barn doing surveillance. 

    Kara tried to give her vest to Lena, but she declined and Alex agreed. 

    "You are in more danger than her, she's protected by both of us. You keeping the vest protects her," her sister explained, but Kara wasn't as convinced. 

    Lena, Alex and herself managed to locate the entrance to the bunker with the help of Lena's tablet. Alex checked for any security measure at the entrance and then they moved in file formation, protecting Lena as much as possible, with Kara as the lead and Alex at the back, their weapons ready to respond to any fire.

    They had lost comms contact with Kelly, because of the distance and Nia's signal was already weak. Lena was quietly guiding her with the help of the blueprint. So far it was a very long hallway, just enough for two people to walk side by side. There were solid metal doors at both sides, every few feet's. It was an incredible feat to have constructed the massive bunker under a barn without anyone noticing.

    "Wait," Lena said, Kara stopped and without turning still aiming the gun ahead of her, pressed herself to the wall, and tried to make Lena do the same.

    "What is it?" Alex asked behind her.

    "This is not on the blueprint, this is new."

    "Maybe you forgot to draw it," Kara supplied.

    Lena scoffed, "I have a perfect memory, and you know it."

    "Or so you say." She mumbled with a tiny smile.

    "It doesn't matter," Alex piped in, cutting off their banter. "Where do you think he could have something incriminating?"

    Finally, Kara turned around to see the device with the blueprint as well. Lena zoomed in the screen and pointed at a room that would be a few feet ahead of their current position, except there was a wall now, and they could only go left or right.

    “He showed me this room, the only time he brought me here. It was like an office.” Lena whispered.

    “Would he have shown you the place as a bluff?” Alex asked.

    Lena thought about for a moment and half shrugged. “The office maybe, the bunker was a test, no doubt.”

    “Maybe the office could be a start point,” Kara offered. “You say he likes mind games, maybe he has a bunker inside a bunker.”

    “You are starting to get my brother. That’s exactly something he would do.”

     

     

    Lena tried to use the blueprint as a guide to find the office, and lead them (still staying behind Kara) into the mess that was the bunker. They entered a few rooms along the way, there were massive jars with weird objects floating in transparent fluids. There were plants that look like they were taken out of sci-fi books. There were machines taken apart, with motherboards and intense circuits lying around. The man clearly had many ideas. Finally, if by sheer luck or by Lena’s guide Kara had no clue, they found a wooden door. Alex went in first, their training taking over as she cleared the room first, Kara sticking next to Lena outside the office.

    From what Kara could see, the office looked incredibly out of place, compared to the rest of the place, full of mahogany, fountain pens, handwritten journals and old science books. 

    “It’s clear,” Alex finally said, and Kara immediately noted that she could only hear her sister because they were close enough to each other, not from her earpiece. She told Alex as soon as she entered the room with Lena.

    “Somehow, that’s a good sign,” Lena commented. “It means we are close to something Lex wanted to hide.” She went to the desk straightaway, looking for clues, Kara supposed.

    Alex walked near Kara, “Can I trust that you won’t do something dumb if I step out for a moment to make contact with Nia?” She whispered.

    Kara side-eyed her sister. “I won’t do anything dumb.”

    “You are going to give me early grays,” Alex mumbled and she stepped out of the room. 

    It’d barely been 7 seconds of Alex stepping out of the room when a metal slab just rolled down on the door. Kara tried to get to it and stop it, but she was too late. She could hear Lena swearing while she looked around the office.

    “Did you touch anything?” Kara asked, trying to find a way to open the thing.

    “Not to make that happen,” Lena bit back. “If he doesn’t know we are here, he will soon. The best we can do is find the evidence and go.”

    “Go where? We are trapped here,” Kara let out, pushing the metal slab that’s blocking the door. 

    “Kara,” Lena calmly whispered. “We will find another way out. This is one of his mind games. I told you. I bet there’s at least one other way out. Help me find it.”

    She looked at Lena and shook her head yes, they would get out of there. Kara got closer to Lena, “You know your brother the best, What would he do? What would be his play?”

    Lena took a moment, and approached the chess board, looking at it intensely. Then she moved one of the ornate legs of the raised board, like a lever. The bookcase opened as a door, creepily, like in a horror movie.

    “Why do I have the feeling that your family is very dramatic?”

    “Because we are,” Lena replied, without missing a beat and walked to the new opening.

    “Wait,” Kara called, and dropped to one knee.

    “A little early, don’t you think?” The brunette asked.

    Kara took out the extra revolver from her ankle holster and gave it to Lena. “What?”  She asked confused, standing up again.

    Lena tried to hold a laugh, but her double dimples gave her away. “Nothing. Let's go.”

    The agent let it go for now, to ask at a later time and went in past the bookcase first. It was another dark hallway, Kara could barely see past the muzzle of her firearm, for a moment, she was sure she could faintly hear Alex’s voice calling her name. She ignored it as the hallway lead them to a big room with a wall full of screens and another desk, more modern and made of steel. There were documents abound, and blueprints, and Kara recognized the map of National City at the top of them all.

    “Bingo,” Kara muttered, and grabbed a few documents to read. There were some experiment reports, something to do with Obsidian’s North virtual reality lenses.

    “He’s gonna use your ex’s company.” She told Lena.

    “What?” Lena asked, leaving the documents she was already looking to take the ones in Kara’s hand. She felt a tinge of jealousy at the worry the simple question carried, but she tried to focus on the mission. 

    She picked up the documents Lena had dropped. It was the drawing of a machine, a seismic device, she recognized. It was the prototype of the thing that basically leveled a part of Star City. 

    “He wants to use the lenses to… brainwash the population.”

    “Is it possible?”

    “Not at the moment, but Lex is brilliant. With enough time he could figure out a way,” Lena mumbled.

    Kara softly bumped her shoulder with Lena’s, “I think you are more brilliant than him. And you don’t want to use your brain for evil.”

    Lena gave her a tiny smile, and Kara was sure she was about to say something, when a loud clack interrupted her. 

    Kara stupidly lifted a finger to her lips, asking Lena to keep quiet, and raised her weapon, looking at the hallway from where the sound came. She slowly walked up to it, searching for danger. Thinking how Lena had said that Lex probably had another way to access the bunker.

    She found only darkness. The silence was eerie, the hairs at the back of her neck were standing up. She had a bad feeling about this. Even with all the evidence they'd found. She got back to the office they first found. It looked exactly as they left it. The metal door, still blocking the entrance.

     

    She retraced her steps, once she finished her search for something new, back to Lena in silence; still looking for the danger. Kara stopped at the end of the hallway and dropped to the floor, hoping she hadn’t been spotted; Lex was right in front of Lena.

    “Fuck!” Kara mumbled to herself.

     

    "You really are a thorn in my side, Ace." It's the first thing Kara heard Lex Luthor tell his sister. Unfazed by the gun she had pointed to his chest. "Please, put that down, we both know you're not ruthless enough to pull that trigger."

    Kara watched Lena actually put her finger on the trigger. "You are despicable, Lex. You set fire to a children's hospital just to get back at me. Children, Lex." 

    "Well, you took my money and were giving it away." Lex answered, not repentant at all. "Did you think I would not figure out it was you and the Indian boy? Please, Lena. You weren't even subtle."

    "Kara," Alex's voice cracked on the comm in her ear, after a low, almost electrical whirr. “Kara, can you hear me?”

    “Yes,” She desperately answered in a whisper to her earpiece, hoping her sister had a way to get to them.

    "She's somehow live streaming it. She needs to keep him talking, to make him confess." Alex announced, and Kara tried not to groan. How Lena managed to set a livestream in the minute and a half they got separated and Lena found herself in front of her brother, she had no idea. But she kept her head low and her position secret. “Copy.”  She mumbled to Alex.

    Lena's voice was a little watery. "You were funneling the Luthor foundation money to finance experimental weapons on Kaznia." Kara's head snapped at the mention of her birth country. "I'm still trying to figure out what's the play there." Lena confessed.

    "I really thought you'd learnt by now that you should be three steps ahead of everyone. But you continue to be a disappointment." Lex dramatically sighed. "Those weapons, while very real, would never make it to Kaznian hands. But I needed someplace to test them. I know someone who has a score to settle with them, after all." Lex's smiled, full of himself. 

    Kara suspected Lex was referring to Rhea, but there was no confirmation. 

    "Kaznia will think they have enough firepower to attack us, we will destroy them instead with those same weapons."

    "Mrs. Matthews," Lena supplied, her brother laughs.

    "So you did do your homework." Lex nodded. "She's been incredibly useful. Fantastic source of information. Perfect foil to blame all this on, if it gets out."

    "A double cross, then. Rhea tells you everything she knows about the shady things the Kaznian government has done, you offer your weapons to them, they buy them, you deliver defective arsenal, and when they attack the US military crushes them with the actual weapons you designed for Kaznia, double profit as well." Lena elaborated, painting the entire picture. "If the Kaznians figure you out, you blame it on Rhea. You'd do the same if the US government figures you were working with the enemy."

    "Not so bad, Sis." Lex praised, and for half a second Kara believed he was actually proud of Lena's deduction. "Except that I don't need to do that when I have the President on my side. He owes me, I put him in the White House. If the public figures it out, then I blame it on Rhea." Lex's eyes were both manic and arrogant. Kara couldn't see Lena's expression, but Lex reveled in it, he just couldn't help himself, he kept talking, as if he was lecturing his sister for her shortcomings. "Of course, the public won't find out anything, unless I want them to. And when the time comes, what they will know is that their own president colluded with the Kaznian government, using Rhea Matthews to harm our country.”

    “A triple cross, of course. It wouldn’t be you if there is not another level to this game you are playing.” Lena egged him on. 

    He lost his temper with that and got incredibly close to his sister, the weapon pointing at him having no effect whatsoever. “It’s not a game! We are being invaded by low-lifes.” He screamed at Lena’s face, irate. “And no one is willing to do what’s necessary because it’s not politically correct. I am willing, Ace. I will make this country magnificent, once again.”

    “Oh, God. You want to be President.” Lena scoffed. “You are planning on creating such turmoil and chaos, just to sweep in at the last second to play Hero.”

    “I am a hero, Ace. A Nobel Peace Prize nominee, even.” He responded, cockily.

    “You are insane, Lex.” Lena accused him. “All the people you have hurt and killed, just to sit in The White House?”

    Kara was on the hypothetical edge of her seat, Lena cornered him, baited him enough for a confession. If he answered, she'd get him. A full confession, enough to arrest him and build a case against Lex Luthor.

    He walked back from Lena, a smile on his face, “You know I don’t accept mediocrity, Lena, let alone scumbags. Everyone who died deserved it.”

    “They were innocent people, Lex. People with an incredible future ahead of them, you killed children.”

    “They were nobodies. Scoundrels, degenerates, thugs and thieves, Lena. Our country doesn’t need them.”

    Lena kept silent for a moment, as the siblings seized each other.

    Alex’s voice cracked again in Kara’s ear, “I think that’s enough, I got the go ahead to capture him. Be careful, Kara.”

    “Roger,” She mumbled, but maintained her position for the moment, not wanting Lex to attack Lena without having a plan.

    “You're incredibly full of shit, Lex,” Kara heard Lena mumble. “You are despicable, and a bigot.”

    Kara started moving, closing in on the siblings, as quiet as possible, her aim directed at Lex’s body.

    “Spare me the sanctimonious lecture, Sis.” He calmly and elegantly sat at his desk. “Your alliances are dubious at best, since you are one of the deviates.”

    “Perhaps,” Lena smiled, and Kara finally had a clear look at her face. “But at least I don’t monologue so much I incriminate myself.” She pointed at a high point on a pillar, a good 15-feet from them, at a minuscule, blinking, red light. “Say hello to the camera, Lex.”

     

    It took a moment for Lex to catch up, but when he did, it was like a bull who had seen red. He grabbed something from under the desk and Kara just needed a fraction of a second to realize it was a small gun. 

    Kara didn't think, her training was the last thing on her mind, she simply acted.

    When Lex shot directly at his sister, screaming in rage, Kara was already in front of Lena, hugging her to her body.

    He shots three times, two bullets lodged themselves in Kara’s back. The air was expelled from her lungs by the two hits. It felt like being hit by a hammer twice, and the shots were still ringing in her ears, as both Lena and herself fell to the ground.

    She faintly heard Lena calling her name, cold hands on her face for a second and then they were gone. By the time she felt another hit on her back, Lena had taken the 9mm from her own hands. 

     

    Maybe she shot the gun, maybe she didn’t. Kara was not really sure what’d happened, she couldn't breathe, and her entire upper body hurt.

     

    She was uncertain if she'd passed out, or if she just managed to breathe a little better after the initial pain subsided. But now she can hear Lena, clearly. She tries to look at her.

    “Hey, hey, don’t move, okay?” Lena said, her voice a little watery. “Your sister is already on her way with some EMTs. She told me to not let you move. She’s looking for a way in.”

    Kara realized she didn't have her earpiece in anymore. “Your brother?” She asked faintly, trying not to move as Lena (and Alex said). She can breathe better now, but she feels pain every time. Something was definitely wrong, but she tried to keep calm. 

    Lena didn't respond. To Kara that was answer enough.

    “It was self-defense.” She tried to soothe the brunette. “He was going to kill you.”

    Lena was quiet, and then Alex arrived with the EMTs and other dozen of agents, and Kara had to let herself be taken away from Lena.

     

    The DEO medics examine her on site. They suspected broken ribs. The extent of it was still to be determined. They moved her to a covert ambulance and then to headquarters. None of her team accompanied her, it didn't bode well.

     

    Doctor Hamilton, DEO's Chief Doctor, did all of her exams herself after taking off her now extremely useless vest. Examined her torso, and every little movement resulted in pain. Cat Grant arrived just as Doctor Hamilton was telling her she would do a CT Scan to confirm the possible fractures, Kara only needed to look once at Major Grant to realize she was gonna get the chewing off of her entire life, however the woman abstained herself. At least for the moment.

    “How is she?” She asked Doctor Hamilton.

    “I will do a CT scan next, but she seems fine. She has some bruising, but she’s responsive and her lungs sound fine.” Hamilton recite. 

    “Can the CT wait?” Cat Grant questioned.

    “I prefer we do it immediately, so we can manage her pain adequately.”

    Kara could see Cat was not happy with that answer. She left after nodding her head at the doctor. 

    Kara wanted to ask about Lena, and her team, or about the bunker. Live Streaming a confession, and Lex dying wasn’t something she had thought about. She was so worried and yet the pain doesn’t really let her think about anything else for the duration of the scan.

     

    It was around 45 minutes later when Major Grant returned, this time with J’onn and a very pale looking Alex.

    “I’m fine,” Kara forced herself to tell her sister. “I promise, I’m okay. Right, Doc?”

    Doctor Hamilton was already looking at the results on her tablet. Then she smiled at Kara. “Overall, yes. You only have one broken rib, without separation. There are some hairline fractures on two other ribs but everything else looks good. It's like a miracle. Seems like you are really living up to your moniker, Supergirl.”

    She injected some pain medication, explained that she would need to do some breathing exercises to prevent complications, and prescribed some over the counter medication. Kara stopped truly paying attention when, whatever the hell Hamilton gave her started having effect, and she wasn't in so much pain anymore.

    She reacted when she felt Alex presence in front of her, defending her fiercely as she had always done.

    “She was following my orders, Argo One will always stand together.” Alex said to Cat and Jonn. “Lex Luthor confessed to his crimes, we have him on record, I don’t understand what’s the problem.”

    Kara knew Alex was lying to herself. The entire thing was a complete mess, especially now with Lex dead.

    “You four went on an unsanctioned mission, with two known criminals, one of whom killed one of the most prestigious men in the continental US, no, the world. Do you not think that’s complicated, Agent Danvers?” Major Grant hissed. 

    Alex maintained her silence, long enough for Kara to realize what the Major had said.

    “Two known criminals?”

    “Querl Dox followed us,” Alex explained. “He’s the reason I could even make contact with your comm.”

    “I think it’s best that we have this conversation outside Headquarters,” J’onn interrupted. “You go home, we have to deal with the aftermath of that recording. Alex, no information to anyone but Major Grant and I. Understood?”

    “Yes, sir.” Alex turned to Cat. “Ma’am.”

    As soon as they left the room, Kara took Alex’s hand. “Where is Lena? Is the team okay? Is she okay?”

    “She’s with Kelly and Nia. They are all okay, no one but you got hurt.” Alex mumbled while she helped Kara dress herself. “I’ll explain when we get home.”

     

     

    Instead of being driven like they usually were, Alex took the keys of one of the black vans and drove herself. She knew Alex use driving as a mindless task, while she's thinking. Kara was still in pain, but it was more manageable. She wanted to ask Alex about so much stuff. About the bunker, about Lex, and how on earth Brainy knew about them going to the farm. Instead she waited to get to her apartment.

    Once they arrived at their building, they moved slowly, Alex close to her, telling her to take it easy. The next six weeks in recovery already felt like a nightmare.

    She was surprised when she opened her door and Lena was there, with Brainy. Nia and Kelly too.

    In the blink of an eye, Lena was hugging her. Kara groaned in pain, but hugged Lena back anyways, and kept her close when she tried to retract.

    “Easy there,” She heard Alex say. Lena grabbed Kara’s face looking at her eyes.

    “That was very dumb, you know?” She whispered, and Kara smiled.

    “Well, you are the genius out of the two.” Kara looked at Lena’s eyes. “I couldn’t let him kill you.” She murmured.

    Alex faked a cough, that made Lena recoil a bit, taking a step back from her.

    “She has a broken rib,” her sister announced. “J’onn and Cat are pissed, and they will drop in sometime soon.” She turned to Kara again. “Sit, and rest, let me know if you have any pain flare up, or if you have problems breathing.”

    Kara obeyed. She figured she had given enough grays to her older sister for the day. “I’m glad you’re all okay,” she said to the team as she plopped on the couch with a slight wince. She grabbed Lena’s hand when she sat next to her, smiling when the brunette looked at her with surprise. “But I’m gonna need someone to explain exactly what the hell happened?” She finished, looking at Querl in particular. 

    “I put a tracker on the tablet,” he supplied, like that was enough of an answer. “I wanted to make sure Lena was okay.”

    “I’d told you I was okay,” Lena answered, a touch exasperated. In a way that felt unbelievably familiar to Kara. It was like when she teased Alex.

    “You say you are fine even when you are not,” Brainy said, matter of fact. “I followed the signal to the farm. When I arrived Miss Nal told me she did not have clear communication with you.”

    “I almost shot him thinking it was Lex’s security,” Nia added.

    “I was setting up a remote workstation when Miss Danvers came back from the bunker,” he continued absolutely stoic. “She almost fired too, but she was desperate enough to accept my help.”

    “I couldn’t get a signal to Nia and when I tried to go back in there was a piece of metal blocking the door.” Alex further explained. “I was coming for Nia and Kelly to try and find another point of entry to get to you.”

    “I accessed the tablet you still had with you, to use the location of it to know where in the building you were,” Brainy recounted. “That’s when I detected that you had installed the camera and started the recording.”

    “Why did you install the camera? When did you even get that?” Alex asked Lena. Kara couldn’t tell if she was still suspicious of her or not.

    Lena sighed. “Before I met you, we had planned on infiltrating the farm and plant cameras. They were discreet in size, but the battery life was too short, and they still had the recording light, it was not sustainable for long recordings. When I got the tablet I also got the camera.” She explained. “When we heard that noise, I guessed it was my brother. I had nothing to lose, figured that with me here he would be distracted and I could make him babble enough to incriminate himself and record it.”

    “As I was connected to the tablet, we could all see and hear Lex,” Brainy continued his tale. “Miss Danvers asked if I could amplify the signal of your communication devices, and I did. Then Miss Nal had the fantastic idea of sending the live feed to your bosses.”

    “J’onn called me immediately. The Major was pissed off. I told her she wasn’t letting us do our job,” Alex explained, and Kara felt more appreciation for her sister than ever before. Which was saying something because she loved Alex with all her being. “You were right, Kara. Since we’ve been in this job, I’ve told you to follow your gut. It has always worked out, including this time.”

    “Major Grant had seemingly been selecting a team she could trust, she had just informed us she was sending it our way when he shot you,” Kelly joined the conversation when Alex couldn’t continue. “We called the medical team, Lena defended you when he was closing in on you.”

    “We have tons of evidence against all of them. Including Lillian Luthor, Sam Lane and the President.” Nia told her. “It will be a lot of paperwork, and it will require a lot of planning but we got them.”

    The silence creeped in her living room, Kara remembered the sleepless hours of just the past week, and looked at Lena. She’d spent the last two years going after her brother.

    “What will happen with you two now?” Kara asked her. “You and Brainy?”

    Lena shrugged, “I’ll take the blame and plead for Brainy to have a deal. I can pay for a good team of lawyers.”

    Kara was not particularly happy with that answer, and by the look of it neither was Brainy, but she didn't really have a better alternative at the moment.

     

     

    She wasn't sure when she fell asleep, but she woke up because of the screaming of Alex, Lena and Cat Grant. Her already cramped apartment was now claustrophobic with the addition of the Major and J'onn. 

    She tried to sit down, and winced in pain. The effects of the anesthetic, were already wearing off. Lena noticed her and went to help, Alex and Cat stopped fighting for the time being.

    "What's going on? Why all the yelling?" She asked.

    "Cat is on a rampage," Lena mumbled. "She didn't like that you guys didn't inform her about storming the bunker."

    "You went against my orders," Major Grant insisted.

    "With all due respect, ma'am," Kara started. "You were never going to let us surveil Lex Luthor. He was a fish too big. We saw an opportunity and went for it. It didn't pan out as we would've liked, but we got the evidence."

    There was certain satisfaction in seeing Cat Grant be speechless for something she said. However, she didn't want to be on the woman's bad side, if she wanted her help in getting Lena and Brainy pardoned.

    "Do we have a plan on how to proceed?" She asked, looking at her bosses.

    Silence reared its head again on her living room for almost a minute, and then it was J'onn who talked. "We need to construct very solid cases against the President, Sam Lane, Lex and Lillian Luthor, as soon as possible. Especially with Mr. Luthor's death. We must deliver it to the House so they can prepare the impeachment of the president."

    "Our biggest concern right now is," Alex said after looking at J'onn and Ms. Grant for a second. "People will doubt everything, because we got the information from Lena."

    "We got Lex confessing on tape," Kara protested. "What else would everyone need?"

    "Everyone thinks Lena is the criminal, it's her word against his," Kelly supplied. 

    "I was there, I can be a witness," Kara offered, Lena tensed up next to her.

    They all looked at each other without a word.

    "That would end your career, Kara." J'onn explained. "Your identity would be exposed."

    Kara looked at her sister. She could not decipher Alex's expression, so she looked at Lena, who avoided her eyes. The room being silent when there were 8 people in it felt unnatural. Kara finally settled her sight on Ms. Grant. "Is it too late to accept that offer to call your journalist friends?" She asked, half serious, half in jest.

    Unexpectedly, Lena started cackling up.

    "Oh my god, 'Journalist friends'? That's what you call it now?"

    "I didn't say friends,” Their boss objected. ”And as you must know, I obviously had to give up Catco when I took the job at the DEO."

    “Why did you do that?” Nia asked.

    Major Grant rolled her eyes. “Honestly, Nora, I was bored. Olivia was the president and she wanted me to be the White House Press Secretary. When she decided she didn’t want to run for a second term, I was once again bored of it all. I told her about this idea, of an all female spy team. She gave me the Honorary Rank. It’s been fun making the old guard address me with a full military rank, sometimes higher than theirs.”

    “Was the SATs nonsense part of the boredom too?” Kara couldn't help, but ask.

    Nia, Kelly and Alex’s heads all whipped at the same time to look at her.

    “What?” Kelly questioned.

    Cat Grant laughed, unrepentant. “Not entirely. Believe it or not it wasn't easy for us to build up the Argo Program at the DEO. The mystery helped it. The urban legend that the government spies on us to that degree, helps sell the idea to recruit spies.”

    “So our entire careers have been built on a lie?” Alex asked.

    “The SATs results may have been a lie, Agent Danvers. But you were recruited because you are brilliant, have a medical degree, and your father was already a legend at the DEO.” Cat Grant firmly replied. “Argo One, it’s still the best of the teams. You four were carefully selected for it. Do no mistake the mysticism and legend of the creation of the program with unworthiness. We’ve done an excellent job doing what we set out to do. Let people, men underestimate us, so we can extract the information we need for national security.”

    It’s enough to shut them all up. The silence was a bit tense, until Brainy cleared his throat.

    “Would it be better if Lena, as a source, was more credible?” 

    “Of course it would, Mr. Dox,” J’onn replied.

    “But that ship has sailed.” Miss Grant added.

    Brainy frowned and seemed like he wanted to say something else, when Lena interrupted him. “She means that the opportunity for that is already gone, because the public has a perception of me as a criminal.” She explained.

    “Hm.” He took a moment, and tilted his head slightly. “Understood. I think I have an idea, nonetheless.”

    “What is it?” Nia inquired.

    “Given the previous conversation, Is it safe to assume that everyone in this room is willing to lie about something to accomplish the greater good?” 

    Everyone seemed to be looking at each other, pondering Brainy’s question.

    Lena chuckled next to Kara. “Well, obviously I am,” She said. “So are you, and if they are not, they may want to reconsider their line of work.”

    “Depends on how big the lie is, Mr. Dox,” Cat Grant replied. “There are some lines that must not be crossed.”

    “Would it be preferable that Lena had approached you with the information at the time when we found it in the LuthorCorp servers?”

    “Yes, that’s what she should’ve done.”

    “If she’d done that, and considering we didn’t know who to trust, would it have been plausible that you could have sent Lena undercover?”

    Miss Grant furrowed her brows, Kara tilted her head, confused.

    J’onn laughed, “I see what you’re proposing, Mr. Dox.”

    “Enlighten us then, Colonel,” Miss Grant encouraged him to talk.

    “He’s suggesting we say that Miss Luthor approached us with the information, and that thinking her involvement had already been compromised, the DEO would suggest that they go clandestine.” 

    “Could it work?” Miss Grant asked.

    “Our story would need to be airtight,” J’onn supplied.

    The room became still, for a while. No one said anything.

    “Is someone here not willing to do it?” Cat Grant finally asked.

    No one gave an answer. 

    “Very well, then we must get our account of it perfected.” J’onn declared.

    “I’ll make some coffee,” Kelly offered, and just like that, they sat to craft one big lie to protect the innocent.

     

     

    It was so late, that it was actually early. The sky was already slightly pink, the sun peeking at the horizon. No one really slept, they ironed out their story, two years worth of it, Lena’s almost flawless memory came in handy. The hardest part to come up with an explanation for was actually her kidnapping, but the story was credible enough.

    Cat had left with J'onn 15 minutes ago, the rest were just half asleep in whatever space they had been sitting. Kara was completely awake, either for the adrenaline of it all or because of the accidental nap she took earlier. She stepped out onto the balcony, just to breathe air that wasn't from her over stuffed apartment. 

    She took a deep breath, and the pain was still there, but it wasn't terrible. She wished she could go to the gym, she felt like she had too much energy and nowhere to put it. Yet she felt entirely worn-out.

    "Are you all right?" Lena's voice was barely a whisper. "Are you in pain?"

    Kara turned around and smiled at the brunette. "I'm okay, a bit sore but it'll pass. Are you all right?"

    Lena didn't answer right away, instead looking at the sky. Kara let her think it through, looking at her in silence. 

    "There was a time when Lex was my everything, you know?" She questioned softly. Kara nodded. "He was attentive and a good brother. At least for a while, he was. Sometimes I still think of him as my loving brother, but I think that he and I would always end up like that. One killing the other." Lena stood next to Kara and her pinky touched hers on the balcony's rail. "I'm thankful you saved me. But part of me is honestly baffled and incredibly angry at the way you did it."

    Kara laughed, but stopped immediately because her chest started to hurt, because of it, "I think you are allowed to be both." She grabbed Lena's pinky with hers. "It wasn't my best save, to be honest."

    "I'm just glad you are okay. It could have been so much worse."

    "Yeah," she smiled, her eyes stuck on Lena's face. "You know how you could thank me?"

    Lena turned to her and lifted an impressively sharp eyebrow, both in question and defiance.

    "I have heard that a kiss can do wonders."

    "Oh, really?"

    "Pretty sure I read it in a magazine or something," she smiled.

    Lena laughed, and Kara definitely forgot about the pain for a moment.

    "I cannot believe I like someone so goddamn cheesy," Lena declared in a whisper.

    "Is that a no?"

    Lena didn't answer, she simply got closer and kissed Kara on the lips. Her lips were still as soft as Kara remembered them. It was a quick kiss, innocent even. But oh so sweet. 

    "Never," Lena confessed, into her lips. Distancing just enough to look at her eyes. Kara was a little hypnotized by the blue and green in them. "You, Kara Danvers, are my hero. And I'd kiss you without you having to take a bullet for me."

    Kara said nothing, she just rested her forehead on Lena's. And then kissed her once again, just because she could.

    Chapter End Notes

    TW: There are some racist and homophobic undertones from one character in particular, be mindful of that. Also, politics, because comics.

    Hope you liked the chapter. The final one (what I'm calling an epilogue), shall be posted next month.

    Comments help feed the muses, if you feel like doing that.
    You can follow me on twitter and/or tumblr.

    Epilogue: Endgame

    Chapter Notes

    Don't you love when 'January' extends for another two months?
    Anyways, this it is, the final stretch. Thank you for accompanying me and our girls in this journey.
    Thank you to NF for reading over this chapter and being my cheerleader the entire time, along with Clark.
    Enjoy!

    The six weeks after she was shot were almost unbearable. She wasn’t used to being cooped up in her apartment with nothing to do. She couldn’t work out, she was in pain, looking for stuff to eat in the cabinets was excruciating, and sometimes even walking from the bed to the living room left her gasping for air. 

    Alex was somehow both not home enough, going over all the documentation of the case, and being extremely overprotective of her, that sometimes Kara felt she couldn’t breathe because of it, either.

    Kara used her time alone to really think about what she wanted to do with her life. In the middle of her breathing exercises, her mind traveled to an uncertain future, and all its possibilities. Could she stay in the DEO when everything has now been tainted by the actions of a few?  The president had been complicit in trying to incarcerate an innocent woman. A woman who discovered that the conspirators' motivations were based on bigotry and prejudice. A woman that tried and did her best to stop her own family, because that was the right thing to do. Disregarding her own security and reputation. Could she have done the same? Could she have gone against Alex, for example, if it was the right thing to do? She wanted to think that she would.

    If she left the DEO, what would she do, exactly? She left Midvale to go to college, and when she finished that, she started as an Argo recruit immediately. She doesn't even remember how to write-up a draft anymore. Her resume would definitely be lacking, since the DEO bit would have to be redacted. 

    One thing was for damn sure, though. She had to end whatever relationship she had with James, whether it was a sham or not. Because,  in all honesty, Kara liked Lena a lot. A bit too much perhaps, for someone she'd met two months earlier. But she made Kara feel like no one else had made her feel before. There was a silent understanding between them, Kara felt like she could communicate with Lena only with a look. They get each other, on an unprecedented deep level. But could she trust her? She ruminated on Alex's words before they went into the bunker, those first weeks of her recovery: ‘She has you wrapped around her finger, and I don't know if I can trust her with my little sister's heart’. But Alex didn't get to know Lena like she did. Not Lena intimately, not just her body but her soul and mind. Unfiltered and maskless. Just Lena.

    Alex didn’t know that Lena is incredibly shy, but her sure and commanding persona has been drilled into her since childhood, and has now become an armor. That paired up with lipstick and 6-inches heels, could be lethal in a meeting, convincing and mesmerizing at the same time. 

    She doesn't know that, when Lena smiles and laughs wholeheartedly, her double dimples show up. Or how she really loves burgers and chocolate milkshakes, but she feels obligated to order green salads and water instead, because her mother always commented on her body and weight. How she moves her hands animatedly, whenever she’s passionate about the topic she’s talking about, and that she wrings at them when she’s nervous. 

    Alex doesn’t know that Lena’s something of a rebel, and can be very petty if she wants to be, but that she would never let that pettiness be at fault for someone getting hurt. That Lena protects those she cares about, no matter the consequences to her own person. That her mind is always thinking of ways to optimize something, to create something to make life better, easier. That Lena was competitive, and sometimes even cocky. She was brilliant and good, but she’s used to living under her brother’s shadow, and that she doubts herself constantly because of it.

    Alex didn’t know Lena. Not like Kara.

    Because even when Lena was guarded and skittish, Kara figured her out, and she wanted to know her more. Kara wanted to know everything there was to know about Lena.

    She decided right then, that she would do just that.

     

     

    Two weeks into her recovery, Kara called James to invite him to her apartment, he declined and said he knew what she was going to say. 

    “I know it's over between us. I was holding onto you,” he said on the phone. “I do care about you, but it’s clear that I was just somehow guilty about how I left things with Lucy, and then I started dating you immediately, it was not right.” James made a small pause, “and now you’re clearly ready to move on.”

    Kara was a bit surprised by his words, so much that without even thinking about it she blurted out; “Have you been talking with your sister, who happens to be a psychologist?”

    James laughed, loud, and the Kara from two years ago would’ve been smitten by the sight, no doubt. “Perhaps,” he coyly replied. “Be careful, please. That Lena Luthor incident was really dangerous.”

    Kara had to bite her tongue, because the world was still under the impression that Lena was evil. “Don’t worry.” She settled on saying.

    “I asked to be transferred,” James mumbled. “I think it is time for me to leave National City.”

    “I’m gonna miss seeing you around,” she admitted after a few seconds of silence.

    “I have a feeling we will still be in each other's lives,” he laughed. And this time, their relationship ended for good.

     

     

    Seven weeks after being shot, and after passing the physical exam, Kara returned to work full time at the DEO. Argo One had been the one secretly making sure the case against Lex and his co-conspirators was as air tight as possible. No one other than them  handled evidence, until they had no other choice and had to pass the case along. They turned every hypothetical rock over for evidence, with the help of Brainy and Lena. Who painstakingly went over every document, report and experiment they found on Lex's bunker. 

    After her recovery, Kara was allowed to be with them while they worked in the bunker as they were finishing turning that place upside down. She could not take her eyes off of Lena. It was incredible watching her, determined and focused, yet she still sent her such flirtatious looks at her, that Kara had a really tough time keeping her hands to herself. And her mind out of the gutter.

    But Lena was completely dedicated to the task of catching everyone involved with her brother’s plans, whether they were aware of it or not. 

    It was one of those days in the bunker when Lena told her about her father’s involvement with the original Medusa project, back in Kaznia.

    “He was the lead scientist, Kara,” Lena told her. “That's how he knew he had to get out of Kandor.”

    “But why would he create something like that?” Kara mind-numbingly asked.

    Lena took her hand and clutched it in support. “I think only he could answer that question. But I figured I should tell you, because you deserved to know. And I would like you to tell me if it was the other way around.” She sighed and bit her lip. “I asked your sister if I should let you know, she wasn’t so sure if you’d like to.”

    Kara had to think for a moment. 

    When she was a kid, even just a few years earlier, the news would have destroyed her and the memories of her parents. Now, she thinks that she simply doesn’t know enough. Her parents were humans, they were allowed to make mistakes, she has certainly made a lot. And knowing what she knows about the Kaznian government there was a high probability that he was coerced into cooperation. 

    Now, as an adult, she was willing to accept that her parents were not perfect.

    “Thank you for telling me,” she told Lena.

    “Of course,” Lena smiled and went back to reading another experiment report. Kara kept looking at her, entirely captivated by her beauty.

     

     

    A few days later, Kara finally asked her out… or something like that.

    “Do you think Alex and Kelly would like to go to Oblivion, and play pool against us?” She asked Lena.

    “Do you really think your cop sister would like to go to an underground, full of criminals, bar?” Lena laughed.

    Kara smiled and shrugged. “Maybe if she’s promised very good scotch,” she got closer to the brunette, for no reason at all, other than the feeling that she's being pulled by her, as if she was a magnet. “And don’t call her ‘cop', she prefers ‘agent’. Cop reminds her of her ex.”

    “Noted,” Lena chuckled. “You can invite her, though. There is no harm in trying. Just let me know.”

     

    Kara made sure to ask Alex about it with Kelly present, since she was unable to say no to her girlfriend.

    “It will be fun,” Kelly commented, after Alex negative grumbles. “Also, maybe knowing a place like that could come in handy in the future.”

    Kara smiled when her sister accepted the invitation, even if she was still grumbling.

    Lena extended the invitation to Brainy and Nia, and a few days later all six of them found themselves back at Oblivion. Alex, Kelly, Lena and herself around a pool table, while Nia and Brainy were busy in a world of their own, at a foosball table. 

    Lena had somehow woken up Alex’s competitive streak and they had both forgotten about Kelly and Kara. By the third game, for the win, Alex was teasing Lena and Kara realized that everything would be okay, that her sister just needed to know Lena better to accept her. 

    “I’m gonna marry your sister,” Kelly told her, making her lose her train of thought.

    “Duh,” Kara let out. “I know you guys will be ‘ happily ever after’, and all that.”

    “Not what I meant.”

    Kara looked at Kelly who was looking at her sister as if she’d hung the stars, and that tipped Kara over. “Oh! Kelly, that's great!”

    “I’m… not asking permission, because that’s not who we are, but announcing my intentions. I suppose…”

    “Kelly, you are part of our family already, I’m stoked  for you guys to make it official.” She told her sister-in-law. “I would hug you, but that would probably alert Alex that something is happening.”

    “Yeah, probably,” Kelly laughed.

    Kara smiled, incredibly happy for her sister.

    “You know, after all this is cleared up,” Kelly kept her gaze on their pool partners. “You better invite that girl out. I like her, and I like how giddy she makes you.”

    Kara was sure her face was beet red,so she busied herself by drinking a gulp of her beer. “I will. I like her too.”

    “I can tell,” Kelly grinned.

    She could feel her cheeks burning up, instead of indulging Kelly into seeing her blush to extinction, she asked; “hey, do you guys want another round?”

     

     

    The day after their outing to Oblivion, Alex showed up at her apartment after work, with a six pack of her favorite craft beer. 

    “Shouldn’t you be with your girlfriend?” Kara asked her, confused by the presence of her older sister, but letting her in anyway.

    “She’s helping Jimmy pack up his apartment.” Alex bylined for the kitchen, picked up two beer bottles and left the rest in the fridge. “And we need to talk.”

    Kara walked up to her couch and sat down, “About what?”

    “I still don’t know if I should trust her with your heart,” Alex went directly to the heart of the matter, offering her a bottle after opening it. “But I understand why you like her.”

    Kara smiled, taking the beer and drinking a swig of it, "I knew you would like her, she's great," Kara whispered. "And I feel that you would’ve been good friends if you had met in different circumstances.”

    Alex shrugged. “Maybe. I am willing to give her the benefit of the doubt with my baby sister, but she only gets one shot.”

    “You don’t really have a say in it, Alex” Kara commented, bumping her shoulder with her sister’s.

    “I know. But I’d like to think that you would at least listen to your big sister from time to time.”

    “From time to time,” Kara conceded, gently hitting Alex’s beer bottle with hers.

    Her sister nodded, and then silence swallowed them. Kara finally noticed that Alex was nervous about something. 

    “What’s actually on your mind?” Kara gently asked, after letting some minutes pass by. “You are taking too much time finishing that beer.”

    Alex rolled her eyes, finally finishing her beer, just to spite Kara.

    “Do you remember… What feels like a lifetime ago, before you went frolicking around with Lena, that I told you Kelly was applying for other jobs and that J’onn offered me his when he retires?” Alex asked.

    “Yeah, of course I remember. You have plans.”

    “We do, now that stuff has calmed down, and after the grief you put me through,” Alex commented, half in jest, half very seriously. “I– I wouldn’t have survived that without Kelly. She was my rock. Made me realize, I don’t wanna be without her.” Alex bit her lip, as if she was nervous about something. “When Jimmy told us he was leaving National City I just blurted it out. I told him I was marrying his sister soon and that he better make it to the wedding.”

    “Alex! That’s great!” Kara smiled, and kept to herself the absolute elation she feels about her sister finding a person so attuned to her that also decides to propose around the same time as her.

    “I know. I already ordered the ring. Which is the second reason I’m here.”

    “What do you mean?” Kara frowned.

    “I put your address for the delivery. I can’t have Kelly finding out that ring by accident. I already know when and where I’m going to propose. I just need to wait.”

    Kara laughed. “Yeah, of course. I’ll keep it safe for you.” She smiled, sharing the moment with her sister, and didn't tell a soul about that conversation, or about Kelly and Alex deciding to propose to each other the same week. 



    A couple of days later, Lillian Luthor was arrested, along with anyone who they could prove was involved with Lex. In the subsequent weeks, the President was officially impeached, and arrested too. Cat Grant made a big announcement about the operation, and named Lena and Querl as the key witnesses and collaborative partners on the investigation, just as according to plan. Cleaning up both their names, at least for the foreseeable future. 

    The day after Cat’s press conference Kara made good on her promise and asked Lena out.

    “No secret escapades to Lovers Lane , I promise,” she said in a whisper, as they were both at Argo One’s deck at the DEO.

    Lena smiled at her, “I don’t mind, actually. I’m really fond of that place.”

    “Yeah, me too.” Kara replied in a whisper, the smile seared into her face. “But we should do something else, celebrate your freedom.”

    Lena started fidgeting with her hand as soon as Kara said that. “Kara I can’t celebrate a lie,” she said in a mumble, after making sure they were truly alone.

    “It’s not a lie… Don’t think about it like that.” Kara took Lena’s hand, interlocking their fingers. “It's what you would have done if anyone had given you the chance. If you could have been sure no one would betray you.”

    “But that’s not what happened, isn’t it?”

    Kara stayed quiet for a moment, “It’s what happened now, for everyone else. It’s better… safer like that.” She said quietly.

    “People say that if you tell a lie enough, you might start to believe it yourself.”  Lena mumbled after a while, and laughed a bit sardonically. “I guess I will find out soon. Still, it doesn't make it right.”

    Just like that Kara learned something new about Lena. Despite everything she had done, she valued honesty.

    “Okay,” she conceded. “What if we celebrate that we stopped very dangerous people from harming innocent ones? That’s always worth celebrating.”

    Lena nodded, and Kara planned to help her feel better about the whole thing, by making their date the best date she’d ever been to.

     

    Lena requested ‘nothing fancy,’ whatever that means. Kara asked Nia for help, which somehow consisted in Nia asking Brainy about Lena's favorite stuff.

    “She values her privacy, now more than ever,” Brainy’s voice announced from Nia’s speakerphone. “Her favorite flowers are plumerias. Loves to eat burgers and she’s very fond of her Irish heritage. Loves a fancy oaky scotch, but appreciates a cold Guinness. The most important thing is a good conversation.” Brainy made a pause and then added; “Miss Danvers, you'll be fine. She enjoys spending time with you.”

    Those were the most encouraging words he could have said, a boost of confidence, they made Kara smile, and she was even more determined to make their first date special.

     

    Instead of a restaurant reservation that was bound to call up paparazzis, Kara planned to make use of her building’s rooftop, which no one ever uses. She spent an entire afternoon cleaning it and tidying the place up. 

    She called Eliza and asked for a recipe of something simple, something that even someone as incompetent in a kitchen as her could make. Relying to her mom everything she knew Lena liked to eat.

    “Burgers are not really a dinner date food, Kara,” her mom told her in between laughs.

    “Hey! I like them.”

    Her mom kept laughing, “Well, let’s try something else, if you really want to impress her.”

    Eliza gave her a recipe for ‘Pasta alla norma;’ It was simple, she’d seen her mom make it a thousand times. Not that she liked it too much, it had too many veggies, but Lena liked veggies, she remembers that. The Thursday before the date she even practiced making it, Kelly and Nia loved it. She found a bakery across town Friday afternoon that sold Irish baked goods, and ordered soda bread and scones, after tasting them both, for Saturday night, just to make sure they wouldn’t run out of them. And then she got a very possibly terrible idea. 

     

    Saturday arrived without much fanfare, she cleaned her apartment, prepared the food, and made sure the roof was still spotless before leaving the apartment to pick up the bread and the scones. When she returned home, Nia was waiting for her at her door.

    “I really hope you are not wearing that to a date with Lena freaking Luthor.”

    Kara looks at her own cerulean polo shirt and blue-jeans. “No, but at this point she’s seen me with worse.”

    “She has seen you with nothing, you mean.” Nia quipped.

    “I rather not know about that.” Alex’s voice surprises her coming out of Kara’s apartment. “Mom called me to make sure the pasta was edible. I had to tell her there was no pasta at your apartment.” Alex continued when she saw Kara’s crinkle in the middle of her forehead.

    “Yeah, I decided to improvise.”

    “Kara you are a terrible cook,” Alex accused her.

    “It’s gonna be fine,” Kara confidently said and entered her apartment, ignoring the look Alex and Nia shared, the latter looking up something on her phone.

     

    Kara had a plan, a very simple one. The plan included the rooftop decorated by fairy lights, a lot of pillows and blankets just in case there was a drop in the temperature. Low instrumental music playing in the background, and a couple of tropical flowers she had to pick from two different flower-shops because they were incredibly rare. Dark beer, a burger served on soda bread. Simple. Fun, even. 

     

    She didn't account for the fact that Lena would show up at her apartment, looking extremely soft. With her long hair in soft waves, barely any makeup, jeans and a very soft-looking purple long sleeve shirt. She recognized it for what it was; Lena being vulnerable, open. It reminded Kara about what she was wearing that fateful day, when she kissed her for the first time, and decided to follow her, when she asked her to go with her. 

    "I feel a bit overdressed," Kara confessed as soon as she saw her, looking down at her gray chinos, and white long sleeved button up Oxford shirt, that Nia insisted she wore to ‘impress Lena freaking Luthor’. 

    “Not at all. I appreciate the effort,” Lena smiled, getting close enough to kiss her cheek. “You look very handsome. And in my defense, you told me to dress comfortably."

    "That's true," Kara mumbled, maybe a little dazzled by Lena's mere presence. "Come with me, I have everything ready."

     

    Lena didn’t say much at first. She looked around the roof, her hands brushing past the pillows, and looking up at the fairy lights. She gave an appreciative look at the food, as the burger patties were still on the grill, but she stopped in front of the flowers. “Plumerias,” she mumbled. “They are almost impossible to find this time of year.”

    “I know, but they are your favorite,” Kara shrugged. “I’d like this date to be special. Made for you. I’ve got your favorite flowers, your favorite foods, the music is very much to your liking…”

    “Kara, I– This looks amazing and all, but I don’t need you to sweep me off my feet,” Lena said, tentatively getting close to Kara, close enough for her to hear her whisper. “You already did that, just by being you.”

    Kara shrugged and smiled, even if a little relieved. “That doesn’t mean you cannot enjoy good things. You deserve beautiful things.” She took Lena’s hands, and cradled them in between hers. “After all these years of hiding yourself behind this evil persona, let me sweep you off your feet, you deserve to be.”

    Lena snickered and shook her head, as if she was exasperated, “Okay,” she mumbled, and that was all the permission Kara needed.

     

    She finished plating their food, and by the time the night had completely arrived, they were sitting over the pillows, Guinness in hand, laughing and conversing. Kara felt completely at ease with Lena, and it was as if nothing or no one but them existed, she was mesmerized. Lena complimented her cooking, and commented how long it had been since she had eaten a good soda bread. 

    "I have never cooked something remotely edible, until this week," she confessed. "My mom will be relieved that I didn't ruin our date by going rogue with the menu."

    "You told your mom about me?" Lena incredulously asked.

    "Of course I did.  I like you a lot."

     

     

    Waking up with Lena warm and cozy in her arms, on her own bed, in her own apartment was nothing short of transcendent. Like it was supposed to always be like that. 

    The first time it happened, a week after her first official date, was after one of the first interviews Lena had with the District Attorney about her mother’s trial. Kara wanted to distract her from it all, and invited her to watch a movie. They settled on Sleeping Beauty, and after Lena ranted about betrothing a newborn to a preteen, and how ridiculous it was for Aurora and Phillip to fall in love after 3 minutes of meeting each other; by the time Maleficent tricked Aurora, and the fairies placed the spell to make everyone sleep, Lena had already fallen asleep with her head on Kara’s lap. 

    Kara didn’t move until the movie finished playing. And when it did, she only moved to accommodate the both of them on her thankfully comfy couch, to sleep the rest of the night.

    The next time it happened was decidedly less innocent. They had been dining with Brainy and Nia in the later’s apartment, the conversation was amazing, the food had been delicious and the wine had been flowing freely. And when the night ended, Kara invited Lena to her apartment.

    The energy of sleepy satisfaction, completely shifted on the elevator to quietly electrifying, all the way to her floor. And by the time Kara had managed to open her own door, her free hand was already under Lena’s black blouse, climbing up to delightfully more naughty places.

    She woke up the next day with Alex squeaking after finding them in bed, “I’m sorry!” She said with a hand covering her eyes. “Listen, I would be more scandalized about this, but I got the notification that the ring was delivered, and I’m more nervous about that. Where is it?”

    Lena groaned next to her, covering herself with the blankets, Kara picked up a shirt and dressed still half asleep. She remembered getting the ring before the dinner with Nia and Brainy, the delivery man arrived at the same time as Lena, and… she was still too groggy to remember where she put it for safekeeping.

    “In the kitchen,” Lena mumbled, still under the covers. “You said something about everyone knowing better than to touch your chips?”

    “It's true, no one touches my chips,” Kara nodded wobbly walking to her kitchen, Alex right behind her still with her eyes covered.

    “So, when is the big day?” Kara asked her sister, while retrieving the red velvet box next to her plates.

    “Two days from now, it's the anniversary of when I knew I was in love with Kelly,” Alex replied, someplace close to the dining room, her hand still affixed to her eyes.

    "You can open your eyes, Alex," Kara laughs leaving the box on the butcher's block. "Lena's still in the bed and I'm totally dressed." She waited for her sister to open her eyes. "It had something to do with Die Hard, right?"
