    "We were talking about the greatest villains of all time, Winn was in his rant about Darth Vader, and Kelly and I both said at the same time…"

    " Hans Grubber; I remember now," Kara smiled.

    Alex did too, "So, Lena spent the night…"

    "I didn't think you were a prude, Alex," Kara tried to joke. Alex is somewhat of a prude, she freaked out once because she had a one night stand after her first girlfriend.

    "I am not! I'm just trying to figure out if you guys are dating," Alex grumbled as she carefully put the ring box into her pocket. 

    “I’m not… sure, actually. We had a date, we had dinner with Nia and Brainy last night, while you and Kelly were in the movies. It’s not the first time she spends the night,”  she commented.

    “I think it would be wise for you to clear that up with her, Kara,” Alex recommended.

    "Are you gonna tell me that you changed your mind and don't approve anymore?" 

    "No, not like that. I just think that she's about to be thrown into a very public trial, against her own family." Alex sighed. "You need to decide if you are ready for that type of commitment. And if you are not, you need to tell her so she has someone who would do that for her."

    Silence enveloped them for a few seconds, and then Kara finally vocalized the decision she'd taken since her recovery weeks. "Alex, I'm leaving the DEO."

    Alex nodded, after taking in the news. 

    "I'm all in, I'll support her with the trial, I'll testify if I have to, she won't do this alone." 

    "Okay, I'm here whatever you need."

     

    Lena wasn’t as cool with it as Kara thought she would be. 

    “That’s your entire career, Kara, the whole reason we concocted a truly farfetched facade, and now you want to leave it?”

    “It’s not because of you,” Kara softly explained. “You are part of it, I won’t lie. But that's not all. I love my job, but I want more. I told you I felt more me with you than with anyone else, I think you also made me remember that I had other dreams, dreams I stopped pursuing because of the secrecy of my job. I want those back.”

    Lena looked at her, searching for deceit on her face, then very slowly, she nodded. “Okay, then. What will you do next?”

    “I’m going to collect my last favor, I think,” Kara smiled.

     

     

    Alex and Kelly got engaged (proposing to each other the same day, at the same place for the same reason, because they are connected like that), and Kara presented her resignation letter. 

    It was easier than she thought. Not only facing change, something she was admittedly not good at, leaving the only job that actually bought her joy, but also asking Ms. Grant for another favor. 

    "You'll do great at The Tribune, you have a knack for discovering the truth," the tiny blonde commented. "And it will be good for you personally."

    "What's that supposed to mean?"

    “Lena Luthor is the best thing that has happened to you in over a year,” Cat Grant declared. “Not because of any nonsensical romance, but because you’ve been bored out of your mind before meeting her. I’ve been watching you. Just standing there at the shore, not scared to dive, but standing still because you were comfortable with your life, but you were still bored . But you were dying to go for a swim, that’s why it was so easy for you to go with a criminal you’d just met.”

    "Do you know why I selected you to be the face of the Argo Team's, instead of your sister like Mr. J'onnz' wanted?"

    “Yes, you reminded me about a very young me, a me that was scared of taking her power in her own hands.” Major Grant told her. "You were just looking around, fumbling, unsure. Now you are in a similar position, you are bored. You were content, but not happy. You need to dive once more. Congratulations on finally making that decision, Kira. Now move along, I have stuff to do."

She never quite made the apartment her own, it was comfortable no doubt, but it was not her, at least not anymore. Ms. Grant was right, she'd outgrown everything in her life; she'd been content with everything, but she hadn't been happy. It was time to find what would make her so.

But she seemed frustrated by the apartment hunting at times, not even by Kara's budget limitations or her insistence for it to be close to her favorite coffee shop, she made Kara go to visit some apartment and then say that it wasn't right, that Kara could find a bigger one, or a closer to Noonan's one, when they were perfectly fine as they were. Alex was as confused as her, and Kelly laughed so much that Kara was worried she would have an asthma attack (not that Kelly was asthmatic, but it happened to someone she knew back in Midvale).

    "She's resisting the Sapphic urge to U-Haul," Kelly said, when she finally calmed down.

    "Oh," Alex and Kara said at the same time, Kara didn't know that could be an option. 

    "It's too soon…?" Alex commented, or asked, Kara wasn't sure.

She had basically abandoned her job, her sister, her life for Lena, and she barely knew her at the time. Now, now there was no going back, Lena had burrowed into her  heart, irrevocably, indelibly. 

    "Oh," she repeated, under her breath. 

    Kelly smiled at her like sensing her internal struggle. "Maybe look out for a short lease?"

    "Yeah," Kara whispered, maybe that was a good idea.

    Key Lime and Mocha Chocolate cake won the day.

She signed a lease for a year. When Kara let Lena know about that factoid, she didn't outwardly comment about it, but she seemed incredibly less tense. Until she got the call that she would be testifying soon.

It wasn't something they planned, but apparently Lena needed some document or another from LuthorCorp, and the CEO, who used to be her best friend, was worried about her. But also still pissed, and kept just giving Lena conflicting information, to keep her in the building. When Kara called Lena up about her DEO mandated mock trial, Lena passive-aggressively let her know on the phone that her best friend was holding her hostage in her own old office, and to please pick her up.

    And just like that, Kara had met one of the most important people in Lena's life. It was clear from the first moment that Samantha cared about Lena. 

    "You could've told me," was the first thing Kara heard her say, before even setting foot in her office. "I would've kept the secret, I could've helped you gather evidence."

    "I know," Lena replied, "but it would've been too dangerous. You have a kid and I would've never forgiven myself if anything had happened to you or Ruby because of Lex's ambitions. It worked out for the better in the end."

    Lena took a moment to officially introduce Kara and interlocked their fingers together, like drawing strength just with the contact.

    "What if you both come to have dinner with Ruby and I," Samantha invited them. "She missed you immensely, so did I, and I'm willing to bury the hatchet."

    "Of course, we would love to. But I have to get going," Lena whispered, standing up and picking the documents she came looking for in the first place. "I will text you to iron out the details," Lena smiled, timid, but so happy. "It was great seeing you, Sam."

    "You too," the woman replied and then turned to Kara. "And very nice meeting you."

    "Likewise," Kara smiled back.

     

     

    Dinner at the Arias’ was like a reprieve from the DEO work, and dinner with her own coworkers. To Kara was getting to know an entirely new side of Lena, and to Lena it was probably like coming home, more than her going back to her actual apartment. 

    Ruby was a tall teen, clearly something she got from her mother. She was almost as tall as Kara, and it was heartwarming to see a very lanky 16-years-old, envelop a comparatively tiny Lena. And cling to her for minutes on end.

    “I missed you so much, Aunt Lena,” Kara heard Ruby whisper. “I told mom you couldn’t be doing what the news said you were doing.”

    There was a guilty look on Lena’s face, a blink and you miss expression, “I was doing what they said, Ruby. It’s just that I was doing it for a different reason.” Lena said, sticking as close to the truth as she could, just like Kara had recommended. 

    She took a moment to actually look at the teenager in front of her. “Look at you, honey. You are all grown up. Sorry I missed it.”

    “Don’t worry, the important thing is that you are back, and I have great news!”

    “We can talk about that and much more while we eat, come on,” Samanta— Sam, she insisted Kara called her– interrupted.

    “Oh, Ruby, I want to introduce you to Kara,” Lena said just before they started walking towards the table. Kara smiled and extended her hand to shake Ruby’s. “My girlfriend.”

    Kara’s heart skipped a beat, and she looked at Lena, with a smile. It was the first time she introduced her, as such.

    “Nice to meet you,” Ruby said, bringing Kara back to reality, she hadn’t even realized that the teen had been shaking her hand.

    She heard Sam laughing in the distance, and Lena was still blushing prettily next to her, “was that okay?”

    “Of course,” Kara whispered. “I liked it.”

    Lena laughed, “Food?”

    “Yes, please.”

     

    It was easy to see how Sam and Lena were best friends. They had hundreds of shared memories, and Ruby was included in a lot of them, sometimes at the center of them, sometimes as an accomplice. Lena screeched like a teen when Ruby told her that a scout from MET U had already gone to one of her soccer games.

    Kara knew Lena was already hating the fact that she would have to lie to them for the rest of their lives.

    “How did you meet?” Sam asked, as they (the adults) sipped on white wine, and ate apple pie. Lena busied herself drinking more wine.

    “Work, actually.” Kara replied.

    “Are you in tech too?” Ruby frowned.

    “No, no. I– Until very recently I was the agent that was her contact,” Kara settled on saying. “I worked for the agency that she approached with her suspicions. I was the one that kept tabs on her.”

    “Isn't that some sort of conflict of interest?”

    “To some extent, which is why I left the agency,” Kara shrugged.

    “Oh,” Sam shared a look with Lena, but didn’t say anything else.

    “So, what position do you play?” Kara asked Ruby, trying to change the topic.

     

    Lena and Sam had time to catch up, Lena in hers and Brainy’s shenanigans, as Sam called them. Lena revealed that Brainy had also started dating someone, and Sam told her about the goings-on of LuthorCorp's employees, and her own personal life. There was a date with someone named Cisco, and Sam seemed stuck talking about his hair, for some reason. There was also some teasing about Ruby liking a kid from the debate team, something she denied profusely. Kara understood why Lena wanted to keep them from the life she’d gone when she decided to stop her brother. 

    All in all, it was a really nice dinner.

     

     

    The trial dragged on for months. And Lena's life was put under the microscope with it. The lie held up, but the press brought back her previous relationship with Andrea Rojas, even when neither confirmed nor denied anything, the rumor mill was ruthless. People who Lena shared a classroom once, were giving interviews as if they'd been friends for years. But she was relieved that all the attention was on her instead of Brainy.

    Kara had left the DEO, and was working now for The Tribune, as a Jr. Reporter. Her editor, a very grumpy and half bald man named Snapper Carr, didn't want her to write anything ‘big’ until the trial was done and Kara was happy to brush up her skills writing movie reviews and pop culture puff pieces. She hadn't realized how much she missed the simplicity of putting her feelings and thoughts to words into paper, and those words to carry significance and have the power to change people, instead of almost robotically filling reports. Even with just puff pieces. 

    Not to say she didn't miss the DEO sometimes. She missed seeing Alex, Kelly and Nia, whom she didn’t see as much since she moved to her new apartment. She missed Winn and J’onn. She also missed the easy way she used to find information on the agency database, now there was no database to look into. At best there were archived documents inside infinite boxes that were still in the process of someday being digitized. But Kara still couldn’t bring herself to regret her decision, because even when her articles she was writing were about a new community theater play where tickets cost $45 dollars each, or the new pastry shop that sold dick-shaped waffles, she loved it. She’d found love for writing again. 

     

    Meanwhile, Lena was allowed to go back to LCorp as CEO, even with the trial still going, the board wanted to use Lena’s Press ’relatively good’ image for catching her mother and everyone involved with Lex, as a way to stop the profits of the company from plummeting; and in all honesty, they needed her genius to do so. 

    She immediately renamed the company LCorp, to separate her last name from it. She named Sam as her CFO, and promoted Brainy as the head of Research and Development. 

    As soon as the renaming ceremony took place, in between protestors and paparazzi, their stock market price stabilized. 

     

    Kara suggested that Lena give an interview, and with a lot of back and forth between the DEO and the DA, about what she could say and what she couldn’t, and who could be the best to have the exclusive, the Daily Planet was selected, Kara having recommended Lois for it. That was how Lena met her and Clark.

    As always, Lois was determined to get to the truth and deviated from the pre-approved questionnaire several times. For a few tense seconds, Kara thought she’d figured out the entire web of half truths and lies, but Lena was steadfast and sure of herself, giving nothing away.

    The DA guy was sweating bullets and letting Lena know what questions she couldn’t answer.

     

    Clark wasn’t particularly warm to Lena at first, watching her like a hawk. It irritated Kara who kept scowling at him. Once the interview ended, Lena got close to Kara and smirked, in a way that the blonde knew only spelled trouble.

    “Let me tell you,” She started, loud enough not only for Kara to hear, but to everyone in the room, including Kara’s former bosses. “I get why you had a crush on her, I think you could’ve won her over your cousin if you’d tried.”

    Lois let out a delighted snort, “Oh, Midvale, I think I like her.”

     

    Two days later, when the interview went to print, LCorp’s stock price slowly but surely started to climb up again. 

     

    Kelly left the DEO too. She’d been offered a job at Obsidian North, which confused them all, since Alex had literally pointed a weapon at the CEO. 

    Lena suggested that maybe Kelly made a strong impression on Andrea at that moment, and that she’d always had a great eye for spotting talent. 

    She’d accepted the job, regardless. And Kara remembered how her sister had told her that they wanted to adopt as soon as possible. With the wedding so soon, it seemed that the possibility now was very real. She couldn’t wait to have a niece or nephew. 

     

    Alex and Kelly moved out of the DEO building, too. They found a big apartment, with a nice school nearby, and close to Obsidian’s North Headquarters. Alex's commute was longer, but Kara could tell she was happy, and Kara was happy for her too. 



    Kara was asked by Alex and Kelly to sing at their wedding. They had no song in mind, but they wanted her to find a song for their first dance as wives. Kara was touched by the request. 

    “I don’t think there is anyone who knows us better than you,” Alex had said.

    “Except maybe Nia, but I think she will drive herself mad, if she tries to do something else, besides what she’s already doing.” Kelly finished.

    Nia had taken upon herself to deal with flowers, vendors, DJ and catering; she'd organized the entire thing, using Lena’s contacts when needed. She was a ruthless negotiator, but it was clear she was stressed most of the time.

    “I’m sure you’ll pick up something great,” Alex commented. “Just let me know so I can look up a YouTube video to pretend that I know how to dance.”

     

    Kara listened to thousands of songs in the weeks leading up to the wedding. She thought she found the one, just a week before the big day. Just enough time to do the musical arrangement with a contact she’d made when she started working for the tribune. It had been a while since she sang outside her shower or the car. But she'd do anything for Alex and Kelly. Lena knew she was going to sing, but she never did it in front of her, she’d never heard Kara sing. And Kara wasn’t going to lie, she did feel nervous about Lena's opinion, but her priority was her sister and Kelly.

     

    When the day of the wedding finally arrived, she was calm. She had been with her sister the entire day, calming a very nervous Alex, next to their mother Eliza.

    Eliza had been unavailable for the dress rehearsal, she had arrived in the early morning just enough time to dress and calm Alex down. It didn’t dawn on Kara that her mother would be officially meeting her girlfriend until she was making a sound test at the venue just two hours before the wedding.

    She called Lena immediately. 

    “Do… Do you want me to pick you up?” She asked. “I know we agreed that you would get here alone, but I think I need the reassurance more than you.” She confessed, once she told Lena about it.

    Lena was quiet for a moment, and then asked, in a tiny voice, “Are you nervous because you think she would not like me?”

    “It’s not that,” Kara was quick to calm her. “I think she would love you, I think she already does, but… The only other person I’ve introduced to mom was Jimmy… And it was before we started dating.”

    “I don’t understand.”

    Kara hesitated, they’d been dating just for a few months, she sighed and then, she dived. 

    “I’m hoping you will be the last person I ever introduce to my mom,” she said, sure of herself. “That’s why I’m nervous about it.”

    Lena didn’t answer for a few seconds, but when she talked, Kara could hear the smile on her voice. “You can pick me up, the town car is still not here.”

     

    Kara was fast to dress herself. The blue shirt and red tie, with the especially tailored gray suit, had been Nia’s idea. She was the only person who had seen all the outfits, including the brides. She’d been the one to select the red tie, something to match Lena, she’d said. Kara drove her car back to Lena’s penthouse where Lena told her to park on her spot, while trying to keep her composure. However, nothing could prepare her for the majestic view Lena was once she got out of the elevator in the underground parking lot, where the Town Car was already waiting for them. Kara’s mouth felt dry, as Lena approached her, in a red dress with a plunging, yet classy, cleavage. Her hair in a high ponytail, and her lips as red as her dress. She was breathtaking.

    “Wow,” she breathed once Lena was close to her. “You look amazing.”

    “And you look dashingly handsome as well.” Lena smiled at her. “Shall we?”

    Kara opened the door of the car, beating the driver for it, and waited for her partner to sit before closing the door and going over through the other door.

    “I want to kiss you, but I'm afraid I could wipe off your makeup,” Kara confessed with a smile, taking Lena’s hand in hers.

    Lena laughed, a delighted sound. “It's a very high quality lipstick, you should test its resistance.” She teased.

    It was an invitation that Kara was not going to ignore. She leaned in and kissed her. Forgetting for a moment about the imminence of her girlfriend meeting her mom, at her sister’s wedding. 

    “How does it look?” Lena flirtatiously asked, once they split apart.

    “As perfect as when you got in the car,” Kara smiled.

    “Excellent.”

     

    Eliza was with Alex when they got back to the Ranch. Alex was almost driving a path on the floor with her pacing. Still barefoot, her white tailored suit perfectly pressed and her hair as crazy as ever.

    “What if she changes her mind? What if she thinks we are not ready yet,” was the first thing Kara heard her sister say once her and Lena entered the room.

    “Oh, we are on the fatalist stage, already? A bit early, isn’t it?” Kara jested. Alex scowled and their mother just smiled.

    “You must be Lena,” she said. “It’s nice to finally meet you. Kara can’t stop talking about you.”

    That made Alex snort a laugh. 

    “MOM!”

    Lena smiled, graciously. “It’s nice to meet you too, Doctor Danvers.”

    “I appreciate the title, dear but just Eliza is fine.”

    “Of course,” Lena said, and then turned to Alex. “I don’t think you have to worry about Kelly changing her mind, she’s very excited to be your wife.” 

    Lena and Kelly had developed a really close relationship after Lena was cleared by the DA. Kara was willing to say that they’d become really good friends.

    Alex stopped pacing from that moment on, reassured by Lena’s words, probably having noted the same thing as Kara.

     

    Clark and Lois made the trip from Metropolis. Jimmy showed up with Lucy hugging his arm, apparently they were back together, Kara couldn’t say she was surprised about that. Lena was her own date. 

    It should've been awkward. Kara and Lena were responsible for the Lane sisters' father's preventive arrest, and he was about to be court-martialed. Kara had, at some point, taken Lucy's boyfriend from her, then she left Jimmy, and a week later she was with Lena. 

    It should absolutely be awkward. It wasn't. Lois had been aware of her dad's prejudices, she said he was against her dating Clark when he found out he'd been born outside the US. And Lucy, despite everything, believed in the justice system, with its flaws and everything.

    She looked at Lena up and down, when they found each other as they took their seats. 

    "I don't approve of the methods, or the path you took, but I know how difficult it would have been to build a case the right way against the most powerful people in the country" Lucy commented. “Arresting those involved in it was the priority and I commend you for taking the risks you took.”

    “Thank you for saying that,” Lena muttered.

    Kara thanked Lucy for her words, with a nod.

     

    J'onn was the wedding’s officiant, he’d been a silent witness and quiet supporter of their love. The ceremony was quite straightforward, surrounded by their friends and family. Alex almost cried when she first saw Kelly walk down the aisle on James’ arm. Neither of them could keep the tears at bay while they were saying their vows. 

     

    It was beautiful. Their first kiss as wives was both desperate and precious. Kara heard Kelly faintly and excitedly say to Alex, “We are married,” right before kissing her. She felt at peace with the song she’d chosen.

    There were toasts, mainly James, Winn and J’onn, talking about their experience with the happy couple, and when they figured out they were made for each other. Kara had no doubt Kelly was her sister's perfect match, and vice versa.

     

    “I invite the happy couple to the dance floor. It is my sisterly duty to sing for your first dance,” Kara said to the mic as she signaled to the DJ for her to play the music track. 

     

    It feels like home was the song she picked, because it was something she vividly remembered Alex saying when she started actually dating Kelly instead of just charged looks in locker rooms, and barbed comments from her part, while Kelly just laughed at how ridiculous she was. Alex had seen Kelly as her home since then.

     

    Something in your eyes

    Makes me wanna lose myself

    Makes me wanna lose myself

    In your arms

    There's something in your voice

    Makes my heart beat fast

    Hope this feeling lasts

    The rest of my life

     

    Kara started singing, and she could see the smile on Alex and Kelly’s faces as they swayed on the middle of the dance floor. She saw as her sister gave her a very subtle thumbs up, for her song selection, she supposed. But as she continued singing, there was a pull, something that called her to look at Lena. 

     

    Lena, who was watching her sing for the first time in a space full of people, and she could see even from a distance, how her green-blue eyes were big and round, and swallowed by black. And while the reason she chose the song was because the lyrics reminded her of the love Alex and Kelly had for each other, Kara couldn’t stop herself from thinking that she wanted exactly the same. With that thought, she kept singing, with her eyes fixed on her girlfriend.

     

    If you knew how happy you are making me

    I've never thought that I'd love anyone so much

    Feels like home to me

    Feels like home to me

     

    “Mrs. and Mrs. Danvers-Olsen, everyone,” Kara presented them after finishing singing, and applauded at her sister and her now wife. “Now, let’s celebrate,” the smile on her face, big and radiant. 

    The wedding from that moment on was a blur, she drank a lot, and she danced with Lena, with her mom, Lois, and Kelly. She goofed around with Clark and Alex. 

    Lucy and James kept their distance, socializing with Winn and Kelly’s side of the family. Kara couldn’t say she minded it. Their break up was recent, and Kara could understand why James wanted to keep his distance, and why Lucy wanted to keep her distance too. Didn’t help that Lucy and Lois never really saw eye to eye on anything. In any case, the wedding reception was for all intent and purposes, a complete success.

     

    But her night, in the privacy of her own apartment, in her bedroom was even better. Suffice to say that Lena was very impressed with her singing, and Kara hadn’t even realized that she’d never sung in front of her, since she felt she sang all the time.

    She spent her night with her mouth very busy, kissing Lena senseless and singing in her ear while she teased her and touched her in the most sensitive places.

     

     

    While Alex and Kelly were on their honeymoon, Lena and Kara went out with Eliza, Clark and Lois. 

    There was some tension between her cousin and Lena, but it was on her part. With Lois and Eliza, she seemed to get along tremendously. When Kara asked her about it one night at Lena’s apartment, Lena gave her an answer that actually surprised her.

    “You are too good with him,” She explained. “From what you told me, he never visited you while you were growing up. And even now, he only visited because he was accompanying his wife, who happens to need an interview with me.” Lena put her hand over hers. “He abandoned you with strangers. He was your only connection to your family as you were his and he simply vanished from your life.”

    A laugh escaped Kara, “You know, Alex says the same thing,” she shrugged. “In all honesty, I can’t blame him. He was never an El. He was born Kal-El and a year later he was Clark Kent, from Smallville. He doesn’t know the first thing about Kandor, or our family. He was to me the same as Alex, Eliza and Jeremiah at the time; a complete stranger.”

    She smiled at Lena, and gave her a kiss on her cheek. “Thank you for being mad on my behalf, but it’s not necessary. We have a nice relationship, and he tries. It’s just that he doesn’t have much in common with me, besides the fact that we are both adopted and that I somewhat followed his career path.”

    “But you are family,” Lena whispered. 

    “By blood, perhaps. But the family I chose is more important to me.”

     

    Lena eased off after that, she still pointedly took some digs at Clark’s absence in her life, but Lois assured her that at least she would call and visit more often. And made Kara promise to try and visit Metropolis too. 

    When Kara was dropping them off at the airport, while hugging her goodbye, Clark promised he would write to her more often.



    Some of the minor players in the Lex case got convicted, sentences ranging from simple fines, to jail sentences from 3 years to 10 years. Lillian Luthor’s trial was still midway, but at least, the press had backed off of Lena. And off Andrea, since her wedding was around the corner, she’d at some point had a very lavish bachelorette party that was the talk of the entire city.
    There were several kickers in the same week, for one Andrea called Lena and invited her to lunch. For another, Nia also quitted the DEO, to work for Catco Magazine, recommended by Cat Grant herself, again. Kara was starting to wonder if Nia had some dirt on the woman. And for the pièce de résistance, Olivia Marsdin announced she was running for President again. She had a lot of the public’s support, right away.

     

    Kara didn’t really want anything to do with Andrea and Lena’s lunch. Something that her girlfriend misinterpreted as jealousy. And she wasn’t, not really. A bit intimidated, maybe. Andrea not only had been Lena’s first love, but also one of her oldest friends. Probably no one knew her as well as Andrea did, bar Sam. Maybe.

    In any case, Lena asked her to not let her be alone with Andrea for more than 20 minutes, and so, Kara was outside this Michellin Star restaurant, in the middle of the day and, no way of actually entering the place because she didn’t have a reservation.

    Thankfully, Lena was apparently looking at her phone like a hawk, that not even two minutes after letting her know she was outside but couldn’t get in, a waiter showed up at the door asking her name and telling her to follow them. 

    As soon as she saw her, Lena smiled, and once she realized her presence, Andrea scoffed.

    “Really, Lena. Did you really call your girlfriend, 911? I should be offended.”

    “I am not going to pretend I wasn’t nervous about your invitation,” Lena confidently replied after giving Kara a peck. “I did tell her to come rescue me, but I do want you to meet her. Andrea, this is Kara Danvers, my partner.”

    “Very nice to meet you, Ms Rojas.” Kara extended her hand.

    “Your sister almost shot me,” Andrea drawled, considering for a second before actually taking Kara’s hand. “Pleasure. Are you going to join us?”

    “Actually I already had lunch, but I can join you for dessert,” Kara lied easily, there was no way she could actually afford a lunch there without upsetting her entire monthly budget. And she refuses to let Lena pay for everything. 

    It was of the few things they had disagreements on, Lena wanted to buy extremely expensive stuff in Kara’s eyes, and Kara didn’t want her to. And like sensing her train of thought, Lena pursed her lips.

    “Are you sure?”

    “Yeah, yeah, I had sushi with Nia.”

    Lena narrowed her eyes, and called the waiter, in defiance. “We will have a steak, thank you.”

    “No, we will not. Thank you. I told you I already ate—”

    “So, Kara, Lena tells me you are a reporter?” Andrea asked, likely sensing the tension.

    “Yes! I mean, yes. I’m still relearning everything, but it’s going great,” she smiled, taking the metaphorical hand Andrea was extending.

    “Well, if you want once we come back from our honeymoon, Russell and I could give you an exclusive.” Andrea offered. “Do you write about technology? Russell has his own company, he specializes in robotics, I suppose that’s kind of your turf, Lena. I hope there is no bad blood.”

    Kara was sure there was a double meaning to her words, especially to Lena, who was looking at her ex as if she was the biggest red flag to exist. Kara was left to flounder.

    “Well, I can pitch it to my editor, I personally don’t really write about technology, but I'm sure The Tribune will be accommodating.”

    “What are you playing?” Lena mumbled, Kara didn’t dare breathe.

    “I’m not playing anything.” Andrea replied, her blue eyes fixed on Lena. “I’m trying to have a cordial relationship with your girlfriend because, regardless of our relationship and how it ended, I miss you. Not like a girlfriend, but as my best friend.”

    Lena didn’t say anything. Not for a while.

    “I miss you, Lena. You are my oldest friend, and I do want you in my wedding.”

    “Are you seriously asking me to go to your wedding? And seeing you marry the man you left me for?”

    “First of all, I didn’t leave you for him,” Andrea defended herself. And for what Lena told Kara when they first met, she was right. ”And second of all, it seems to me that you moved on too. We were friends before being girlfriends. Please?”

    When Lena didn’t answer, Andrea was taken aback, somewhat flabbergasted.

    “Are you… Do you—”

    “No.” Lena was swift. “No. It took a while, but I don’t have feelings for you anymore. I— I found someone else, just as you did.”

    “I’m happy for you,” Andrea said, her eyes fixed on Lena, seeming sincere, at least to Kara.

    “I’m happy for you too,” Lena took Kara’s hand under the table and interlocked their fingers, smiling at her. “And… We would love to go to your wedding.”

     

     

    To Kara, that wedding was decidedly less thrilling than Alex and Kelly’s, but to Lena it was nerve-wracking. Not only was she going to the wedding of her former girlfriend, but it was the first time she was around the people she used to socialize with before turning her life to crime, even in the pursuit of justice.

    “Kara, these people are backstabbing, judgemental and I’m almost certain that more than a couple of them would have been on Lex’s plan if given the chance,” Lena commented, while they were on route, apparently tired of biting her tongue. “I don’t really want to go to this wedding, if it weren’t because it’s Andrea’s. And she’s right, we were best friends longer than we were girlfriends.”

    Kara noticed that Lena was wringing her hands on her lap. She took one of them and interlocked their fingers, before kissing the back of her hand. That seemed to calm her.

    “What I'm trying to say is… Thank you, Kara. For coming with me.”

    “Of course. There is no place I rather be.”

     

    Kara spent the night, playing buffer between Lena and whatever awkward question the wedding guests had for her. Never confirming nor denying any previous relationship between Andrea and Lena, and avoiding any comment about Lillian’s trial. If Lena needed her to not shut up all night talking nonsense, so no one would ask her about tabloid rumors, she could do that.

    “Do you wanna dance?” she asked the brunette, after another awkward conversation with someone named Jeff Colby who was actually hitting on Lena, right in front of her, even when she had been introduced as her girlfriend.

    Lena coyly smiled and accepted the invitation. “It’s that jealousy, I sense?” She asked, in the middle of the dance floor, away from nosey ears.

    “I don’t know what you are talking about.”

    “Sure you don’t,” Lena laughed next to her ear. “Thank you, you have saved me all night.”

    “It’s nothing, Lena. In fact, I think I will pester Snapper to let me write something scandalous about some of these people, maybe how Bernardo Rojas’ date is even younger than his daughter, what do you think?”

    “If you want that exclusive with Andrea, you’ll leave her father alone,” Lena mumbles in her ear, Kara could still sense the smile on her words. “She’s always been incredibly overprotective of him. Not that I think she approves of that.”

    Kara hummed, still guiding Lena on their slow dance, to some waltz or another. “Do you think it’s weird?”

    “I think she’s overcompensating because she’s afraid he will stop loving her if she does something wrong,” Lena whispered.

    “I’m guessing this was a point of tension between the two of you,” Kara let out. Curious more than jealous.

    “It was.”

    “Can I ask you something?”

    “You just did…” Lena’s voice was teasing. When Kara didn’t say anything, she sighed. “Sure, Kara. You can ask me anything.”

    “If we— If she—” Kara started, but Lena took a step back to look at her eyes, without breaking their dance. 

    “Kara… Does it really matter? She is married now, and I'm happy for her. And you and I did meet. The rest is irrelevant,” Lena said with her eyes fixed on Kara’s. Like daring Kara to contradict her, better yet, daring her to believe her. And Kara did, so she nodded.

    Lena got closer again to Kara, fully into her arms again, swaying in place. “But I would still choose you.” Lena rasped close to her ear.

     

     

    Olivia Marsdin won the Presidency in a landslide, and appointed Cat Grant as her Press Secretary as soon as she officially took office. There was overwhelming evidence against Lillian Luthor, and the DA was confident about getting a conviction, until Lillian’s lawyers reached out and told them she wanted a deal. Promising to reveal even more information. With one condition; that Lena went to visit her. 

    To Kara, it screamed trap. To Lena, Lillian was always going to be an Achilles heel.

    “I’m going with you,” she told her girlfriend.

    “She will never accept the DA, and you in the room,” Lena refuted.

    “She will, if you give her that ultimatum, if she really wants to see you… Please, Lena. This seems dangerous.”

    “I can take care of myself.”

    “I know you can,” Kara whispered, taking Lena’s hands, placatingly. “But you don’t have to confront your mom alone.”

    Lena nodded after a moment, looking at her. “I’ll let the lawyers know.”

     

    Lillian Luthor was an imposing woman, even sitting on a metal table, in gray prison clothes. Her hair was free, and her back was ramrod straight, with so much poise and her face was full of judgment, especially for someone who was facing up to 20 years in prison. She pursed her lips as soon as she saw them enter the room with their hands interlocked. 

    “I see you have no shame,” she drawled as a greeting.

    “I have nothing to be ashamed of. I’m not the one chained to a table because I wanted to murder people.” Lena answered back, clear and sure.

    “A blonde, Lena, seriously?”

    “You are impossible, mother. Why am I here?”

    “Because I want to make a deal,” Lillian said, like someone who just ordered a drink in a bar, and not someone sitting chained to a metal table.

    “I am aware, still doesn’t answer why am I in here,” Lena argued. Her back was so upright that Kara was sure it must hurt. “Why did you ask me to come here?”

    “The deal I want to make it’s not with the DA office, but with you, Lena,” the older woman explained, like she was explaining something to a five-year-old. “If I'm going to reveal more about your brother's plan, and give even more names, I want you to visit me at least twice a month.”

    “You are out of your mind,” Lena noted.

    “I’ve been told, but it doesn't change the fact that If we don’t make a deal, I will not talk, and someone outside could continue with Lex’s plans,” Lillian Luthor finished.

    Lena was crushing Kara’s hand with her own. Kara tried not to wince. She looked at her girlfriend, and cleared her throat. 

    “If she decides to accept, I’ll be coming with her.”

    “Kara…” 

    “She will not be alone with you,” Kara finished.

    Mrs. Luthor pursed her lips once again, “I see you found someone to fight your battles. Typical.”

    “Not at all,” Kara said before Lena could even open her mouth. “She’s very capable of taking care of herself. But that doesn't mean she has to do anything alone. Ever again, if she doesn’t want to. She has me now.”

    Lena’s mother was quiet for a minute, maybe longer, Kara wasn’t sure. She was more interested in Lena’s hand in hers. 

    “Well, Lena, do you accept the deal?”

     

    Turns out, Lex’s personal assistant was more than his personal assistant. Eve Teschmacher was his lover, his henchwoman, and heir to his plans. Apparently Lena’s brother had contingencies, putting his genius reputation to good use. He had left Eve with an enormous amount of money so she could finish what he started. Lillian told the DA enough to start a proper investigation that was fruitful pretty soon. 

    Lillian's sentence was reduced to 8 years with the option of parole for good behavior and cooperation. Which to Kara was bullshit. And Lena forced herself to visit her mother every two weeks. They usually just played chess, at first in silence, Lillian looking at Kara from time to time, her expression unreadable. Then she would ask something that was meant to be digs at her. 

    “How is Jack?”

    “Seriously, mother…” Lena mumbled, taking a rook from Lillian.

    “Will you lie and tell me he didn’t call when he heard of my arrest? He was always very polite and affectionate.”

    “And If I remember correctly, you hated him because of the color of his skin, so I don’t see why he is relevant to you now.”

    “For one, at least he has some class…”

     

    “Who is Jack?” 

    Lena stopped reading her tablet to look at her, eating ice cream from the tub in the kitchen, as if the question hadn’t been bouncing around in her brain since their last visit to Lillian Luthor.

    “Don’t let mother get in your head,” Lena warned.

    “I’m just curious,” she deflected.

    Lena nodded. “Jack Spheer. He’s someone I met at MIT. He was… interested in me. We shared similar goals, he’s Spheerical Industries, CEO.”

    “Did— Did you date?”

    Lena took a moment. “Almost, but no.”

    “Okay. Why did your mom ask about him, then?”

    Lena stands up and walks up to her, putting her arms around her neck, after taking the ice cream from her hands and putting it on the fridge again. “Because she wants you to be insecure, and wreak havoc on our relationship, so you stop accompanying me to visit her.”

    “Oh.”

    “Like I said, don’t let her get in your head,” Lena whispered before kissing her. “You have nothing to worry about.”

    Kara let herself be convinced by Lena’s kisses and caresses. She picked her up, kissing her neck, and walked with Lena hanging from her hips to her bed. 

    At some point of the night, Lena was, taking Kara's seven inches, with her mouth half open in ecstasy, clawing at her shoulders, and looking at her with feral and desperate eyes. And Kara was hit with the single minded thought that she didn't want Lena to ever look at anyone else like that, to look at anyone but her. That she also didn't want to feel like this with anyone who wasn't Lena.

     

     

    It’d been eighteen months since she was shot and Lex died, when it happened. She’d been late for an early interview with a Doctor, because she stayed up late the night before because she was painting. Lena was still in her National City University hoodie, still half asleep and drinking coffee, and Kara was fleeting around the apartment collecting her notepads and keys. She got closer to Lena, kissed her lips and said her goodbye. “I’m so late, Snapper is going to kill me, this man is known for not giving interviews. Lunch is on me today. See you later,” and that was when it happened. “I love you.”

    She didn’t register, she said the words, until she was halfway to her car. She meant it, absolutely. She was just… not sure why it took so long for her to say the words, when she’d been feeling it for months… too many months. 

    Sadly she couldn’t dwell on it at the moment, she was late to her interview with Amadei Derros.

     

    She showed up to Lena’s office past noon with food and a couple of white orchids, since Lena’s favorite flowers were impossible to find on such short notice. Lena was talking to someone on the phone when Jess let her enter her office. The crinkle in her girlfriend’s forehead was troubling, but her words were calm, so no one was on the line to be fired that day.

    “So, are you serious about it?” Lena asked, her eyes softening for a moment. “Okay then. Send me the address, Kara and I will be there.” And just like that she hung up.

    “Where will we be?”

    “Samantha wants us to go with her and Cisco to a thing. Apparently it’s the first gala she’s going to with him, she needs moral support.”

    “They have been dating for ages,” Kara commented.

    “Hetero life does sound dull,” Lena jested. “Anyways, how was your interview, darling?”

    “Great actually, Amadei’s really great, even if a bit prickly.” Kara smiled. “Hey, listen, I got you these, and I was wondering if we could talk for a moment?”

    “Oh… Sure. What’s up?”

    “This morning, I–I told you something when I was leaving,” she started. Lena visibly deflated.

    “Do you regret it?”

    “Not like that,” Kara got close to Lena, pulling her towards herself by the waist. “But because, I’m surprised it took me this long to actually say it to you,” she whispered. “Because if i’m being honest, I think I’ve loved you pretty much since our first week together. And now that I've said it, I want to keep saying it. I love you, Lena Luthor, I hope you know that.”

    Lena kissed her, and Kara could very possibly melt on the spot. “I do know it. I didn’t really need you to say it, but it’s very nice to hear it.”

    Kara laughed, her heart full and she would be happy to keep just existing in that moment.

    “But just so we are clear,” Lena continued talking. “I love you too. With everything I have. I can’t actually believe that its taken us this long to say the words, either.”

    “Maybe we said it in other ways,” Kara shrugged.

    “Perhaps,” Lena whispered. “Thank you for the flowers too.”

    “You are very welcome. Are you hungry? Because I'm starving.”

    “Yes, darling. Let’s eat.”

     

    Alex and Kelly had been married for a little over three months when Alex got the call. And she was a mess. But the social worker liked them, and they’d been approved to foster a 5-year-old with the option to adopt her if they were a good fit. Alex remained a mess, only capable of being calmed by Kelly. 

    Lena and Kara were supposed to meet Esme the same day she arrived at Kelly’s and Alex’s home for dinner. Just to give them time to get to know each other in their new home. 

    “Do you think she’s scared? She met them before, unlike me when I was dragged to the Luthor’s,” Lena said while they were walking around a toy store. 

    “Hopefully she got used to them, but I think she must be nervous, it’s still new people, and a new house.” Kara shrugged looking at a coloring book.

    “Were you scared when you met the Danvers?”

    Kara sighed, “I suppose. I was so sad and angry back then. At my parents for moving us across the world, for dying. And then at Clark for not taking me in. But Eliza was always warm. She made me feel safe almost instantly.”

    “Let’s hope she feels the same,” Lena commented while picking a teddy bear.

     

    Esme was adorable, even if a little withdrawn, but that was expected given the situation. Alex was still nervous as hell, if her constant babbling to the 5-year-old was anything to go by.

    “Babe, breathe.” Kelly whispered after her and Kara shared a look of concern.

    “Yes, of course,” Alex laughed, after taking a deep breath. “Okay, Esme, I want you to meet some very important people in our lives.” Her sister kneeled next to the kid, and pointed to Kara. “This is my sister Kara, besides Kelly and now you, the most important person in my life.”

    “I love you too, sis,” Kara smiled, plopping on the floor in front of Esme and Alex. “Hi Esme. It’s very nice to meet you.”

    “You too,” the kid replied, hugging her zebra plush toy so hard that her knuckles were white. 

    “And this is Lena, she’s Kara’s girlfriend,” Alex introduced the brunette.

    “Hello, Esme,” Lena leaned in, trying to be at the same height as the child. “I’m very happy to finally meet you.”

    “You are really pretty,” Esme mumbled, looking at Lena with a smile. 

    Lena laughed, her nose wrinkling a little. “So are you, Esme.”

    “I couldn’t agree more, buddy,” Kara told Esme, as if it were a secret. She smiled and got even closer to Esme, trying her best not to scare her. “You know, Lena and I were also adopted. We know the change can be a bit scary, and we know that Alex and Kelly will do their very best to make it as easy as possible. But if you ever need anything, you could call either of us, okay?”

    “Anytime,” Lena added.

    Esme looked at the both of them with her big brown eyes, and then nodded, “Okay.”

    “Great! Now, we got you some stuff, but I can see you have a cool buddy over there, what’s its name?” Kara asked.

    “Her name is Lovey,” the child smiled, all innocent and happy. “Mama Alex fixed her eye.” And Kara knew she would be okay. They would all be okay.

    “Very nice to meet you too, Lovey.”



    There was another visit to Lillian Luthor, and Lena seemed a bit nervous about it. Kara didn’t push, she just waited for her to be ready to tell her whatever it was whenever she was ready. But the visit was as ordinary as all the previous ones. Lillian and Lena played chess, Lillian made a comment about Kara’s inadequacy as a partner for Lena, and they both ignored her. Lena made a comment about Lillian’s abysmal parenting. Nothing out of the ordinary. And when they went to eat at a hole in the wall restaurant, Kara visited while doing an interview, Lena remained nervous, so Kara pushed a little.

    “It’s everything okay?”

    “Yes, just… there is something in my mind,” Lena bounced her leg in place, which was odd, to say the least.

    “Anything I can help you with?” Kara asked, Lena shook her head no. “Okay, but I’m sure it will work out. You always figure it out.”

    Lena looked at her and smiled, soft and beautiful. “You always know how to calm me.”

    “We work well together, I calm you, and you calm me,” Kara grinned.

    Lena looked at her like she was looking for some divine answer on her face. If she found it, Kara had no idea what could have been, but Lena took a deep breath, decided.

    “I was going to wait for us to be back in your apartment, just to be sarcastic about that damn kitchen cabinet that you know drives me crazy because it never closes properly,” Lena confessed. “And how your upstairs neighbor has very loud, very bad music playing all day and how it gives us headaches. And I was going to complain again about how stupid it was that you wouldn’t just let me call your landlord and tell him to go to hell and fix the stupid elevator once and for all, so it works for more than two days a week.”

    “I’m sensing that maybe you don’t really like my apartment,” Kara quipped.

    Lena laughed, “I like your apartment okay, but I think I would like it better if you moved in with me,” she mumbled, the last words sounding a bit like a question.

    “I don’t see how you would like my apartment better if I moved in with you…” 

    “Kara…”

    “I would love to move in with you,” Kara whispered, with her hand on Lena’s jaw. “But can it be someplace other than your penthouse? I– I don’t—”

    “I know,” Lena interrupted, with a goofy smile, and getting close to her lips. “But I really wish you didn’t mind the money as much.”

    “It just feels weird,” Kara kissed Lena’s lips, soft and pillowy. “But also, that penthouse never felt like yours anyway.”

    “I couldn’t agree more.”

     

    Apparently Lena had been planning to ask her to move in together for a while. The very next day they met with a realtor, who was showing them everything from 2-bedroom apartments, to lofts, to 3-floors houses. All within Kara’s budget. Which seemed insane in Kara’s mind, because she couldn’t possibly afford a 3-floor house. 

    “That one is part of LCorp’s assets,” Lena explained. “I think my father bought it before I was born.”

    Kara said nothing, it was a weird thing, but she didn’t want to pry. Lena gave her an answer anyways.

    “I think he bought it for my mother. My biological mother, I mean. I don’t know why I even asked Victor to show it to us. I don’t think I would like to live here…” She wistfully admitted.

    “That’s okay, you were just curious,” Kara commented, hugging Lena from her waist, and hooking her chin on her shoulder and looking around. “If you wouldn’t like living in a place like this, where do you think you would like to live?”

    Lena took a moment, before answering. “I’d like something like your apartment, but bigger. That place also has really bad plumbing. I can’t believe I let you move there.”

    Kara laughed, “So, my apartment, but more expensive.”

    “Sure,” Lena smiled.

    “Hey, Victor!” Kara called out.

     

    They did find a more upscale version of her old apartment. With better plumbing and a functioning elevator. Still out of Kara’s budget, but Lena was a billionaire, and she put her foot down; telling Kara she didn’t need to feel insecure about her money, because she was a billionaire when they met and there was really nothing they could do to change that fact. Kara eased up a bit after that; Lena was right.

     

    Andrea, Lena and sometimes Sam had weekly meetings, for brunch or dinner. Kara finally met Jack when he came to National City for a tech conference, (something Sam had lovingly dubbed the nerd convention for all Lena’s exes. Kara was not so thrilled about that nickname.) He was very charming, and a constant flirt, and for some reason, Kara had the urge to introduce him to Winn. 

    Lois and Clark also came to the conference, Lois was apparently gonna grill some poor soul about some microchip that had been exploding inside computers that said soul had donated to low income schools. And Clark, said he was keeping his promise, and showed up with an entire itinerary that Kara had no intention of following.

    Lena and Kara hosted game nights every other week, with Nia, Brainy, Sam, Ruby, Winn, and sometimes Cisco. Rotating hosting duties between theirs and Alex and Kelly’s house. It was on one of those nights that Esme called Lena aunt for the first time, even before she even called Kara as such.

    Lena was a mess when they got home.

    Eliza visited more frequently too, refusing to lose time with her granddaughter. As the official adoption came in surprisingly fast and easy.

    Kara didn’t know if she could be happier. 

     

     

    “Remember that you told me you wanted to be an artist when you were younger?” Lena asked out of nowhere, an unremarkable Tuesday night, while Kara was reading a draft in front of the TV, and Lena was looking at her phone. 

    “Of course,” Kara distractedly replied.

    “I have a friend—”

    “Of course you do,” Kara interrupted her, snorting a laugh. 

    “And he has connections in the Barcelona Academy of Arts,” Lena continued as if nothing. “I showed him some of your sketches—”

    Kara stopped reading, and looked at her girlfriend. “You did not! Lena, those are personal!”

    “Not those.” Lena assured her. “But I showed him some of your sketches, and he talked to his friend. Apparently they have an opening for a summer three month course, if you want it.”

    Kara was a bit speechless, “Are you serious?”

    “I thought you could talk with Snapper and pitch something related to Barcelona, and we could go.” Lena kept rambling. “Or you could take a non-paid leave, you know money is no problem. I just thought it was something cool, and you are so talented—”

    Kara wanted to marry her. The thought was a bit surprising, just because that conclusion came as easy as breathing. Kara wanted to marry Lena.

    “I love you, you know?”

    “I like it when you say it,” Lena smiled at her.

    And Kara decided; she was going to marry her. (If Lena wanted to).
